# Kira — Bahasa Malaysia review

**Every BM string in the app that Claude wrote, not Julius.**

Generated by `node scripts/bm-review.mjs` from `seed_content.json`. Scope: **236 items** across 15 topics — Stage 6, Stage 7, and the two balancing-off lessons. Stages 1–5 were ported from Julius's own material and are deliberately excluded.

## How to use this

Work top to bottom — the sections are ordered by how much a correction buys you.

1. **Inconsistencies** are already bugs: the same English term is rendered two ways in BM, or one BM string covers two different English terms. Pick the right one and the other is a fix, whichever way it goes.
2. **Terminology** is the highest-leverage read. A term used 14 times is checked once here and fixed in 14 places.
3. **Full text** is the careful pass over prompts and explanations.

Write corrections on the `→` line under anything wrong. Leave the rest untouched. Hand the file back and the edits get applied to `seed_content.json`, which is the only real source — this document is generated and is never itself the content.

---

## 0. Against the SPM 2024 papers

Transcribed from 3756/1 and 3756/2. This is the one section that needs no Malay to act on: where our word differs from the exam board's, the board wins.

### 3 clashes — we use a different word from the paper

- **Payables** — paper says `Akaun Belum Bayar`, we say `Pemiutang` (5×)
  - paper: K1 Q4, Q10, Q25; K2 Q2, Q4 · we also use the paper's term 19×
  - → 
- **Carrying amount / book value** — paper says `Nilai buku`, we say `Amaun bawaan` (6×)
  - paper: K1 Q17; K2 Q5(ii) · we also use the paper's term 22×
  - → 
- **Appropriation of profit** — paper says `Pengasingan Untung Rugi`, we say `Pengagihan Untung` (4×)
  - paper: K1 Q31
  - → 

### Not found in our content either way

Neither the paper's term nor a known alternative appears. Usually just a concept we word differently in prose — worth a glance, not an alarm.

- Manufacturing account — paper says `Akaun Pengeluaran` (K1 Q38; K2 Q3(a))
- Trading and profit and loss account — paper says `Akaun Perdagangan dan Untung Rugi` (K1 Q20; K2 Q2(a), Q3(b))
- Receipts and payments account — paper says `Akaun Penerimaan dan Pembayaran` (K1 Q35)
- Cash budget — paper says `Penyata Belanjawan Tunai` (K1 Q27, Q28)
- Provision for doubtful debts — paper says `Peruntukan hutang ragu` (K2 Q2, Q4, Q5)
- Working capital — paper says `Modal kerja` (K1 Q30; K2 Q2(b))
- Petty cash float — paper says `Panjar runcit` (K1 Q8)
- Fixed deposit — paper says `Simpanan tetap` (K2 Q2, Q4)
- Cost price / market price — paper says `Harga kos / Harga pasaran` (K1 Q11; K2 Q2(i), Q6)

## 1. Inconsistencies found mechanically

### One English term, several BM renderings

At most one of each group is right.

- **Receipt**
  - `Resit` — 5× (sd-001, sd-003, sd-004, sd-005, sd-006)
  - `Terimaan` — 6× (cb-001, cb-002, cb-003, cb-004, cb-005, cb-006)
  - → 

- **Fading the Appropriation**
  - `Melunturkan Pengagihan` — 1× (l29-faded-appropriation)
  - `Melunturkan Pengagihan Untung` — 1× (l34-faded-company)
  - → 

### Not a BM problem — but the ENGLISH disagrees with itself

Malay marks neither plural nor articles, so one BM string covering both of these is correct. What it exposes is the English side: the same concept is offered under two different canonical labels, in an app that can also be read in English.

- `Akaun Belum Terima` ← **Trade Receivables** / **Trade receivables** (ta-006, ta-008, ir-201)
- `Tunai diterima daripada pelanggan` ← **Cash received from customer** / **Cash received from customers** (ta-006, ta-008, ca-201)
- `Akaun Belum Bayar` ← **Trade Payables** / **Trade payables** (ta-007, ta-010, ir-201)
- `Aset` ← **Assets** / **An asset** (ir-201, cl-201, cl-202)
- `Liabiliti` ← **Liabilities** / **A liability** (ir-201, cl-201, cl-202)
- `Bahan langsung` ← **Direct material** / **Direct materials** (mf-001, mf-002, mf-003, mf-004, mf-005, mf-006, be-002)
- →

- `Kelab tidak berniaga untuk untung, jadi penyatanya dinamakan semula — tetapi perakaunannya sama seperti yang anda sudah tahu. Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan ialah penyata pendapatan kelab: hanya item hasil bagi tahun INI, dengan terakru dan prabayar dikenakan sama seperti Tahap 5. - `Sesebuah syarikat menjemput orang ramai memohon syer. Jika permohonan melebihi syer yang ditawarkan, terbitan itu TERLEBIH LANGGANAN, dan lebihan wang itu bukan milik syarikat — ia dikembalikan kepada pemohon, atau ditahan dan ditolak daripada jumlah seterusnya yang mereka perlu bayar. Terkurang langganan ialah masalah sebaliknya: permohonan kurang daripada syer yang ditawarkan, dan penaja jamin yang menjamin terbitan itu mengambil kekurangannya. Perhatikan juga kedudukan masing-masing: pemegang syer keutamaan mendapat dividen pada kadar tetap SEBELUM pemegang syer biasa mendapat apa-apa, dan sebagai pertukaran mereka biasanya tiada hak mengundi.` ← **A company invites the public to apply for shares. If applications come in for more shares than were offered the issue is OVERSUBSCRIBED, and the excess money is not the company’s to keep — it is refunded to the applicants, or held and set against the next amount they owe. Undersubscribed is the opposite problem: fewer applications than shares offered, and the underwriter who guaranteed the issue takes up the shortfall. Note also who ranks where: preference shareholders get their dividend at a fixed rate BEFORE the ordinary shareholders get anything, and in exchange they usually have no vote.** · 1×

- `Syarikat dimiliki oleh pemegang syernya dan sebahagian pembiayaannya datang daripada pemberi pinjaman. Modal syer dan setiap rizab adalah milik pemilik, jadi ia ekuiti. Debentur pula ialah pinjaman: pemegangnya pemberi pinjaman, bukan pemilik, dan mesti dibayar faedah sama ada syarikat untung atau tidak.` ← **A company is owned by its shareholders and financed partly by lenders. - `Akaun kawalan memampatkan keseluruhan lejar ke dalam satu akaun T, supaya jumlahnya boleh disemak dengan akaun individu. Returns get their own two books: returns inwards (from customers) and returns outwards (to suppliers). Cash and bank movements go to the cash book. Everything left over — buying a van on credit, correcting an error, opening entries, the owner taking goods — goes to the GENERAL JOURNAL. - `Imbangan duga yang seimbang bukan bukti buku itu betul. Enam kesilapan mengekalkan kedua-dua belah sama dan lolos terus: PENINGGALAN (tertinggal sepenuhnya), KOMISEN (jenis akaun betul, orang salah), PRINSIP (kelas akaun salah — aset dianggap belanja), CATATAN ASAL (angka salah yang sama digunakan di kedua-dua belah), TERBALIK SEPENUHNYA (debit dan kredit bertukar), dan SALING MENGIMBANGI (dua kesilapan sama besar yang membatalkan). Hanya kesilapan sebelah — catatan yang tertinggal, atau lajur yang salah campur — membuat imbangan duga tidak seimbang, dan itulah gunanya akaun penyelesaian.` ← **A trial balance that agrees is not proof the books are right. Six errors keep both sides equal and slip straight through: OMISSION (left out entirely), COMMISSION (right kind of account, wrong person), PRINCIPLE (wrong class of account — an asset treated as an expense), ORIGINAL ENTRY (the same wrong figure used on both sides), COMPLETE REVERSAL (debit and credit swapped), and COMPENSATING (two errors of the same size cancelling out). Only a one-sided error — a missing posting, or a column added up wrongly — makes the trial balance disagree, and that is what a suspense account is for.** · 1×

- `Perniagaan wujud dalam pelbagai bentuk, dan bentuk itu menentukan dua perkara terutamanya: siapa pemiliknya dan sejauh mana pemilik bertanggungjawab secara peribadi. PEMILIK TUNGGAL ialah seorang pemilik dengan liabiliti tidak terhad — pemiutang boleh menuntut aset peribadinya. PERKONGSIAN ialah 2 hingga 20 pekongsi, juga dengan liabiliti tidak terhad. SYARIKAT dimiliki oleh pemegang syernya dan liabilitinya TERHAD kepada jumlah yang mereka setuju bayar bagi syer mereka; syarikat ialah entiti undang-undang yang berasingan. KOPERASI dimiliki oleh anggotanya, yang masing-masing memegang syer dan mendapat satu undi seorang walau berapa banyak syer dipegang, dan liabiliti mereka juga terhad. Berasingan daripada itu, peraturannya ditetapkan oleh badan tertentu: di Malaysia, MASB mengeluarkan piawaian perakaunan, manakala MIA mengawal selia akauntan yang menggunakannya.` ← **Businesses come in forms, and the form decides two things above all: who owns it and how far the owner is personally on the hook. A SOLE TRADER is one owner with unlimited liability — creditors can reach their private assets. A PARTNERSHIP is 2 to 20 partners, also with unlimited liability. A COMPANY is owned by its shareholders and its liability is LIMITED to what they agreed to pay for their shares; the company is a separate legal person. A COOPERATIVE is owned by its members, who each hold shares and get one vote each however many shares they hold, and their liability is limited too. Separately, the rules themselves are set by bodies: in Malaysia the MASB issues the accounting standards, while the MIA regulates the accountants who apply them.** · 1×

  - →

- `Menutup akaun ada dua bahagian yang berada di sebelah BERTENTANGAN. Baki c/d dicatat di sebelah yang lebih kecil, kerana tugasnya menyamakan kedua-dua jumlah. Baki b/d kemudian dibawa turun di sebelah yang lebih besar — dan itulah sebelah baki sebenar akaun tersebut. Akaun Belum Terima biasanya berakhir dengan baki debit dan Akaun Belum Bayar dengan baki kredit, tetapi catatan yang menutup setiap satunya ditulis di sebelah yang satu lagi.` ← **Closing an account has two halves that sit on OPPOSITE sides. The balance c/d is written on the smaller side, because its job is to make the two totals equal. The balance b/d then comes down on the larger side — and that is the side the account really has its balance on. Setiap anak tangga di bawah memberi anda kurang daripadanya.` ← **Closing any account runs the same four moves: total each side, put the difference on the smaller side as the balance c/d, then bring it down on the larger side as the balance b/d. - `Inventori akhir dinilai pada nilai yang LEBIH RENDAH antara kosnya dan harga ia boleh dijual. Bandingkan kedua-dua angka baris demi baris — bukan pada jumlahnya — dan ambil yang lebih rendah setiap kali. Sebabnya ialah berhemat: sesuatu aset tidak boleh dibawa pada nilai melebihi harga sebenar yang boleh diperoleh, dan kerana inventori akhir ditolak dalam akaun perdagangan, menyatakan lebih akan menyatakan kurang kos jualan dan menyatakan lebih untung. Itulah sebab peraturan ini wujud, dan sebab ia dikenakan setiap produk dan bukan pada jumlahnya, di mana kenaikan pada satu baris boleh menyembunyikan penurunan pada baris lain.` ← **Closing inventory is valued at the LOWER of what it cost and what it can be sold for. Compare the two figures line by line — not on the totals — and take the lower one each time. The reason is prudence: an asset must never be carried at more than it can actually fetch, and since closing inventory is deducted in the trading account, overstating it understates cost of sales and overstates profit. That is why the rule exists, and why it is applied per product rather than to the total, where a gain on one line could hide a fall on another.** · 1×

- `Setiap catatan bermula daripada satu dokumen, dan dokumen itu memberitahu apa yang berlaku. INVOIS dikeluarkan bagi jualan atau belian secara kredit. NOTA KREDIT mengurangkan hutang pelanggan — barang dipulangkan, atau mereka terlebih caj. NOTA DEBIT menambahkannya, apabila invois terkurang caj. RESIT membuktikan wang diterima, BIL TUNAI membuktikan jualan tunai, KERATAN CEK membuktikan bayaran dikeluarkan. MEMO ialah sumber bagi perkara dalaman, seperti pemilik mengambil tunai. Tetapi NOTA SERAHAN, PENYATA AKAUN, pesanan belian dan sebut harga tidak merekod sebarang jumlah terhutang atau dibayar pada masa itu — ia bukan dokumen sumber dan tiada apa dicatat daripadanya.` ← **Every entry starts from a document, and the document tells you what happened. An INVOICE is issued for a credit sale or purchase. A CREDIT NOTE reduces what a customer owes — goods came back, or they were overcharged. A DEBIT NOTE increases it, when the invoice undercharged them. A RECEIPT proves money came in, a CASH BILL proves a cash sale, a CHEQUE COUNTERFOIL proves a payment went out. A MEMO is the source for something internal, like the owner taking cash. But a DELIVERY NOTE, a STATEMENT OF ACCOUNT, a purchase order and a quotation record no amount owed or paid at that moment — they are not source documents and nothing is entered from them.** · 1×

- `Baki berkurangan mengenakan kadar ke atas nilai aset yang masih tinggal dalam buku — nilai buku, juga dipanggil amaun bawaan — bukan ke atas kosnya. Tahun 2 dan seterusnya: kadar × (kos − susut nilai terkumpul setakat ini). Perangkapnya ialah mengenakan kadar ke atas kos setiap tahun: itu jawapan garis lurus dalam soalan baki berkurangan.` ← **Reducing balance charges the rate on what the asset is still worth in the books — the book value, also called the carrying amount — not on its cost. So the first year's charge is the largest and every year after it is smaller. Year 1: rate × cost (there is no accumulated depreciation yet). Year 2 onwards: rate × (cost − accumulated depreciation to date). The trap is applying the rate to cost every year: that is the straight-line answer wearing a reducing-balance question.** · 1×

- `Bayaran kecil — pos, teh tarik untuk mesyuarat, teksi — tidak berbaloi dikeluarkan cek satu per satu, jadi juruwang runcit memegang panjar. Di bawah sistem PANJAR, jumlah panjar itu ialah amaun tetap. Pada akhir tempoh, juruwang dibayar balik tepat seperti yang dibelanjakan, yang memulihkan panjar kepada angka tetap yang sama seperti pada permulaan. Jadi: bayaran balik = jumlah pembayaran, dan panjar = baki yang masih ada + bayaran balik diterima. Yang kedua itu ialah cara peperiksaan biasanya bertanya — anda diberi bakinya dan tambahannya, dan panjar ialah jumlah kedua-duanya.` ← **Small payments — postage, teh tarik for a meeting, a taxi — are not worth a cheque each, so a petty cashier holds a float. Under the IMPREST system that float is a fixed amount. At the end of the period the cashier is reimbursed with exactly what was spent, which restores the float to the same fixed figure it started at. So: reimbursement = total payments, and imprest = balance still in hand + reimbursement received. That second one is the way an exam usually asks it — you are given the leftover and the top-up, and the float is their sum.** · 1×
  - l49-petty-cash
  - → 
- `Sesetengah aset terlalu banyak dan terlalu kecil untuk disusutnilaikan satu per satu — alat-alat kecil di kilang, pinggan mangkuk di restoran, peti pembungkus. Bagi aset itu, cajnya dikira secara PENILAIAN SEMULA: nilaikan apa yang ada pada permulaan, tambah apa-apa yang dibeli sepanjang tahun, kemudian nilaikan apa yang ada pada akhirnya. Apa yang telah hilang itulah susut nilainya. Nilai awal + belian − nilai akhir = caj bagi tahun tersebut. Tiada kadar dan tiada hayat berguna; penilaian pada akhir tahun yang melakukan kerjanya.` ← **Some assets are too many and too small to depreciate one by one — loose tools in a factory, crockery in a restaurant, packing cases. For those the charge is worked out by REVALUATION: value what is on hand at the start, add anything bought during the year, then value what is on hand at the end. Whatever has gone is the depreciation. Opening value + purchases − closing value = the charge for the year. There is no rate and no useful life; the valuation at the end does the work.** · 1×

  - → 
- `Sesetengah kos berubah mengikut berapa banyak anda keluarkan — bahan, upah ikut unit — dan sesetengahnya tidak: sewa tetap sama sama ada anda mengeluarkan satu unit atau seribu. Setiap unit yang dijual memperoleh harga jualannya tolak kos BERUBAHnya, dan beza itu ialah sumbangan. Kos tetap mesti ditampung sebelum ada sebarang untung, jadi titik pulang modal ialah kos tetap ÷ sumbangan seunit: bilangan unit apabila jumlah hasil sama tepat dengan jumlah kos dan untung ialah sifar. Di bawahnya perniagaan rugi; di atasnya setiap unit tambahan menyumbang keseluruhan sumbangannya kepada untung.` ← **Some costs change with how much you make — materials, piece-rate wages — and some do not: rent is the same whether you produce one unit or a thousand. Every unit sold earns its selling price less its VARIABLE cost, and that difference is the contribution. Fixed costs have to be covered before there is any profit at all, so the break-even point is simply fixed costs ÷ contribution per unit: the number of units at which total revenue exactly equals total cost and profit is zero. Below it the business loses money; above it every further unit adds its whole contribution to profit.** · 1×

- `Apabila perkongsian dibubarkan, aset dijual, pemiutang dibayar, dan bakinya diberikan kepada pekongsi. AKAUN REALISASI melakukan pengiraannya. Bakinya ialah untung atau rugi atas realisasi, dan ia dikongsi antara pekongsi mengikut nisbah perkongsian untung — itulah maksudnya: tiada siapa tahu harga sesuatu aset sehingga ia dijual, jadi lebihan atau kekurangan itu milik pekongsi, bukan milik perniagaan.` ← **When a partnership is dissolved, the assets are sold, the creditors are paid, and whatever is left goes to the partners. ## 3. Full text, by topic

### The Accounting Equation

`t1-equation` · BM title: `Persamaan Perakaunan`
- → 

#### Who Owns It, Who Sets the Rules

`l56-entities-and-bodies` · BM title: `Siapa Pemiliknya, Siapa Menetapkan Peraturan`

> **Worked example (EN)** Businesses come in forms, and the form decides two things above all: who owns it and how far the owner is personally on the hook. A SOLE TRADER is one owner with unlimited liability — creditors can reach their private assets. A PARTNERSHIP is 2 to 20 partners, also with unlimited liability. A COMPANY is owned by its shareholders and its liability is LIMITED to what they agreed to pay for their shares; the company is a separate legal person. A COOPERATIVE is owned by its members, who each hold shares and get one vote each however many shares they hold, and their liability is limited too. Separately, the rules themselves are set by bodies: in Malaysia the MASB issues the accounting standards, while the MIA regulates the accountants who apply them.
> **(BM)** Perniagaan wujud dalam pelbagai bentuk, dan bentuk itu menentukan dua perkara terutamanya: siapa pemiliknya dan sejauh mana pemilik bertanggungjawab secara peribadi. PEMILIK TUNGGAL ialah seorang pemilik dengan liabiliti tidak terhad — pemiutang boleh menuntut aset peribadinya. PERKONGSIAN ialah 2 hingga 20 pekongsi, juga dengan liabiliti tidak terhad. SYARIKAT dimiliki oleh pemegang syernya dan liabilitinya TERHAD kepada jumlah yang mereka setuju bayar bagi syer mereka; syarikat ialah entiti undang-undang yang berasingan. KOPERASI dimiliki oleh anggotanya, yang masing-masing memegang syer dan mendapat satu undi seorang walau berapa banyak syer dipegang, dan liabiliti mereka juga terhad. Berasingan daripada itu, peraturannya ditetapkan oleh badan tertentu: di Malaysia, MASB mengeluarkan piawaian perakaunan, manakala MIA mengawal selia akauntan yang menggunakannya.
- → 

**`en-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · classification

- *Question (EN)* Who owns a cooperative?
- *Question (BM)* **Siapakah pemilik sesebuah koperasi?**
- *Choices* Its members → **Anggotanya** · One owner → **Seorang pemilik** · The government → **Kerajaan** · Outside shareholders → **Pemegang syer luar**
- *Why (EN)* The people who use it own it — members buy shares in it and it is run for their benefit.
- *Why (BM)* **Orang yang menggunakannya memilikinya — anggota membeli syer di dalamnya dan ia dijalankan untuk faedah mereka.**
- → 

**`en-002`** · classify · difficulty 3 · classification

- *Question (EN)* What is the liability of a cooperative’s members?
- *Question (BM)* **Apakah liabiliti anggota sesebuah koperasi?**
- *Choices* Limited → **Terhad** · Unlimited → **Tidak terhad**
- *Why (EN)* Limited to what they put in, as with a company — their private assets are not at risk.
- *Why (BM)* **Terhad kepada apa yang mereka laburkan, seperti syarikat — aset peribadi mereka tidak terancam.**
- → 

**`en-003`** · classify · difficulty 3 · classification

- *Question (EN)* In a cooperative, how many votes does a member with many shares have?
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam sesebuah koperasi, berapakah undi seorang anggota yang memegang banyak syer?**
- *Choices* One → **Satu** · One per share → **Satu bagi setiap syer** · One per RM100 of shares → **Satu bagi setiap RM100 syer**
- *Why (EN)* One member, one vote — which is the clearest difference from a company, where votes follow the shares.
- *Why (BM)* **Seorang anggota, satu undi — itulah perbezaan paling jelas daripada syarikat, di mana undi mengikut syer.**
- → 

**`en-004`** · classify · difficulty 3 · classification

- *Question (EN)* Whose private assets can be reached by creditors if the business cannot pay?
- *Question (BM)* **Aset peribadi siapakah yang boleh dituntut oleh pemiutang jika perniagaan tidak mampu membayar?**
- *Choices* A sole trader’s → **Pemilik tunggal** · A shareholder’s → **Pemegang syer** · A cooperative member’s → **Anggota koperasi**
- *Why (EN)* Unlimited liability means the owner and the business are not separated in law.
- *Why (BM)* **Liabiliti tidak terhad bermakna pemilik dan perniagaan tidak dipisahkan di sisi undang-undang.**
- → 

**`en-005`** · classify · difficulty 3 · classification

- *Question (EN)* Which body issues accounting standards in Malaysia?
- *Question (BM)* **Badan manakah yang mengeluarkan piawaian perakaunan di Malaysia?**
- *Choices* MASB → **MASB** · MIA → **MIA** · CIMA → **CIMA** · CPA Australia → **CPA Australia**
- *Why (EN)* The Malaysian Accounting Standards Board writes the standards; the MIA regulates the accountants who apply them.
- *Why (BM)* **Malaysian Accounting Standards Board menulis piawaiannya; MIA mengawal selia akauntan yang menggunakannya.**
- → 

**`en-006`** · classify · difficulty 3 · classification

- *Question (EN)* Which body regulates accountants practising in Malaysia?
- *Question (BM)* **Badan manakah yang mengawal selia akauntan yang beramal di Malaysia?**
- *Choices* MIA → **MIA** · MASB → **MASB** · CIMA → **CIMA** · ACCA → **ACCA**
- *Why (EN)* The Malaysian Institute of Accountants registers and regulates them. No one may call themselves an accountant in Malaysia without it.
- *Why (BM)* **Malaysian Institute of Accountants mendaftar dan mengawal selia mereka. Tiada siapa boleh menggelar diri akauntan di Malaysia tanpanya.**
- → 

### Documents & First Entry

`t22-documents` · BM title: `Dokumen & Catatan Pertama`
- → 

#### Which Document?

`l47-source-documents` · BM title: `Dokumen Yang Mana?`

> **Worked example (EN)** Every entry starts from a document, and the document tells you what happened. An INVOICE is issued for a credit sale or purchase. A CREDIT NOTE reduces what a customer owes — goods came back, or they were overcharged. A DEBIT NOTE increases it, when the invoice undercharged them. A RECEIPT proves money came in, a CASH BILL proves a cash sale, a CHEQUE COUNTERFOIL proves a payment went out. A MEMO is the source for something internal, like the owner taking cash. But a DELIVERY NOTE, a STATEMENT OF ACCOUNT, a purchase order and a quotation record no amount owed or paid at that moment — they are not source documents and nothing is entered from them.
> **(BM)** Setiap catatan bermula daripada satu dokumen, dan dokumen itu memberitahu apa yang berlaku. INVOIS dikeluarkan bagi jualan atau belian secara kredit. NOTA KREDIT mengurangkan hutang pelanggan — barang dipulangkan, atau mereka terlebih caj. NOTA DEBIT menambahkannya, apabila invois terkurang caj. RESIT membuktikan wang diterima, BIL TUNAI membuktikan jualan tunai, KERATAN CEK membuktikan bayaran dikeluarkan. MEMO ialah sumber bagi perkara dalaman, seperti pemilik mengambil tunai. Tetapi NOTA SERAHAN, PENYATA AKAUN, pesanan belian dan sebut harga tidak merekod sebarang jumlah terhutang atau dibayar pada masa itu — ia bukan dokumen sumber dan tiada apa dicatat daripadanya.
- → 

**`sd-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · source-documents

- *Question (EN)* A business sells goods on credit. Which document does it issue to the customer?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah perniagaan menjual barang secara kredit. Dokumen manakah yang dikeluarkan kepada pelanggan?**
- *Choices* Invoice → **Invois** · Receipt → **Resit** · Credit note → **Nota kredit** · Delivery note → **Nota serahan**
- *Why (EN)* The invoice states what is owed and is the source for the credit sale entry.
- *Why (BM)* **Invois menyatakan jumlah yang terhutang dan menjadi sumber bagi catatan jualan kredit.**
- → 

**`sd-002`** · classify · difficulty 2 · source-documents

- *Question (EN)* A customer returns goods. Which document does the seller send?
- *Question (BM)* **Seorang pelanggan memulangkan barang. Dokumen manakah yang dihantar oleh penjual?**
- *Choices* Credit note → **Nota kredit** · Debit note → **Nota debit** · Invoice → **Invois** · Statement of account → **Penyata akaun**
- *Why (EN)* The customer owes less than the invoice said, so the seller credits their account.
- *Why (BM)* **Pelanggan berhutang kurang daripada yang dinyatakan dalam invois, jadi penjual mengkreditkan akaun mereka.**
- → 

**`sd-003`** · classify · difficulty 3 · source-documents

- *Question (EN)* An invoice undercharged the customer by RM50. Which document corrects it?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu invois terkurang caj kepada pelanggan sebanyak RM50. Dokumen manakah yang membetulkannya?**
- *Choices* Debit note → **Nota debit** · Credit note → **Nota kredit** · Memo → **Memo** · Receipt → **Resit**
- *Why (EN)* A debit note ADDS to the invoice. A credit note would take away, which is the wrong direction here.
- *Why (BM)* **Nota debit MENAMBAH kepada invois. Nota kredit akan menolak, iaitu arah yang salah di sini.**
- → 

**`sd-004`** · classify · difficulty 3 · source-documents

- *Question (EN)* Which of these is NOT a source document?
- *Question (BM)* **Antara berikut, yang manakah BUKAN dokumen sumber?**
- *Choices* Delivery note → **Nota serahan** · Invoice → **Invois** · Receipt → **Resit** · Cash bill → **Bil tunai**
- *Why (EN)* A delivery note proves goods arrived, not that anything is owed or paid — no entry is made from it.
- *Why (BM)* **Nota serahan membuktikan barang telah sampai, bukan bahawa ada hutang atau bayaran — tiada catatan dibuat daripadanya.**
- → 

**`sd-005`** · classify · difficulty 3 · source-documents

- *Question (EN)* The owner takes RM150 of business cash for private use. What is the source document?
- *Question (BM)* **Pemilik mengambil tunai perniagaan RM150 untuk kegunaan peribadi. Apakah dokumen sumbernya?**
- *Choices* Memo → **Memo** · Invoice → **Invois** · Receipt → **Resit** · Cheque counterfoil → **Keratan cek**
- *Why (EN)* Nobody outside the business is involved, so there is no invoice or receipt. An internal memo is the source for drawings.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada pihak luar terlibat, jadi tiada invois atau resit. Memo dalaman ialah sumber bagi ambilan.**
- → 

**`sd-006`** · classify · difficulty 2 · source-documents

- *Question (EN)* Which document proves that money was received?
- *Question (BM)* **Dokumen manakah yang membuktikan wang telah diterima?**
- *Choices* Receipt → **Resit** · Invoice → **Invois** · Purchase order → **Pesanan belian** · Delivery note → **Nota serahan**
- *Why (EN)* An invoice says money is owed; a receipt says it has actually been paid.
- *Why (BM)* **Invois menyatakan wang terhutang; resit menyatakan ia telah benar-benar dibayar.**
- → 

**`sd-007`** · classify · difficulty 4 · source-documents

- *Question (EN)* A supplier sends a monthly summary of all invoices and payments. Is an entry made from it?
- *Question (BM)* **Seorang pembekal menghantar ringkasan bulanan semua invois dan bayaran. Adakah catatan dibuat daripadanya?**
- *Choices* No — it is not a source document → **Tidak — ia bukan dokumen sumber** · Yes — it is a source document → **Ya — ia dokumen sumber**
- *Why (EN)* A statement of account only repeats transactions already entered from their own invoices. Entering it again would double-count them.
- *Why (BM)* **Penyata akaun hanya mengulangi urus niaga yang telah dicatat daripada invois masing-masing. Mencatatnya semula akan mengira dua kali.**
- → 

#### Into Which Book?

`l48-books-of-first-entry` · BM title: `Ke Dalam Buku Yang Mana?`

> **Worked example (EN)** A document is written up in a book of first entry before it ever reaches the ledger. Which book depends on two things: what was traded, and whether it was on credit. The SALES and PURCHASES journals take credit dealings in GOODS THE BUSINESS TRADES IN — nothing else. Returns get their own two books: returns inwards (from customers) and returns outwards (to suppliers). Cash and bank movements go to the cash book. Everything left over — buying a van on credit, correcting an error, opening entries, the owner taking goods — goes to the GENERAL JOURNAL. The two traps are a credit purchase of a non-current asset, which is not "purchases", and a service supplied on credit, which is not "sales".
> **(BM)** Sesuatu dokumen dicatat dalam buku catatan pertama sebelum ia sampai ke lejar. Buku yang mana bergantung pada dua perkara: apa yang diniagakan, dan sama ada ia secara kredit. Jurnal JUALAN dan BELIAN mengambil urusan kredit bagi BARANG YANG DINIAGAKAN OLEH PERNIAGAAN — bukan yang lain. Pulangan mempunyai dua buku sendiri: pulangan masuk (daripada pelanggan) dan pulangan keluar (kepada pembekal). Pergerakan tunai dan bank masuk ke buku tunai. Selebihnya — membeli van secara kredit, membetulkan kesilapan, catatan pembukaan, pemilik mengambil barang — masuk ke JURNAL AM. Dua perangkapnya ialah belian aset bukan semasa secara kredit, yang bukan "belian", dan perkhidmatan diberi secara kredit, yang bukan "jualan".
- → 

**`bfe-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · books-of-first-entry

- *Question (EN)* A shop sells goods on credit to a customer. Which book records it first?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah kedai menjual barang secara kredit kepada pelanggan. Buku manakah merekodnya dahulu?**
- *Choices* Sales journal → **Jurnal jualan** · General journal → **Jurnal am** · Cash book → **Buku tunai** · Purchases journal → **Jurnal belian**
- *Why (EN)* Credit sales of the goods the business trades in — that is exactly what the sales journal is for.
- *Why (BM)* **Jualan kredit barang yang diniagakan oleh perniagaan — itulah tujuan jurnal jualan.**
- → 

**`bfe-002`** · classify · difficulty 4 · books-of-first-entry

- *Question (EN)* A trading business buys a delivery van on credit. Which book records it first?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah perniagaan perdagangan membeli van penghantaran secara kredit. Buku manakah merekodnya dahulu?**
- *Choices* General journal → **Jurnal am** · Purchases journal → **Jurnal belian** · Cash book → **Buku tunai** · Sales journal → **Jurnal jualan**
- *Why (EN)* The purchases journal is only for goods bought to resell. A van is a non-current asset, so it goes to the general journal.
- *Why (BM)* **Jurnal belian hanya untuk barang yang dibeli untuk dijual semula. Van ialah aset bukan semasa, jadi ia masuk ke jurnal am.**
- → 

**`bfe-003`** · classify · difficulty 3 · books-of-first-entry

- *Question (EN)* A customer returns goods that were sold to them on credit. Which book?
- *Question (BM)* **Seorang pelanggan memulangkan barang yang dijual kepadanya secara kredit. Buku yang mana?**
- *Choices* Returns inwards journal → **Jurnal pulangan masuk** · Returns outwards journal → **Jurnal pulangan keluar** · Sales journal → **Jurnal jualan** · General journal → **Jurnal am**
- *Why (EN)* The goods come back IN to the business, so it is returns inwards.
- *Why (BM)* **Barang itu kembali MASUK ke perniagaan, jadi ia pulangan masuk.**
- → 

**`bfe-004`** · classify · difficulty 3 · books-of-first-entry

- *Question (EN)* The business returns faulty goods to its supplier. Which book?
- *Question (BM)* **Perniagaan memulangkan barang rosak kepada pembekalnya. Buku yang mana?**
- *Choices* Returns outwards journal → **Jurnal pulangan keluar** · Returns inwards journal → **Jurnal pulangan masuk** · Purchases journal → **Jurnal belian** · General journal → **Jurnal am**
- *Why (EN)* The goods go back OUT to the supplier. Naming these from the goods’ direction, not from who is unhappy, is what keeps them straight.
- *Why (BM)* **Barang itu kembali KELUAR kepada pembekal. Menamakannya mengikut arah barang, bukan mengikut siapa yang tidak berpuas hati, itulah yang menjadikannya jelas.**
- → 

**`bfe-005`** · classify · difficulty 4 · books-of-first-entry

- *Question (EN)* A laundry provides washing services to a hotel on credit. Which book records it first?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah dobi memberikan perkhidmatan mencuci kepada sebuah hotel secara kredit. Buku manakah merekodnya dahulu?**
- *Choices* General journal → **Jurnal am** · Sales journal → **Jurnal jualan** · Cash book → **Buku tunai** · Returns inwards journal → **Jurnal pulangan masuk**
- *Why (EN)* The sales journal is for goods the business trades in. A service is not goods, so it goes to the general journal.
- *Why (BM)* **Jurnal jualan adalah untuk barang yang diniagakan. Perkhidmatan bukan barang, jadi ia masuk ke jurnal am.**
- → 

**`bfe-006`** · classify · difficulty 3 · books-of-first-entry

- *Question (EN)* An error found in the ledger has to be corrected. Which book records the correction?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu kesilapan yang dijumpai dalam lejar perlu dibetulkan. Buku manakah merekod pembetulan itu?**
- *Choices* General journal → **Jurnal am** · Cash book → **Buku tunai** · Sales journal → **Jurnal jualan** · Purchases journal → **Jurnal belian**
- *Why (EN)* Corrections belong to no trading book, so they go to the general journal — where the narration explains what happened.
- *Why (BM)* **Pembetulan bukan milik mana-mana buku perdagangan, jadi ia masuk ke jurnal am — di mana keterangan menjelaskan apa yang berlaku.**
- → 

**`bfe-007`** · classify · difficulty 3 · books-of-first-entry

- *Question (EN)* Goods are sold and paid for in cash immediately. Which book records it first?
- *Question (BM)* **Barang dijual dan dibayar secara tunai serta-merta. Buku manakah merekodnya dahulu?**
- *Choices* Cash book → **Buku tunai** · Sales journal → **Jurnal jualan** · General journal → **Jurnal am** · Returns inwards journal → **Jurnal pulangan masuk**
- *Why (EN)* The sales journal takes CREDIT sales only. Money moved here, so it is a cash book entry.
- *Why (BM)* **Jurnal jualan hanya mengambil jualan KREDIT. Wang bergerak di sini, jadi ia catatan buku tunai.**
- → 

#### Petty Cash & the Imprest

`l49-petty-cash` · BM title: `Tunai Runcit & Panjar`

> **Worked example (EN)** Small payments — postage, teh tarik for a meeting, a taxi — are not worth a cheque each, so a petty cashier holds a float. Under the IMPREST system that float is a fixed amount. At the end of the period the cashier is reimbursed with exactly what was spent, which restores the float to the same fixed figure it started at. So: reimbursement = total payments, and imprest = balance still in hand + reimbursement received. That second one is the way an exam usually asks it — you are given the leftover and the top-up, and the float is their sum.
> **(BM)** Bayaran kecil — pos, teh tarik untuk mesyuarat, teksi — tidak berbaloi dikeluarkan cek satu per satu, jadi juruwang runcit memegang panjar. Di bawah sistem PANJAR, jumlah panjar itu ialah amaun tetap. Pada akhir tempoh, juruwang dibayar balik tepat seperti yang dibelanjakan, yang memulihkan panjar kepada angka tetap yang sama seperti pada permulaan. Jadi: bayaran balik = jumlah pembayaran, dan panjar = baki yang masih ada + bayaran balik diterima. Yang kedua itu ialah cara peperiksaan biasanya bertanya — anda diberi bakinya dan tambahannya, dan panjar ialah jumlah kedua-duanya.
- → 

**`pc-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · petty-cash

- *Question (EN)* Under the imprest system, what happens to the petty cash float at the start of each period?
- *Question (BM)* **Di bawah sistem panjar, apakah yang berlaku kepada panjar tunai runcit pada permulaan setiap tempoh?**
- *Choices* It is restored to a fixed amount → **Ia dipulihkan kepada amaun tetap** · It grows each period → **Ia bertambah setiap tempoh** · It is cleared to zero → **Ia dikosongkan kepada sifar**
- *Why (EN)* That fixed amount is the imprest. Restoring it means the cashier always starts with the same sum.
- *Why (BM)* **Amaun tetap itu ialah panjar. Memulihkannya bermakna juruwang sentiasa bermula dengan jumlah yang sama.**
- → 

**`pc-002`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · petty-cash

- *Question (EN)* The petty cash float is RM300. During the month RM175 was paid out. How much is reimbursed at month end?
- *Question (BM)* **Panjar tunai runcit ialah RM300. Sepanjang bulan itu RM175 telah dibayar. Berapakah yang dibayar balik pada akhir bulan?**
- *Why (EN)* Exactly what was spent, so the float returns to RM300.
- *Why (BM)* **Tepat seperti yang dibelanjakan, supaya panjar kembali kepada RM300.**
- → 

**`pc-003`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · petty-cash

- *Question (EN)* The float is RM300 and RM175 was paid out during the month. How much is still in hand just before the reimbursement?
- *Question (BM)* **Panjar ialah RM300 dan RM175 telah dibayar sepanjang bulan. Berapakah yang masih ada sebelum bayaran balik dibuat?**
- *Why (EN)* 300 − 175 = 125. Add the 175 reimbursement and the float is back to 300.
- *Why (BM)* **300 − 175 = 125. Tambah bayaran balik 175 dan panjar kembali kepada 300.**
- → 

**`pc-004`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · petty-cash

- *Question (EN)* A petty cash book shows a balance brought down of RM48 and RM202 received from the chief cashier. What is the imprest amount?
- *Question (BM)* **Buku tunai runcit menunjukkan baki dibawa ke bawah RM48 dan RM202 diterima daripada ketua juruwang. Berapakah amaun panjar?**
- *Why (EN)* 48 + 202 = 250. The leftover plus the top-up IS the float — the payments figure is not needed at all.
- *Why (BM)* **48 + 202 = 250. Baki yang tinggal campur tambahan ITULAH panjar — angka pembayaran tidak diperlukan langsung.**
- → 

**`pc-005`** · classify · difficulty 3 · petty-cash, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the petty cash book, on which side is a payment for postage entered?
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam buku tunai runcit, di sebelah manakah bayaran untuk pos dicatatkan?**
- *Choices* Credit → **Kredit** · Debit → **Debit**
- *Why (EN)* Money leaving is a credit, exactly as in the cash book. The reimbursement coming in is the debit.
- *Why (BM)* **Wang keluar ialah kredit, sama seperti dalam buku tunai. Bayaran balik yang masuk ialah debit.**
- → 

**`pc-006`** · classify · difficulty 3 · petty-cash

- *Question (EN)* Which of these would NOT be paid from petty cash?
- *Question (BM)* **Antara berikut, yang manakah TIDAK akan dibayar daripada tunai runcit?**
- *Choices* A new delivery van → **Sebuah van penghantaran baharu** · Postage stamps → **Setem pos** · A taxi fare → **Tambang teksi** · Cleaning materials → **Bahan pencuci**
- *Why (EN)* Petty cash is for small everyday payments. An asset costing thousands is paid by cheque and recorded properly.
- *Why (BM)* **Tunai runcit adalah untuk bayaran kecil harian. Aset bernilai ribuan dibayar dengan cek dan direkod dengan sewajarnya.**
- → 

### Ledger & T-Accounts

`t5-ledger` · BM title: `Lejar & Akaun T`
- → 

#### Balancing Off

`l30-balance-off` · BM title: `Mengimbangkan Akaun`

> **Worked example (EN)** Closing an account has two halves that sit on OPPOSITE sides. The balance c/d is written on the smaller side, because its job is to make the two totals equal. The balance b/d then comes down on the larger side — and that is the side the account really has its balance on. Trade Receivables normally ends with a debit balance and Trade Payables with a credit one, but the entry that closes each of them is written on the other side.
> **(BM)** Menutup akaun ada dua bahagian yang berada di sebelah BERTENTANGAN. Baki c/d dicatat di sebelah yang lebih kecil, kerana tugasnya menyamakan kedua-dua jumlah. Baki b/d kemudian dibawa turun di sebelah yang lebih besar — dan itulah sebelah baki sebenar akaun tersebut. Akaun Belum Terima biasanya berakhir dengan baki debit dan Akaun Belum Bayar dengan baki kredit, tetapi catatan yang menutup setiap satunya ditulis di sebelah yang satu lagi.
- → 

**`dc-006`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · ledger, balancing-off, receivable

- *Question (EN)* A Trade Receivables account has debits of RM5,000 and credits of RM3,200. On which side is the balance c/d written?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun Belum Terima mempunyai debit RM5,000 dan kredit RM3,200. Di sebelah mana baki c/d dicatat?**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The balance c/d goes on the SMALLER side — it is the figure that makes the two totals equal. Here the credit side is smaller, so c/d is written there even though the account has a debit balance.
- *Why (BM)* **Baki c/d dicatat di sebelah yang LEBIH KECIL — ia angka yang menyamakan kedua-dua jumlah. Di sini sebelah kredit lebih kecil, jadi c/d dicatat di situ walaupun akaun ini berbaki debit.**
- → 

**`dc-007`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · ledger, balancing-off, receivable

- *Question (EN)* Same account: debits RM5,000, credits RM3,200. On which side does the balance b/d appear when the account reopens?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun yang sama: debit RM5,000, kredit RM3,200. Di sebelah mana baki b/d muncul apabila akaun dibuka semula?**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The balance b/d comes down on the opposite side to the c/d — the LARGER side. A receivables account with a debit balance means customers still owe the business RM1,800.
- *Why (BM)* **Baki b/d dibawa turun di sebelah bertentangan dengan c/d — sebelah yang LEBIH BESAR. Akaun Belum Terima berbaki debit bermakna pelanggan masih berhutang RM1,800 kepada perniagaan.**
- → 

**`dc-008`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · ledger, balancing-off, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* A Trade Payables account has credits of RM7,000 and debits of RM4,500. On which side is the balance c/d written?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun Belum Bayar mempunyai kredit RM7,000 dan debit RM4,500. Di sebelah mana baki c/d dicatat?**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Again the c/d sits on the smaller side, which here is the debit side. The balance b/d then comes down on the credit side — RM2,500 still owed to suppliers.
- *Why (BM)* **Sekali lagi c/d berada di sebelah yang lebih kecil, iaitu sebelah debit di sini. Baki b/d kemudian dibawa turun di sebelah kredit — RM2,500 masih terhutang kepada pembekal.**
- → 

**`ta-006`** · t_account · difficulty 3 · ledger, balancing-off, receivable

- *Question (EN)* Post these to the Trade Receivables account, then find the closing balance.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke Akaun Belum Terima, kemudian cari baki penutup.**
- *Why (EN)* Debits 6,400. Credits 400 + 3,500 + 200 = 4,100. Balance 2,300 on the debit side — three separate things reduce what a customer owes, and only one increases it.
- *Why (BM)* **Debit 6,400. Kredit 400 + 3,500 + 200 = 4,100. Baki 2,300 di sebelah debit — tiga perkara berasingan mengurangkan hutang pelanggan, hanya satu yang menambahnya.**
- → 

**`ta-007`** · t_account · difficulty 3 · ledger, balancing-off, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* Post these to the Trade Payables account, then find the closing balance.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke Akaun Belum Bayar, kemudian cari baki penutup.**
- *Why (EN)* Credits 2,500 + 5,000 = 7,500. Debits 4,200 + 300 = 4,500. Balance 3,000 on the credit side. A discount received reduces the debt just as a payment does.
- *Why (BM)* **Kredit 2,500 + 5,000 = 7,500. Debit 4,200 + 300 = 4,500. Baki 3,000 di sebelah kredit. Diskaun diterima mengurangkan hutang sama seperti bayaran.**
- → 

**`ta-008`** · t_account · difficulty 4 · ledger, balancing-off, receivable

- *Question (EN)* Post these to the Trade Receivables account, then find the closing balance. Check the side carefully.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke Akaun Belum Terima, kemudian cari baki penutup. Semak sebelahnya dengan teliti.**
- *Why (EN)* Debits 1,800, credits 2,300. The balance is 500 on the CREDIT side. A receivables account usually has a debit balance, but not always: the customer has paid for goods they then returned, so the business now owes them 500.
- *Why (BM)* **Debit 1,800, kredit 2,300. Baki ialah 500 di sebelah KREDIT. Akaun Belum Terima biasanya berbaki debit, tetapi bukan selalu: pelanggan telah membayar untuk barang yang kemudiannya dipulangkan, jadi kini perniagaan yang berhutang 500 kepadanya.**
- → 

**`ta-009`** · t_account · difficulty 4 · ledger, balancing-off, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* Post these to the Bank account, then find the closing balance. Check the side carefully.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke akaun Bank, kemudian cari baki penutup. Semak sebelahnya dengan teliti.**
- *Why (EN)* Debits 5,200, credits 6,400. Balance 1,200 on the credit side — the business has spent more through the bank than it put in, so the account is overdrawn. A credit bank balance is a liability, not an asset.
- *Why (BM)* **Debit 5,200, kredit 6,400. Baki 1,200 di sebelah kredit — perniagaan membelanjakan lebih daripada yang dimasukkan, jadi akaun ini terlebih keluar (overdraf). Baki bank berbaki kredit ialah liabiliti, bukan aset.**
- → 

**`ta-010`** · t_account · difficulty 4 · ledger, balancing-off, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* Post these to the Trade Payables account, then find the closing balance.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke Akaun Belum Bayar, kemudian cari baki penutup.**
- *Why (EN)* Credits 1,600 + 4,400 = 6,000. Debits 500 + 3,000 = 3,500. Balance 2,500 on the credit side. Returns outwards are goods sent back to the supplier, so they cut the debt — debit side.
- *Why (BM)* **Kredit 1,600 + 4,400 = 6,000. Debit 500 + 3,000 = 3,500. Baki 2,500 di sebelah kredit. Pulangan keluar ialah barang dihantar balik kepada pembekal, jadi ia mengurangkan hutang — sebelah debit.**
- → 

#### Fading the Balancing Off

`l31-faded-balancing` · BM title: `Melunturkan Pengimbangan Akaun`

> **Worked example (EN)** Closing any account runs the same four moves: total each side, put the difference on the smaller side as the balance c/d, then bring it down on the larger side as the balance b/d. Each rung below hands you less of it.
> **(BM)** Menutup mana-mana akaun mengikut empat langkah yang sama: jumlahkan setiap sebelah, letakkan bezanya di sebelah yang lebih kecil sebagai baki c/d, kemudian bawa turun di sebelah yang lebih besar sebagai baki b/d. Setiap anak tangga di bawah memberi anda kurang daripadanya.
- → 

**`fd-1001`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · ledger, balancing-off, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Trade Receivables. Debit side: balance b/d RM3,000, credit sales RM8,500. Credit side: cash received RM6,000, discount allowed RM400, returns inwards RM300.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Akaun Belum Terima. Sebelah debit: baki b/d RM3,000, jualan kredit RM8,500. Sebelah kredit: tunai diterima RM6,000, diskaun diberi RM400, pulangan masuk RM300.**
- *Question (EN)* The account is almost closed. Finish the last two lines.
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun ini hampir ditutup. Selesaikan dua baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Total of the debit side → **Jumlah sebelah debit**
  - Total of the credit side → **Jumlah sebelah kredit**
  - Side the balance c/d is written on → **Sebelah baki c/d dicatat** · answer: Credit → **Kredit**
  - Balance c/d → **Baki c/d**
  - ␣ Side the balance b/d comes down on → **Sebelah baki b/d dibawa turun** · answer: Debit → **Debit**
  - ␣ Balance b/d → **Baki b/d**
- *Why (EN)* The c/d of 4,800 was written on the credit side to bring that side up to 11,500. The b/d comes down on the DEBIT side — customers still owe 4,800.
- *Why (BM)* **Baki c/d 4,800 dicatat di sebelah kredit untuk menaikkan sebelah itu kepada 11,500. Baki b/d dibawa turun di sebelah DEBIT — pelanggan masih berhutang 4,800.**
- → 

**`fd-1002`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · ledger, balancing-off, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Trade Payables. Credit side: balance b/d RM2,400, credit purchases RM9,000. Debit side: paid supplier RM5,200, discount received RM300, returns outwards RM500.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Akaun Belum Bayar. Sebelah kredit: baki b/d RM2,400, belian kredit RM9,000. Sebelah debit: bayar pembekal RM5,200, diskaun diterima RM300, pulangan keluar RM500.**
- *Question (EN)* Four lines are missing. Close the account.
- *Question (BM)* **Empat baris hilang. Tutup akaun ini.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Total of the debit side → **Jumlah sebelah debit**
  - Total of the credit side → **Jumlah sebelah kredit**
  - ␣ Side the balance c/d is written on → **Sebelah baki c/d dicatat** · answer: Debit → **Debit**
  - ␣ Balance c/d → **Baki c/d**
  - ␣ Side the balance b/d comes down on → **Sebelah baki b/d dibawa turun** · answer: Credit → **Kredit**
  - ␣ Balance b/d → **Baki b/d**
- *Why (EN)* 11,400 − 6,000 = 5,400. On a payables account the smaller side is the debit side, so the c/d goes there and the b/d comes down as a CREDIT balance — 5,400 still owed to suppliers.
- *Why (BM)* **11,400 − 6,000 = 5,400. Dalam Akaun Belum Bayar, sebelah yang lebih kecil ialah sebelah debit, jadi c/d dicatat di situ dan b/d dibawa turun sebagai baki KREDIT — 5,400 masih terhutang kepada pembekal.**
- → 

**`fd-1003`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · ledger, balancing-off, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Trade Receivables. Debit side: balance b/d RM1,500, credit sales RM2,600. Credit side: cash received RM4,300, returns inwards RM200.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Akaun Belum Terima. Sebelah debit: baki b/d RM1,500, jualan kredit RM2,600. Sebelah kredit: tunai diterima RM4,300, pulangan masuk RM200.**
- *Question (EN)* Nothing is worked for you. Close the account from the scenario alone.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiada apa yang dikira untuk anda. Tutup akaun ini daripada senario sahaja.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - ␣ Total of the debit side → **Jumlah sebelah debit**
  - ␣ Total of the credit side → **Jumlah sebelah kredit**
  - ␣ Side the balance c/d is written on → **Sebelah baki c/d dicatat** · answer: Debit → **Debit**
  - ␣ Balance c/d → **Baki c/d**
  - ␣ Side the balance b/d comes down on → **Sebelah baki b/d dibawa turun** · answer: Credit → **Kredit**
  - ␣ Balance b/d → **Baki b/d**
- *Why (EN)* 4,500 − 4,100 = 400. This time the credit side is the larger one, so the c/d is written on the DEBIT side and the b/d comes down as a credit balance: the customer has overpaid, and the business owes 400 back. The rule is about which side is bigger, never about which account it is.
- *Why (BM)* **4,500 − 4,100 = 400. Kali ini sebelah kredit yang lebih besar, jadi c/d dicatat di sebelah DEBIT dan b/d dibawa turun sebagai baki kredit: pelanggan telah terlebih bayar, dan perniagaan berhutang 400 kepadanya. Peraturannya tentang sebelah mana yang lebih besar, bukan tentang akaun apa itu.**
- → 

### Income Statement

`t7-income-statement` · BM title: `Penyata Pendapatan`
- → 

#### Valuing the Inventory

`l50-inventory-valuation` · BM title: `Menilai Inventori`

> **Worked example (EN)** Closing inventory is valued at the LOWER of what it cost and what it can be sold for. Compare the two figures line by line — not on the totals — and take the lower one each time. The reason is prudence: an asset must never be carried at more than it can actually fetch, and since closing inventory is deducted in the trading account, overstating it understates cost of sales and overstates profit. That is why the rule exists, and why it is applied per product rather than to the total, where a gain on one line could hide a fall on another.
> **(BM)** Inventori akhir dinilai pada nilai yang LEBIH RENDAH antara kosnya dan harga ia boleh dijual. Bandingkan kedua-dua angka baris demi baris — bukan pada jumlahnya — dan ambil yang lebih rendah setiap kali. Sebabnya ialah berhemat: sesuatu aset tidak boleh dibawa pada nilai melebihi harga sebenar yang boleh diperoleh, dan kerana inventori akhir ditolak dalam akaun perdagangan, menyatakan lebih akan menyatakan kurang kos jualan dan menyatakan lebih untung. Itulah sebab peraturan ini wujud, dan sebab ia dikenakan setiap produk dan bukan pada jumlahnya, di mana kenaikan pada satu baris boleh menyembunyikan penurunan pada baris lain.
- → 

**`iv-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · inventory, inventory-valuation

- *Question (EN)* At what value is closing inventory shown?
- *Question (BM)* **Pada nilai manakah inventori akhir ditunjukkan?**
- *Choices* The lower of cost and market value → **Nilai lebih rendah antara kos dan harga pasaran** · Always cost → **Sentiasa kos** · Always market value → **Sentiasa harga pasaran** · The higher of cost and market value → **Nilai lebih tinggi antara kos dan harga pasaran**
- *Why (EN)* Prudence: never carry an asset above what it can actually fetch.
- *Why (BM)* **Berhemat: jangan sekali-kali membawa aset melebihi harga sebenar yang boleh diperoleh.**
- → 

**`iv-002`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · inventory, inventory-valuation

- *Question (EN)* One product cost RM12,000 and can now be sold for RM11,400. At what value is it shown?
- *Question (BM)* **Satu produk berkos RM12,000 dan kini boleh dijual pada RM11,400. Pada nilai manakah ia ditunjukkan?**
- *Why (EN)* The market value is lower, so that is the figure. Recognise the fall now rather than pretend it has not happened.
- *Why (BM)* **Harga pasaran lebih rendah, jadi itulah angkanya. Iktiraf penurunan itu sekarang dan bukan berpura-pura ia tidak berlaku.**
- → 

**`iv-003`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · inventory, inventory-valuation

- *Question (EN)* Product A: cost RM5,400, market RM5,600. Product B: cost RM3,800, market RM3,500. What is the total value of closing inventory?
- *Question (BM)* **Produk A: kos RM5,400, pasaran RM5,600. Produk B: kos RM3,800, pasaran RM3,500. Berapakah jumlah nilai inventori akhir?**
- *Why (EN)* Compare each line and take the lower: 5,400 for A and 3,500 for B, so 8,900. Comparing the TOTALS (9,200 against 9,100) would give 9,100 and let A’s gain hide B’s fall.
- *Why (BM)* **Bandingkan setiap baris dan ambil yang lebih rendah: 5,400 bagi A dan 3,500 bagi B, jadi 8,900. Membandingkan JUMLAH (9,200 berbanding 9,100) akan memberi 9,100 dan membiarkan kenaikan A menyembunyikan penurunan B.**
- → 

**`iv-004`** · classify · difficulty 4 · inventory, inventory-valuation

- *Question (EN)* Closing inventory has been overstated. What happens to gross profit?
- *Question (BM)* **Inventori akhir telah dinyatakan lebih. Apakah yang berlaku kepada untung kasar?**
- *Choices* It is overstated → **Ia dinyatakan lebih** · It is understated → **Ia dinyatakan kurang** · It is unchanged → **Ia tidak berubah**
- *Why (EN)* Closing inventory is deducted in arriving at cost of sales, so a bigger figure means a smaller cost of sales and a bigger profit.
- *Why (BM)* **Inventori akhir ditolak untuk mendapatkan kos jualan, jadi angka yang lebih besar bermakna kos jualan lebih kecil dan untung lebih besar.**
- → 

**`iv-005`** · classify · difficulty 4 · inventory, inventory-valuation

- *Question (EN)* Closing inventory has been overstated. What happens to cost of sales?
- *Question (BM)* **Inventori akhir telah dinyatakan lebih. Apakah yang berlaku kepada kos jualan?**
- *Choices* It is understated → **Ia dinyatakan kurang** · It is overstated → **Ia dinyatakan lebih** · It is unchanged → **Ia tidak berubah**
- *Why (EN)* The two move opposite ways: overstating what is left understates what was sold.
- *Why (BM)* **Kedua-duanya bergerak berlawanan: menyatakan lebih apa yang tinggal menyatakan kurang apa yang telah dijual.**
- → 

**`iv-006`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · inventory, inventory-valuation

- *Question (EN)* Closing inventory was overstated by RM900. By how much is gross profit overstated?
- *Question (BM)* **Inventori akhir dinyatakan lebih sebanyak RM900. Berapakah untung kasar dinyatakan lebih?**
- *Why (EN)* It carries straight through: 900 less cost of sales is 900 more gross profit.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia terus terbawa: kos jualan kurang 900 bermakna untung kasar lebih 900.**
- → 

### Depreciation

`t10-depreciation` · BM title: `Susut Nilai`
- → 

#### Reducing Balance

`l44-reducing-balance` · BM title: `Baki Berkurangan`

> **Worked example (EN)** Reducing balance charges the rate on what the asset is still worth in the books — the book value, also called the carrying amount — not on its cost. So the first year's charge is the largest and every year after it is smaller. Year 1: rate × cost (there is no accumulated depreciation yet). Year 2 onwards: rate × (cost − accumulated depreciation to date). The trap is applying the rate to cost every year: that is the straight-line answer wearing a reducing-balance question.
> **(BM)** Baki berkurangan mengenakan kadar ke atas nilai aset yang masih tinggal dalam buku — nilai buku, juga dipanggil amaun bawaan — bukan ke atas kosnya. Jadi caj tahun pertama adalah yang terbesar dan setiap tahun selepasnya lebih kecil. Tahun 1: kadar × kos (belum ada susut nilai terkumpul). Tahun 2 dan seterusnya: kadar × (kos − susut nilai terkumpul setakat ini). Perangkapnya ialah mengenakan kadar ke atas kos setiap tahun: itu jawapan garis lurus dalam soalan baki berkurangan.
- → 

**`rb-001`** · numeric · difficulty 2 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* A machine cost RM20,000. Depreciation is 10% per year on the reducing balance. What is the charge for year 1?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah mesin berkos RM20,000. Susut nilai ialah 10% setahun atas baki berkurangan. Berapakah caj bagi tahun 1?**
- *Why (EN)* In year 1 there is no accumulated depreciation yet, so the book value IS the cost: 10% × 20,000 = 2,000. Year 1 is the one year both methods agree.
- *Why (BM)* **Pada tahun 1 belum ada susut nilai terkumpul, jadi nilai buku IALAH kos: 10% × 20,000 = 2,000. Tahun 1 ialah satu-satunya tahun kedua-dua kaedah sama.**
- → 

**`rb-002`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* The same machine cost RM20,000 and was charged RM2,000 in year 1. At 10% on the reducing balance, what is the charge for year 2?
- *Question (BM)* **Mesin yang sama berkos RM20,000 dan dicaj RM2,000 pada tahun 1. Pada 10% atas baki berkurangan, berapakah caj bagi tahun 2?**
- *Why (EN)* The book value is now 20,000 − 2,000 = 18,000, so 10% × 18,000 = 1,800. The charge falls because the base fell.
- *Why (BM)* **Nilai buku kini 20,000 − 2,000 = 18,000, jadi 10% × 18,000 = 1,800. Caj berkurang kerana asasnya berkurang.**
- → 

**`rb-003`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* Furniture cost RM12,000 and accumulated depreciation is RM3,200. Depreciation is 5% per year on the reducing balance. What is this year’s charge?
- *Question (BM)* **Perabot berkos RM12,000 dan susut nilai terkumpul ialah RM3,200. Susut nilai ialah 5% setahun atas baki berkurangan. Berapakah caj bagi tahun ini?**
- *Why (EN)* 5% × (12,000 − 3,200) = 5% × 8,800 = 440. Charging 5% on the 12,000 cost gives 600 — that is the straight-line answer, and it is the wrong one here.
- *Why (BM)* **5% × (12,000 − 3,200) = 5% × 8,800 = 440. Mengenakan 5% atas kos 12,000 memberi 600 — itu jawapan garis lurus, dan ia salah di sini.**
- → 

**`rb-004`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* A van cost RM50,000 and is depreciated at 20% per year on the reducing balance. What is the charge in year 3?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah van berkos RM50,000 dan disusutnilaikan 20% setahun atas baki berkurangan. Berapakah caj pada tahun 3?**
- *Why (EN)* Year 1: 20% × 50,000 = 10,000, leaving 40,000. Year 2: 20% × 40,000 = 8,000, leaving 32,000. Year 3: 20% × 32,000 = 6,400.
- *Why (BM)* **Tahun 1: 20% × 50,000 = 10,000, tinggal 40,000. Tahun 2: 20% × 40,000 = 8,000, tinggal 32,000. Tahun 3: 20% × 32,000 = 6,400.**
- → 

**`rb-005`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* Equipment cost RM20,000, depreciated at 10% per year on the reducing balance. What is the book value after two years?
- *Question (BM)* **Peralatan berkos RM20,000, disusutnilaikan 10% setahun atas baki berkurangan. Berapakah nilai buku selepas dua tahun?**
- *Why (EN)* 20,000 − 2,000 = 18,000 after year 1; 18,000 − 1,800 = 16,200 after year 2.
- *Why (BM)* **20,000 − 2,000 = 18,000 selepas tahun 1; 18,000 − 1,800 = 16,200 selepas tahun 2.**
- → 

**`rb-101`** · classify · difficulty 2 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* Under the reducing balance method, the rate is applied to which figure?
- *Question (BM)* **Di bawah kaedah baki berkurangan, kadar dikenakan ke atas angka yang mana?**
- *Choices* Book value (carrying amount) → **Nilai buku (amaun bawaan)** · Original cost → **Kos asal** · Residual value → **Nilai baki**
- *Why (EN)* Cost less accumulated depreciation — what the asset is still worth in the books.
- *Why (BM)* **Kos tolak susut nilai terkumpul — nilai aset yang masih tinggal dalam buku.**
- → 

**`rb-102`** · classify · difficulty 2 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* Under reducing balance, what happens to the annual charge as the years pass?
- *Question (BM)* **Di bawah baki berkurangan, apakah yang berlaku kepada caj tahunan apabila tahun berlalu?**
- *Choices* It falls each year → **Ia berkurang setiap tahun** · It stays the same → **Ia kekal sama** · It rises each year → **Ia bertambah setiap tahun**
- *Why (EN)* The base it is charged on shrinks every year, so the charge shrinks with it.
- *Why (BM)* **Asas yang dikenakan caj mengecil setiap tahun, jadi caj turut mengecil.**
- → 

**`rb-103`** · classify · difficulty 3 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* An asset loses most of its value in its early years. Which method matches that pattern?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu aset kehilangan sebahagian besar nilainya pada tahun-tahun awal. Kaedah manakah yang sepadan dengan corak itu?**
- *Choices* Reducing balance → **Baki berkurangan** · Straight line → **Garis lurus**
- *Why (EN)* It charges most in year 1 and less every year after, which is how vehicles and computers actually lose value.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia mencaj paling banyak pada tahun 1 dan kurang setiap tahun selepasnya, iaitu cara kenderaan dan komputer benar-benar kehilangan nilai.**
- → 

**`rb-104`** · classify · difficulty 4 · depreciation, reducing-balance

- *Question (EN)* An asset with no residual value is depreciated at 20% per year. In YEAR 1, how do straight line and reducing balance compare?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu aset tanpa nilai baki disusutnilaikan 20% setahun. Pada TAHUN 1, bagaimanakah garis lurus dan baki berkurangan dibandingkan?**
- *Choices* The charge is the same → **Cajnya sama** · Reducing balance charges more → **Baki berkurangan mencaj lebih** · Straight line charges more → **Garis lurus mencaj lebih**
- *Why (EN)* In year 1 there is no accumulated depreciation, so both charge 20% of cost. They only separate from year 2.
- *Why (BM)* **Pada tahun 1 tiada susut nilai terkumpul, jadi kedua-duanya mencaj 20% daripada kos. Ia hanya berbeza mulai tahun 2.**
- → 

#### Selling the Asset

`l45-disposal` · BM title: `Menjual Aset`

> **Worked example (EN)** When an asset is sold, ask one question: did it fetch more or less than it was still worth in the books? Book value (carrying amount) = cost − accumulated depreciation. Proceeds above that is a profit on disposal; below it is a loss. Both go to the Income Statement. In the ledger a Disposal account collects three things — the cost (debit), the accumulated depreciation (credit) and the sale proceeds (credit) — and whatever is left over is the profit or the loss.
> **(BM)** Apabila sesuatu aset dijual, tanya satu soalan: adakah ia laku lebih atau kurang daripada nilai yang masih tinggal dalam buku? Nilai buku (amaun bawaan) = kos − susut nilai terkumpul. Hasil jualan melebihi itu ialah untung atas pelupusan; kurang daripadanya ialah rugi. Kedua-duanya masuk ke Penyata Pendapatan. Dalam lejar, Akaun Pelupusan mengumpul tiga perkara — kos (debit), susut nilai terkumpul (kredit) dan hasil jualan (kredit) — dan bakinya ialah untung atau rugi itu.
- → 

**`ds-001`** · numeric · difficulty 2 · depreciation, disposal

- *Question (EN)* A van cost RM30,000 and accumulated depreciation on it is RM18,000. What is its book value?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah van berkos RM30,000 dan susut nilai terkumpulnya ialah RM18,000. Berapakah nilai bukunya?**
- *Why (EN)* 30,000 − 18,000 = 12,000. This is the figure the sale price has to beat.
- *Why (BM)* **30,000 − 18,000 = 12,000. Inilah angka yang perlu diatasi oleh harga jualan.**
- → 

**`ds-002`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · depreciation, disposal

- *Question (EN)* A van with a book value of RM12,000 is sold for RM15,000. How much is the profit on disposal?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah van dengan nilai buku RM12,000 dijual pada harga RM15,000. Berapakah untung atas pelupusan?**
- *Why (EN)* 15,000 − 12,000 = 3,000. It sold for more than the books said it was worth.
- *Why (BM)* **15,000 − 12,000 = 3,000. Ia dijual lebih daripada nilai yang dinyatakan dalam buku.**
- → 

**`ds-003`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · depreciation, disposal

- *Question (EN)* A machine with a book value of RM5,800 is sold for RM4,000. How much is the LOSS on disposal?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah mesin dengan nilai buku RM5,800 dijual pada harga RM4,000. Berapakah RUGI atas pelupusan?**
- *Why (EN)* 5,800 − 4,000 = 1,800. Give the size of the loss as a positive figure; the word "loss" already carries the direction.
- *Why (BM)* **5,800 − 4,000 = 1,800. Nyatakan saiz rugi sebagai angka positif; perkataan "rugi" sudah membawa arahnya.**
- → 

**`ds-004`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · depreciation, disposal

- *Question (EN)* Equipment cost RM40,000 with accumulated depreciation of RM25,000. It is sold for RM12,000. How much is the LOSS on disposal?
- *Question (BM)* **Peralatan berkos RM40,000 dengan susut nilai terkumpul RM25,000. Ia dijual pada harga RM12,000. Berapakah RUGI atas pelupusan?**
- *Why (EN)* Book value is 40,000 − 25,000 = 15,000. Sold for 12,000, so the loss is 3,000. Comparing the proceeds with the COST instead would give 28,000, which ignores every year of depreciation already charged.
- *Why (BM)* **Nilai buku ialah 40,000 − 25,000 = 15,000. Dijual pada 12,000, jadi ruginya 3,000. Membandingkan hasil jualan dengan KOS pula memberi 28,000, yang mengabaikan setiap tahun susut nilai yang telah dicaj.**
- → 

**`ds-101`** · classify · difficulty 2 · depreciation, disposal

- *Question (EN)* An asset is sold for more than its book value. What is the result?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu aset dijual melebihi nilai bukunya. Apakah hasilnya?**
- *Choices* Profit on disposal → **Untung atas pelupusan** · Loss on disposal → **Rugi atas pelupusan**
- *Why (EN)* It was worth more than the books said, so the earlier depreciation charges were slightly too heavy.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia bernilai lebih daripada yang dinyatakan dalam buku, jadi caj susut nilai terdahulu agak terlebih.**
- → 

**`ds-102`** · classify · difficulty 3 · depreciation, disposal, statement-placement

- *Question (EN)* Where does a profit on disposal appear?
- *Question (BM)* **Di manakah untung atas pelupusan muncul?**
- *Choices* Income Statement → **Penyata Pendapatan** · Statement of Financial Position → **Penyata Kedudukan Kewangan**
- *Why (EN)* It is a gain of the period, not something the business still holds.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia keuntungan bagi tempoh tersebut, bukan sesuatu yang masih dipegang oleh perniagaan.**
- → 

**`ds-201`** · journal_entry · difficulty 3 · depreciation, disposal, double-entry

- *Question (EN)* A van costing RM30,000 is being disposed of. Transfer its COST out of the asset account.
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah van berkos RM30,000 sedang dilupuskan. Pindahkan KOSnya keluar daripada akaun aset.**
- *Accounts offered* Disposal → **Pelupusan** · Motor Vehicles → **Kenderaan Bermotor** · Accumulated Depreciation → **Susut Nilai Terkumpul** · Bank → **Bank**
- *Why (EN)* The asset account is emptied at cost and the cost lands in the Disposal account, where the reckoning happens.
- *Why (BM)* **Akaun aset dikosongkan pada kos dan kos itu masuk ke Akaun Pelupusan, tempat perkiraan dibuat.**
- → 

**`ds-202`** · journal_entry · difficulty 4 · depreciation, disposal, double-entry

- *Question (EN)* Accumulated depreciation of RM18,000 on the van being disposed of must be transferred out.
- *Question (BM)* **Susut nilai terkumpul RM18,000 atas van yang dilupuskan itu perlu dipindahkan keluar.**
- *Accounts offered* Accumulated Depreciation → **Susut Nilai Terkumpul** · Disposal → **Pelupusan** · Motor Vehicles → **Kenderaan Bermotor** · Depreciation Expense → **Belanja Susut Nilai**
- *Why (EN)* Accumulated Depreciation normally sits on the credit side, so clearing it means debiting it. The credit reduces the cost sitting in Disposal down towards the book value.
- *Why (BM)* **Susut Nilai Terkumpul biasanya berada di sebelah kredit, jadi mengosongkannya bermakna mendebitkannya. Kredit itu mengurangkan kos dalam Akaun Pelupusan menghampiri nilai buku.**
- → 

**`ds-203`** · journal_entry · difficulty 4 · depreciation, disposal, double-entry

- *Question (EN)* The disposal produced a profit of RM600. Record it.
- *Question (BM)* **Pelupusan itu menghasilkan untung RM600. Rekodkannya.**
- *Accounts offered* Disposal → **Pelupusan** · Profit on Disposal → **Untung atas Pelupusan** · Loss on Disposal → **Rugi atas Pelupusan** · Bank → **Bank**
- *Why (EN)* A profit is a gain, and gains are credited. Debiting the profit account instead would turn a good sale into an expense.
- *Why (BM)* **Untung ialah keuntungan, dan keuntungan dikreditkan. Mendebitkan akaun untung sebaliknya akan menjadikan jualan yang baik itu satu belanja.**
- → 

**`ds-301`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · depreciation, disposal, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Disposal account, on which side is the asset’s COST entered?
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Pelupusan, di sebelah manakah KOS aset dicatatkan?**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The cost arrives in Disposal as a debit; the accumulated depreciation and the sale proceeds come in on the credit side to work against it.
- *Why (BM)* **Kos masuk ke Akaun Pelupusan sebagai debit; susut nilai terkumpul dan hasil jualan masuk di sebelah kredit untuk mengimbanginya.**
- → 

**`err-401`** · spot_error · difficulty 4 · depreciation, disposal, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* A loss on disposal of RM600 was recorded as shown. Correct it.
- *Question (BM)* **Rugi atas pelupusan RM600 direkodkan seperti yang ditunjukkan. Betulkannya.**
- *Accounts offered* Loss on Disposal → **Rugi atas Pelupusan** · Disposal → **Pelupusan** · Profit on Disposal → **Untung atas Pelupusan** · Bank → **Bank**
- *Why (EN)* A loss is an expense, and expenses are debited. The entry as written credits the loss, which would add it to profit instead of taking it away.
- *Why (BM)* **Rugi ialah belanja, dan belanja didebitkan. Catatan seperti yang ditulis mengkreditkan rugi itu, yang akan menambahkannya kepada untung dan bukannya menolaknya.**
- → 

#### Fading the Disposal

`l46-faded-disposal` · BM title: `Melunturkan Pelupusan`

> **Worked example (EN)** The disposal workings run in a fixed order: cost, less accumulated depreciation, book value, sale proceeds, and the difference between the last two. Only then does the entry follow — a profit is credited, a loss is debited.
> **(BM)** Kerja kira pelupusan berjalan dalam susunan tetap: kos, tolak susut nilai terkumpul, nilai buku, hasil jualan, dan beza antara dua yang terakhir. Barulah catatan menyusul — untung dikreditkan, rugi didebitkan.
- → 

**`fd-1501`** · faded_step · difficulty 2 · depreciation, disposal, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* A van cost RM30,000. Accumulated depreciation on it is RM18,000. It is sold for RM15,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Sebuah van berkos RM30,000. Susut nilai terkumpulnya ialah RM18,000. Ia dijual pada harga RM15,000.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the entry.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan catatan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Cost → **Kos** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Less: accumulated depreciation → **Tolak: susut nilai terkumpul** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Book value (carrying amount) → **Nilai buku (amaun bawaan)** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Sale proceeds → **Hasil jualan** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Profit on disposal → **Untung atas pelupusan** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Account to debit → **Akaun untuk didebit** · answer: Disposal → **Pelupusan**
  - ␣ Account to credit → **Akaun untuk dikredit** · answer: Profit on Disposal → **Untung atas Pelupusan**
- *Wrong options offered* Motor Vehicles → **Kenderaan Bermotor** · Loss on Disposal → **Rugi atas Pelupusan** · Accumulated Depreciation → **Susut Nilai Terkumpul**
- *Why (EN)* A profit on disposal is a gain, so it is credited.
- *Why (BM)* **Untung atas pelupusan ialah keuntungan, jadi ia dikreditkan.**
- → 

**`fd-1502`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · depreciation, disposal, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* A van cost RM30,000. Accumulated depreciation on it is RM18,000. It is sold for RM15,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Sebuah van berkos RM30,000. Susut nilai terkumpulnya ialah RM18,000. Ia dijual pada harga RM15,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Work out the result and the entry.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris tiada. Kirakan hasilnya dan catatannya.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Cost → **Kos** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Less: accumulated depreciation → **Tolak: susut nilai terkumpul** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Book value (carrying amount) → **Nilai buku (amaun bawaan)** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Sale proceeds → **Hasil jualan** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - ␣ Profit on disposal → **Untung atas pelupusan** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - ␣ Account to debit → **Akaun untuk didebit** · answer: Disposal → **Pelupusan**
  - ␣ Account to credit → **Akaun untuk dikredit** · answer: Profit on Disposal → **Untung atas Pelupusan**
- *Wrong options offered* Motor Vehicles → **Kenderaan Bermotor** · Loss on Disposal → **Rugi atas Pelupusan** · Accumulated Depreciation → **Susut Nilai Terkumpul**
- *Why (EN)* 15,000 − 12,000 = 3,000 profit. Debit Disposal, credit Profit on Disposal.
- *Why (BM)* **15,000 − 12,000 = 3,000 untung. Debit Pelupusan, kredit Untung atas Pelupusan.**
- → 

**`fd-1503`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · depreciation, disposal, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* A van cost RM30,000. Accumulated depreciation on it is RM18,000. It is sold for RM15,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Sebuah van berkos RM30,000. Susut nilai terkumpulnya ialah RM18,000. Ia dijual pada harga RM15,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the cost and the depreciation are given. Work through the rest.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya kos dan susut nilai diberikan. Kerjakan selebihnya.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Cost → **Kos** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - Less: accumulated depreciation → **Tolak: susut nilai terkumpul** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - ␣ Book value (carrying amount) → **Nilai buku (amaun bawaan)** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - ␣ Sale proceeds → **Hasil jualan** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - ␣ Profit on disposal → **Untung atas pelupusan** · unit: RM → **(none)**
  - ␣ Account to debit → **Akaun untuk didebit** · answer: Disposal → **Pelupusan**
  - ␣ Account to credit → **Akaun untuk dikredit** · answer: Profit on Disposal → **Untung atas Pelupusan**
- *Wrong options offered* Motor Vehicles → **Kenderaan Bermotor** · Loss on Disposal → **Rugi atas Pelupusan** · Accumulated Depreciation → **Susut Nilai Terkumpul**
- *Why (EN)* Book value 30,000 − 18,000 = 12,000; sold for 15,000; profit 3,000, credited.
- *Why (BM)* **Nilai buku 30,000 − 18,000 = 12,000; dijual pada 15,000; untung 3,000, dikreditkan.**
- → 

#### Depreciation by Revaluation

`l54-revaluation-method` · BM title: `Susut Nilai Secara Penilaian Semula`

> **Worked example (EN)** Some assets are too many and too small to depreciate one by one — loose tools in a factory, crockery in a restaurant, packing cases. For those the charge is worked out by REVALUATION: value what is on hand at the start, add anything bought during the year, then value what is on hand at the end. Whatever has gone is the depreciation. Opening value + purchases − closing value = the charge for the year. There is no rate and no useful life; the valuation at the end does the work.
> **(BM)** Sesetengah aset terlalu banyak dan terlalu kecil untuk disusutnilaikan satu per satu — alat-alat kecil di kilang, pinggan mangkuk di restoran, peti pembungkus. Bagi aset itu, cajnya dikira secara PENILAIAN SEMULA: nilaikan apa yang ada pada permulaan, tambah apa-apa yang dibeli sepanjang tahun, kemudian nilaikan apa yang ada pada akhirnya. Apa yang telah hilang itulah susut nilainya. Nilai awal + belian − nilai akhir = caj bagi tahun tersebut. Tiada kadar dan tiada hayat berguna; penilaian pada akhir tahun yang melakukan kerjanya.
- → 

**`rv-001`** · classify · difficulty 3 · depreciation, revaluation-method

- *Question (EN)* Which asset is most likely to be depreciated by the revaluation method?
- *Question (BM)* **Aset manakah yang paling mungkin disusutnilaikan menggunakan kaedah penilaian semula?**
- *Choices* Loose tools → **Alat-alat kecil** · A delivery van → **Sebuah van penghantaran** · A factory building → **Sebuah bangunan kilang** · Office computers → **Komputer pejabat**
- *Why (EN)* They are numerous, individually cheap and constantly replaced — tracking each one’s cost and life would cost more than the tools.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia banyak, murah secara individu dan sentiasa diganti — menjejaki kos dan hayat setiap satu akan menelan kos melebihi alat itu sendiri.**
- → 

**`rv-002`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · depreciation, revaluation-method

- *Question (EN)* Loose tools were valued at RM550 at the start of the year and RM620 at the end. RM2,700 of tools were bought during the year. What is the depreciation charge?
- *Question (BM)* **Alat-alat kecil dinilai RM550 pada awal tahun dan RM620 pada akhir tahun. Alat bernilai RM2,700 dibeli sepanjang tahun. Berapakah caj susut nilainya?**
- *Why (EN)* 550 + 2,700 − 620 = 2,630. What is missing from the closing valuation has been used up.
- *Why (BM)* **550 + 2,700 − 620 = 2,630. Apa yang tiada dalam penilaian akhir telah digunakan.**
- → 

**`rv-003`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · depreciation, revaluation-method

- *Question (EN)* Crockery on hand was RM1,200 at the start and RM900 at the end. RM400 was spent on new crockery. What is the depreciation charge?
- *Question (BM)* **Pinggan mangkuk yang ada ialah RM1,200 pada awal dan RM900 pada akhir. RM400 dibelanjakan untuk pinggan mangkuk baharu. Berapakah caj susut nilainya?**
- *Why (EN)* 1,200 + 400 − 900 = 700.
- *Why (BM)* **1,200 + 400 − 900 = 700.**
- → 

**`rv-004`** · classify · difficulty 4 · depreciation, revaluation-method
