- *Question (EN)* Under the revaluation method, what determines the charge for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Di bawah kaedah penilaian semula, apakah yang menentukan caj bagi tahun tersebut?**
- *Choices* The valuation at the year end → **Penilaian pada akhir tahun** · A fixed percentage rate → **Kadar peratusan tetap** · The asset’s useful life → **Hayat berguna aset**
- *Why (EN)* No rate and no useful life are involved — someone counts and values what is still there.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada kadar dan tiada hayat berguna terlibat — seseorang mengira dan menilai apa yang masih ada.**
- → 

### Bank Reconciliation

`t12-bank-reconciliation` · BM title: `Penyesuaian Bank`
- → 

#### Cash Book vs Bank Statement

`l17-cash-book-vs-bank` · BM title: `Buku Tunai lwn Penyata Bank`

> **Worked example (EN)** The cash book and the bank statement disagree for two different reasons, and each is handled differently. (1) Things the BANK knows and you do not — bank charges, standing orders, direct credits, a dishonoured cheque: these are real transactions you simply have not recorded, so UPDATE THE CASH BOOK. (2) Timing differences — unpresented cheques and uncredited deposits: you recorded them correctly, the bank just has not caught up, so they belong in the RECONCILIATION STATEMENT, never in the cash book.
> **(BM)** Buku tunai dan penyata bank tidak sama kerana dua sebab berbeza, dan setiap satu dikendalikan dengan cara berlainan. (1) Perkara yang BANK tahu tetapi anda tidak — caj bank, arahan tetap, kredit terus, cek tak layan: ini urus niaga sebenar yang anda belum rekod, jadi KEMAS KINI BUKU TUNAI. (2) Perbezaan masa — cek belum dikemukakan dan deposit belum dikredit: anda sudah rekod dengan betul, cuma bank belum menyusul, jadi ia masuk dalam PENYATA PENYESUAIAN, bukan dalam buku tunai.
- → 

**`br-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* Bank charges of RM40 appear on the bank statement only. What do you do?
- *Question (BM)* **Caj bank RM40 muncul dalam penyata bank sahaja. Apa yang anda buat?**
- *Choices* Update the cash book → **Kemas kini buku tunai** · Show in the reconciliation statement → **Tunjuk dalam penyata penyesuaian**
- *Why (EN)* A real cost you had not recorded. Enter it in the cash book first.
- *Why (BM)* **Kos sebenar yang anda belum rekod. Masukkan dalam buku tunai dahulu.**
- → 

**`br-002`** · classify · difficulty 2 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* A cheque you issued for RM800 has not yet been presented at the bank. What do you do?
- *Question (BM)* **Cek RM800 yang anda keluarkan belum dikemukakan di bank. Apa yang anda buat?**
- *Choices* Update the cash book → **Kemas kini buku tunai** · Show in the reconciliation statement → **Tunjuk dalam penyata penyesuaian**
- *Why (EN)* You recorded it correctly when you wrote the cheque — the bank has simply not caught up. A timing difference.
- *Why (BM)* **Anda sudah rekod dengan betul semasa menulis cek — bank cuma belum menyusul. Perbezaan masa.**
- → 

**`br-003`** · classify · difficulty 2 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* A deposit of RM1,200 paid in on 31 December is not yet on the bank statement. What do you do?
- *Question (BM)* **Deposit RM1,200 dimasukkan pada 31 Disember belum ada dalam penyata bank. Apa yang anda buat?**
- *Choices* Update the cash book → **Kemas kini buku tunai** · Show in the reconciliation statement → **Tunjuk dalam penyata penyesuaian**
- *Why (EN)* Another timing difference — the money is yours, the bank has just not credited it yet.
- *Why (BM)* **Perbezaan masa juga — wang itu milik anda, cuma bank belum mengkreditkannya.**
- → 

**`br-004`** · classify · difficulty 3 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* The bank paid a standing order of RM250 that you have not entered. What do you do?
- *Question (BM)* **Bank membayar arahan tetap RM250 yang anda belum catat. Apa yang anda buat?**
- *Choices* Update the cash book → **Kemas kini buku tunai** · Show in the reconciliation statement → **Tunjuk dalam penyata penyesuaian**
- *Why (EN)* The bank really paid it out on your behalf. Record it in the cash book.
- *Why (BM)* **Bank benar-benar membayarnya bagi pihak anda. Rekod dalam buku tunai.**
- → 

**`br-005`** · classify · difficulty 3 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* A customer paid RM600 straight into your bank account. You only found out from the statement. What do you do?
- *Question (BM)* **Pelanggan membayar RM600 terus ke akaun bank anda. Anda hanya tahu daripada penyata. Apa yang anda buat?**
- *Choices* Update the cash book → **Kemas kini buku tunai** · Show in the reconciliation statement → **Tunjuk dalam penyata penyesuaian**
- *Why (EN)* A direct credit is money genuinely received that you had not recorded.
- *Why (BM)* **Kredit terus ialah wang yang benar-benar diterima tetapi belum anda rekod.**
- → 

**`br-006`** · classify · difficulty 4 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* A customer's cheque for RM450 was dishonoured. What do you do?
- *Question (BM)* **Cek pelanggan RM450 telah tak layan. Apa yang anda buat?**
- *Choices* Update the cash book → **Kemas kini buku tunai** · Show in the reconciliation statement → **Tunjuk dalam penyata penyesuaian**
- *Why (EN)* The money never actually arrived, so the receipt you recorded must be cancelled in the cash book.
- *Why (BM)* **Wang itu sebenarnya tidak pernah masuk, jadi terimaan yang direkod mesti dibatalkan dalam buku tunai.**
- → 

**`br-101`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · bank-reconciliation, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the cash book, bank charges are entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam buku tunai, caj bank dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Money going out of the bank reduces the asset, so it is credited in the cash book.
- *Why (BM)* **Wang keluar dari bank mengurangkan aset, jadi ia dikreditkan dalam buku tunai.**
- → 

**`br-102`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · bank-reconciliation, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the cash book, a direct credit from a customer is entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam buku tunai, kredit terus daripada pelanggan dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Money coming in increases the bank asset, so it is debited.
- *Why (BM)* **Wang masuk menambah aset bank, jadi ia didebitkan.**
- → 

**`br-103`** · debit_credit · difficulty 4 · bank-reconciliation, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the cash book, a dishonoured cheque is entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam buku tunai, cek tak layan dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* It cancels a receipt you had already debited, so it goes on the credit side.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia membatalkan terimaan yang telah didebitkan, jadi ia di sebelah kredit.**
- → 

**`br-201`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* The cash book shows RM3,200. Not yet recorded: bank charges RM40, a standing order of RM250, and a direct credit of RM600. What is the updated cash book balance?
- *Question (BM)* **Buku tunai menunjukkan RM3,200. Belum direkod: caj bank RM40, arahan tetap RM250, dan kredit terus RM600. Berapakah baki buku tunai yang dikemas kini?**
- *Why (EN)* 3,200 − 40 − 250 + 600 = 3,510. Only the items the bank knew about go through the cash book.
- *Why (BM)* **3,200 − 40 − 250 + 600 = 3,510. Hanya perkara yang bank tahu melalui buku tunai.**
- → 

**`br-202`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* The updated cash book balance is RM3,510. Unpresented cheques total RM800 and uncredited deposits RM1,200. What is the balance per the bank statement?
- *Question (BM)* **Baki buku tunai dikemas kini ialah RM3,510. Cek belum dikemukakan berjumlah RM800 dan deposit belum dikredit RM1,200. Berapakah baki mengikut penyata bank?**
- *Why (EN)* 3,510 + 800 − 1,200 = 3,110. The bank has not paid the cheques yet, so it still shows more; it has not credited the deposit yet, so it shows less.
- *Why (BM)* **3,510 + 800 − 1,200 = 3,110. Bank belum membayar cek itu, jadi ia masih menunjukkan lebih; ia belum mengkreditkan deposit, jadi menunjukkan kurang.**
- → 

**`br-203`** · numeric · difficulty 5 · bank-reconciliation

- *Question (EN)* The bank statement shows RM6,540. Unpresented cheques are RM1,500 and uncredited deposits RM900. What is the updated cash book balance?
- *Question (BM)* **Penyata bank menunjukkan RM6,540. Cek belum dikemukakan RM1,500 dan deposit belum dikredit RM900. Berapakah baki buku tunai yang dikemas kini?**
- *Why (EN)* Working the other way: 6,540 − 1,500 + 900 = 5,940. Starting from the bank statement, every sign flips.
- *Why (BM)* **Mengira arah bertentangan: 6,540 − 1,500 + 900 = 5,940. Bermula dari penyata bank, setiap tanda berbalik.**
- → 

#### Fading the Reconciliation

`l18-faded-bank-rec` · BM title: `Melunturkan Penyesuaian`

> **Worked example (EN)** The whole procedure runs in one fixed order: start from the cash book, put through the items only the bank knew about to get the UPDATED cash book balance, then add unpresented cheques and take off uncredited deposits to arrive at the bank statement balance. The first time only the last line is blank; after that more of it disappears.
> **(BM)** Keseluruhan prosedur berjalan dalam satu susunan tetap: mula dari buku tunai, masukkan perkara yang hanya bank tahu untuk mendapat baki buku tunai DIKEMAS KINI, kemudian tambah cek belum dikemukakan dan tolak deposit belum dikredit untuk sampai ke baki penyata bank. Kali pertama hanya baris terakhir kosong; selepas itu lebih banyak hilang.
- → 

**`fd-401`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · bank-reconciliation, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Cash book RM3,200. Bank charges RM40 and a standing order RM250 were not recorded, and a direct credit of RM600 was received. Unpresented cheques RM800; uncredited deposits RM1,200.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Buku tunai RM3,200. Caj bank RM40 dan arahan tetap RM250 belum direkod, dan kredit terus RM600 diterima. Cek belum dikemukakan RM800; deposit belum dikredit RM1,200.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Balance per cash book → **Baki mengikut buku tunai**
  - Less: bank charges → **Tolak: caj bank**
  - Less: standing order → **Tolak: arahan tetap**
  - Add: direct credit → **Tambah: kredit terus**
  - Updated cash book balance → **Baki buku tunai dikemas kini**
  - Add: unpresented cheques → **Tambah: cek belum dikemukakan**
  - Less: uncredited deposits → **Tolak: deposit belum dikredit**
  - ␣ Balance per bank statement → **Baki mengikut penyata bank**
- *Why (EN)* 3,510 + 800 − 1,200 = 3,110.
- *Why (BM)* **3,510 + 800 − 1,200 = 3,110.**
- → 

**`fd-402`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · bank-reconciliation, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Cash book RM5,400. Bank charges RM60 and a standing order RM400 were not recorded, and a direct credit of RM1,000 was received. Unpresented cheques RM1,500; uncredited deposits RM900.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Buku tunai RM5,400. Caj bank RM60 dan arahan tetap RM400 belum direkod, dan kredit terus RM1,000 diterima. Cek belum dikemukakan RM1,500; deposit belum dikredit RM900.**
- *Question (EN)* The cash book update is done. Do the reconciliation half yourself.
- *Question (BM)* **Kemas kini buku tunai sudah siap. Buat bahagian penyesuaian sendiri.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Balance per cash book → **Baki mengikut buku tunai**
  - Less: bank charges → **Tolak: caj bank**
  - Less: standing order → **Tolak: arahan tetap**
  - Add: direct credit → **Tambah: kredit terus**
  - Updated cash book balance → **Baki buku tunai dikemas kini**
  - ␣ Add: unpresented cheques → **Tambah: cek belum dikemukakan**
  - ␣ Less: uncredited deposits → **Tolak: deposit belum dikredit**
  - ␣ Balance per bank statement → **Baki mengikut penyata bank**
- *Why (EN)* 5,940 + 1,500 − 900 = 6,540. The unpresented cheques are ADDED — the bank has not paid them out yet, so it still holds more than your book says.
- *Why (BM)* **5,940 + 1,500 − 900 = 6,540. Cek belum dikemukakan DITAMBAH — bank belum membayarnya, jadi ia masih memegang lebih daripada catatan anda.**
- → 

**`fd-403`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · bank-reconciliation, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Cash book RM2,800. Bank charges RM35 and a standing order RM165 were not recorded, and a direct credit of RM500 was received. Unpresented cheques RM700; uncredited deposits RM1,300.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Buku tunai RM2,800. Caj bank RM35 dan arahan tetap RM165 belum direkod, dan kredit terus RM500 diterima. Cek belum dikemukakan RM700; deposit belum dikredit RM1,300.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the opening balance is given. Work through the whole reconciliation.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya baki awal diberi. Kerjakan keseluruhan penyesuaian.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Balance per cash book → **Baki mengikut buku tunai**
  - ␣ Less: bank charges → **Tolak: caj bank**
  - ␣ Less: standing order → **Tolak: arahan tetap**
  - ␣ Add: direct credit → **Tambah: kredit terus**
  - ␣ Updated cash book balance → **Baki buku tunai dikemas kini**
  - ␣ Add: unpresented cheques → **Tambah: cek belum dikemukakan**
  - ␣ Less: uncredited deposits → **Tolak: deposit belum dikredit**
  - ␣ Balance per bank statement → **Baki mengikut penyata bank**
- *Why (EN)* 2,800 − 35 − 165 + 500 = 3,100 updated cash book. Then 3,100 + 700 − 1,300 = 2,500 per the bank statement.
- *Why (BM)* **2,800 − 35 − 165 + 500 = 3,100 buku tunai dikemas kini. Kemudian 3,100 + 700 − 1,300 = 2,500 mengikut penyata bank.**
- → 

### Control Accounts

`t13-control-accounts` · BM title: `Akaun Kawalan`
- → 

#### Which Side?

`l19-control-sides` · BM title: `Sebelah Mana?`

> **Worked example (EN)** A control account squeezes a whole ledger into one T-account, so its total can be checked against the individual accounts. The Sales Ledger Control Account behaves like one giant debtor: it is DEBITED with opening balance, credit sales and dishonoured cheques, and CREDITED with anything that reduces what customers owe — money received, discount allowed, returns inwards, bad debts, and contra set-offs. The Purchases Ledger Control Account is the mirror image: credited with credit purchases, debited with payments, discount received and returns outwards.
> **(BM)** Akaun kawalan memampatkan keseluruhan lejar ke dalam satu akaun T, supaya jumlahnya boleh disemak dengan akaun individu. Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan berkelakuan seperti satu Akaun Belum Terima gergasi: ia DIDEBIT dengan baki awal, jualan kredit dan cek tak layan, dan DIKREDIT dengan apa sahaja yang mengurangkan hutang pelanggan — wang diterima, diskaun diberi, pulangan masuk, hutang lapuk, dan kontra. Akaun Kawalan Lejar Belian ialah cerminannya: dikredit dengan belian kredit, didebit dengan bayaran, diskaun diterima dan pulangan keluar.
- → 

**`ca-001`** · debit_credit · difficulty 2 · control-accounts, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Sales Ledger Control Account, credit sales are entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan, jualan kredit dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* A credit sale increases what customers owe you, and debtors are an asset.
- *Why (BM)* **Jualan kredit menambah hutang pelanggan kepada anda, dan Akaun Belum Terima ialah aset.**
- → 

**`ca-002`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · control-accounts, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Sales Ledger Control Account, discount allowed is entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan, diskaun diberi dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Giving a discount reduces what the customer has to pay, so debtors fall.
- *Why (BM)* **Memberi diskaun mengurangkan bayaran pelanggan, jadi Akaun Belum Terima berkurang.**
- → 

**`ca-003`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · control-accounts, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Sales Ledger Control Account, returns inwards are entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan, pulangan masuk dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Goods coming back cancel part of the sale, so the debtor owes less.
- *Why (BM)* **Barang yang dipulangkan membatalkan sebahagian jualan, jadi Akaun Belum Terima berhutang kurang.**
- → 

**`ca-004`** · debit_credit · difficulty 4 · control-accounts, debit-credit, bad-debts

- *Question (EN)* In the Sales Ledger Control Account, bad debts written off are entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan, hutang lapuk dihapus kira dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Writing the debt off removes it from debtors — the same credit side as a receipt.
- *Why (BM)* **Menghapus kira hutang mengeluarkannya daripada Akaun Belum Terima — sebelah kredit yang sama seperti terimaan.**
- → 

**`ca-005`** · debit_credit · difficulty 4 · control-accounts, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Sales Ledger Control Account, a dishonoured cheque is entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan, cek tak layan dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The payment failed, so the customer owes it again — debtors go back up.
- *Why (BM)* **Bayaran gagal, jadi pelanggan berhutang semula — Akaun Belum Terima naik kembali.**
- → 

**`ca-006`** · debit_credit · difficulty 3 · control-accounts, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Purchases Ledger Control Account, credit purchases are entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Kawalan Lejar Belian, belian kredit dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Buying on credit increases what you owe, and creditors are a liability.
- *Why (BM)* **Membeli secara kredit menambah hutang anda, dan Akaun Belum Bayar ialah liabiliti.**
- → 

**`ca-007`** · debit_credit · difficulty 4 · control-accounts, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Purchases Ledger Control Account, discount received is entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Kawalan Lejar Belian, diskaun diterima dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The discount reduces what you must pay the supplier, so creditors fall — the mirror of discount allowed.
- *Why (BM)* **Diskaun mengurangkan bayaran kepada pembekal, jadi Akaun Belum Bayar berkurang — cerminan diskaun diberi.**
- → 

**`ca-101`** · classify · difficulty 3 · control-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Returns outwards appear in which control account?
- *Question (BM)* **Pulangan keluar muncul dalam akaun kawalan yang mana?**
- *Choices* Sales Ledger Control Account → **Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan** · Purchases Ledger Control Account → **Akaun Kawalan Lejar Belian**
- *Why (EN)* Returns OUTWARDS go back to your supplier, so they belong with creditors.
- *Why (BM)* **Pulangan KELUAR dikembalikan kepada pembekal anda, jadi ia bersama Akaun Belum Bayar.**
- → 

**`ca-102`** · classify · difficulty 3 · control-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Discount allowed appears in which control account?
- *Question (BM)* **Diskaun diberi muncul dalam akaun kawalan yang mana?**
- *Choices* Sales Ledger Control Account → **Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan** · Purchases Ledger Control Account → **Akaun Kawalan Lejar Belian**
- *Why (EN)* You ALLOW a discount to a customer, so it sits with debtors.
- *Why (BM)* **Anda MEMBERI diskaun kepada pelanggan, jadi ia bersama Akaun Belum Terima.**
- → 

**`ca-201`** · t_account · difficulty 4 · control-accounts, ledger

- *Question (EN)* Post these to the Sales Ledger Control Account, then find the closing balance.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan, kemudian cari baki penutup.**
- *Why (EN)* Debits 12,000 + 45,000 = 57,000. Credits 38,000 + 1,200 + 800 + 500 = 40,500. Closing debtors 16,500 on the debit side.
- *Why (BM)* **Debit 12,000 + 45,000 = 57,000. Kredit 38,000 + 1,200 + 800 + 500 = 40,500. Akaun Belum Terima penutup 16,500 di sebelah debit.**
- → 

**`ca-202`** · t_account · difficulty 5 · control-accounts, ledger

- *Question (EN)* Post these to the Purchases Ledger Control Account, then find the closing balance.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke Akaun Kawalan Lejar Belian, kemudian cari baki penutup.**
- *Why (EN)* Credits 8,000 + 26,000 = 34,000. Debits 22,000 + 900 + 600 = 23,500. Closing creditors 10,500 on the credit side.
- *Why (BM)* **Kredit 8,000 + 26,000 = 34,000. Debit 22,000 + 900 + 600 = 23,500. Akaun Belum Bayar penutup 10,500 di sebelah kredit.**
- → 

**`ca-301`** · numeric · difficulty 5 · control-accounts

- *Question (EN)* A customer owing RM700 is also a supplier owed RM700, and the two balances are set off (contra). By how much does the Sales Ledger Control Account balance fall?
- *Question (BM)* **Pelanggan yang berhutang RM700 juga pembekal yang dihutang RM700, dan kedua-dua baki dikontra. Berapakah pengurangan baki Akaun Kawalan Lejar Jualan?**
- *Why (EN)* A contra credits the sales ledger control and debits the purchases ledger control by the same RM700 — no money moves, both balances simply shrink.
- *Why (BM)* **Kontra mengkredit kawalan lejar jualan dan mendebit kawalan lejar belian dengan RM700 yang sama — tiada wang bergerak, kedua-dua baki hanya mengecil.**
- → 

#### Fading the Control Account

`l20-faded-control` · BM title: `Melunturkan Akaun Kawalan`

> **Worked example (EN)** Reading the control account as a running list is faster than drawing the T: start with the opening balance, add what increases the debt, take off everything that reduces it, and what is left is the closing balance. Each rung below takes one more line away.
> **(BM)** Membaca akaun kawalan sebagai senarai berjalan lebih pantas daripada melukis akaun T: mula dengan baki awal, tambah apa yang menambah hutang, tolak semua yang menguranginya, dan yang tinggal ialah baki penutup. Setiap anak tangga di bawah menanggalkan satu baris lagi.
- → 

**`fd-501`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · control-accounts, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Opening debtors RM12,000. Credit sales RM45,000. Cash received RM38,000. Discount allowed RM1,200. Returns inwards RM800. Bad debts written off RM500.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Akaun Belum Terima awal RM12,000. Jualan kredit RM45,000. Tunai diterima RM38,000. Diskaun diberi RM1,200. Pulangan masuk RM800. Hutang lapuk dihapus kira RM500.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Opening balance → **Baki awal**
  - Add: credit sales → **Tambah: jualan kredit**
  - Less: cash received → **Tolak: tunai diterima**
  - Less: discount allowed → **Tolak: diskaun diberi**
  - Less: returns inwards → **Tolak: pulangan masuk**
  - Less: bad debts written off → **Tolak: hutang lapuk dihapus kira**
  - ␣ Closing balance (debtors) → **Baki penutup (Akaun Belum Terima)**
- *Why (EN)* 57,000 − 40,500 = 16,500.
- *Why (BM)* **57,000 − 40,500 = 16,500.**
- → 

**`fd-502`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · control-accounts, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Opening debtors RM9,000. Credit sales RM52,000. Cash received RM44,000. Discount allowed RM1,500. Returns inwards RM1,000. Bad debts written off RM800.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Akaun Belum Terima awal RM9,000. Jualan kredit RM52,000. Tunai diterima RM44,000. Diskaun diberi RM1,500. Pulangan masuk RM1,000. Hutang lapuk dihapus kira RM800.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Complete the control account.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Lengkapkan akaun kawalan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Opening balance → **Baki awal**
  - Add: credit sales → **Tambah: jualan kredit**
  - Less: cash received → **Tolak: tunai diterima**
  - Less: discount allowed → **Tolak: diskaun diberi**
  - ␣ Less: returns inwards → **Tolak: pulangan masuk**
  - ␣ Less: bad debts written off → **Tolak: hutang lapuk dihapus kira**
  - ␣ Closing balance (debtors) → **Baki penutup (Akaun Belum Terima)**
- *Why (EN)* 9,000 + 52,000 = 61,000, less 44,000 + 1,500 + 1,000 + 800 = 47,300, leaving 13,700. Every one of those four reduces what customers owe.
- *Why (BM)* **9,000 + 52,000 = 61,000, tolak 44,000 + 1,500 + 1,000 + 800 = 47,300, tinggal 13,700. Keempat-empatnya mengurangkan hutang pelanggan.**
- → 

**`fd-503`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · control-accounts, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Opening creditors RM8,000. Credit purchases RM26,000. Payments to suppliers RM22,000. Discount received RM900. Returns outwards RM600.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Akaun Belum Bayar awal RM8,000. Belian kredit RM26,000. Bayaran kepada pembekal RM22,000. Diskaun diterima RM900. Pulangan keluar RM600.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the opening balance is given. Build the whole control account.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya baki awal diberi. Bina keseluruhan akaun kawalan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Opening balance → **Baki awal**
  - ␣ Add: credit purchases → **Tambah: belian kredit**
  - ␣ Less: payments to suppliers → **Tolak: bayaran kepada pembekal**
  - ␣ Less: discount received → **Tolak: diskaun diterima**
  - ␣ Less: returns outwards → **Tolak: pulangan keluar**
  - ␣ Closing balance (creditors) → **Baki penutup (Akaun Belum Bayar)**
- *Why (EN)* 8,000 + 26,000 − 22,000 − 900 − 600 = 10,500. The creditors account runs the same shape as the debtors one — only the meaning of each line flips.
- *Why (BM)* **8,000 + 26,000 − 22,000 − 900 − 600 = 10,500. Akaun Belum Bayar berjalan dengan bentuk yang sama seperti Akaun Belum Terima — hanya maksud setiap baris yang berbalik.**
- → 

### Correction of Errors & Suspense

`t14-suspense` · BM title: `Pembetulan Kesilapan & Akaun Penyelesaian`
- → 

#### Six Errors the Trial Balance Cannot See

`l21-error-types` · BM title: `Enam Kesilapan Yang Imbangan Duga Tidak Nampak`

> **Worked example (EN)** A trial balance that agrees is not proof the books are right. Six errors keep both sides equal and slip straight through: OMISSION (left out entirely), COMMISSION (right kind of account, wrong person), PRINCIPLE (wrong class of account — an asset treated as an expense), ORIGINAL ENTRY (the same wrong figure used on both sides), COMPLETE REVERSAL (debit and credit swapped), and COMPENSATING (two errors of the same size cancelling out). Only a one-sided error — a missing posting, or a column added up wrongly — makes the trial balance disagree, and that is what a suspense account is for.
> **(BM)** Imbangan duga yang seimbang bukan bukti buku itu betul. Enam kesilapan mengekalkan kedua-dua belah sama dan lolos terus: PENINGGALAN (tertinggal sepenuhnya), KOMISEN (jenis akaun betul, orang salah), PRINSIP (kelas akaun salah — aset dianggap belanja), CATATAN ASAL (angka salah yang sama digunakan di kedua-dua belah), TERBALIK SEPENUHNYA (debit dan kredit bertukar), dan SALING MENGIMBANGI (dua kesilapan sama besar yang membatalkan). Hanya kesilapan sebelah — catatan yang tertinggal, atau lajur yang salah campur — membuat imbangan duga tidak seimbang, dan itulah gunanya akaun penyelesaian.
- → 

**`ce-001`** · classify · difficulty 3 · error-types

- *Question (EN)* A credit sale to Ali was never entered in the books at all. Which error is this?
- *Question (BM)* **Jualan kredit kepada Ali langsung tidak dimasukkan ke dalam buku. Kesilapan apakah ini?**
- *Choices* Error of omission → **Kesilapan peninggalan** · Error of commission → **Kesilapan komisen** · Error of principle → **Kesilapan prinsip** · Error of original entry → **Kesilapan catatan asal** · Error of complete reversal → **Kesilapan terbalik sepenuhnya** · Compensating error → **Kesilapan saling mengimbangi**
- *Why (EN)* Nothing was recorded on either side, so the trial balance still agrees.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada apa direkod di kedua-dua belah, jadi imbangan duga masih seimbang.**
- → 

**`ce-002`** · classify · difficulty 4 · error-types

- *Question (EN)* A credit sale to Ali was posted to Abu's account instead. Which error is this?
- *Question (BM)* **Jualan kredit kepada Ali dicatat ke akaun Abu sebaliknya. Kesilapan apakah ini?**
- *Choices* Error of omission → **Kesilapan peninggalan** · Error of commission → **Kesilapan komisen** · Error of principle → **Kesilapan prinsip** · Error of original entry → **Kesilapan catatan asal** · Error of complete reversal → **Kesilapan terbalik sepenuhnya** · Compensating error → **Kesilapan saling mengimbangi**
- *Why (EN)* The right TYPE of account was used — a debtor — just the wrong person. That is commission, not principle.
- *Why (BM)* **JENIS akaun yang betul digunakan — Akaun Belum Terima — cuma orang yang salah. Itu komisen, bukan prinsip.**
- → 

**`ce-003`** · classify · difficulty 4 · error-types

- *Question (EN)* The purchase of a van was debited to the Purchases account. Which error is this?
- *Question (BM)* **Pembelian van didebitkan ke akaun Belian. Kesilapan apakah ini?**
- *Choices* Error of omission → **Kesilapan peninggalan** · Error of commission → **Kesilapan komisen** · Error of principle → **Kesilapan prinsip** · Error of original entry → **Kesilapan catatan asal** · Error of complete reversal → **Kesilapan terbalik sepenuhnya** · Compensating error → **Kesilapan saling mengimbangi**
- *Why (EN)* A non-current asset was treated as an expense — a whole different CLASS of account. That is principle.
- *Why (BM)* **Aset bukan semasa dianggap belanja — KELAS akaun yang berbeza sama sekali. Itu prinsip.**
- → 

**`ce-004`** · classify · difficulty 4 · error-types

- *Question (EN)* A sale of RM540 was entered as RM450 in both the sales account and the customer's account. Which error is this?
- *Question (BM)* **Jualan RM540 dimasukkan sebagai RM450 dalam kedua-dua akaun jualan dan akaun pelanggan. Kesilapan apakah ini?**
- *Choices* Error of omission → **Kesilapan peninggalan** · Error of commission → **Kesilapan komisen** · Error of principle → **Kesilapan prinsip** · Error of original entry → **Kesilapan catatan asal** · Error of complete reversal → **Kesilapan terbalik sepenuhnya** · Compensating error → **Kesilapan saling mengimbangi**
- *Why (EN)* The wrong figure was taken from the source document, then used consistently on both sides.
- *Why (BM)* **Angka salah diambil daripada dokumen sumber, kemudian digunakan secara konsisten di kedua-dua belah.**
- → 

**`ce-005`** · classify · difficulty 5 · error-types

- *Question (EN)* Cash paid to a supplier was debited to Cash and credited to the supplier's account. Which error is this?
- *Question (BM)* **Tunai dibayar kepada pembekal didebitkan ke Tunai dan dikreditkan ke akaun pembekal. Kesilapan apakah ini?**
- *Choices* Error of omission → **Kesilapan peninggalan** · Error of commission → **Kesilapan komisen** · Error of principle → **Kesilapan prinsip** · Error of original entry → **Kesilapan catatan asal** · Error of complete reversal → **Kesilapan terbalik sepenuhnya** · Compensating error → **Kesilapan saling mengimbangi**
- *Why (EN)* Both entries are on exactly the wrong sides. Correcting it needs DOUBLE the amount — once to cancel, once to record it properly.
- *Why (BM)* **Kedua-dua catatan berada di sebelah yang salah. Membetulkannya perlu DUA KALI ganda amaun — sekali untuk batal, sekali untuk rekod dengan betul.**
- → 

**`ce-006`** · classify · difficulty 5 · error-types

- *Question (EN)* The sales account was overcast by RM100 and the purchases account was also overcast by RM100. Which error is this?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun jualan tercampur lebih RM100 dan akaun belian juga tercampur lebih RM100. Kesilapan apakah ini?**
- *Choices* Error of omission → **Kesilapan peninggalan** · Error of commission → **Kesilapan komisen** · Error of principle → **Kesilapan prinsip** · Error of original entry → **Kesilapan catatan asal** · Error of complete reversal → **Kesilapan terbalik sepenuhnya** · Compensating error → **Kesilapan saling mengimbangi**
- *Why (EN)* One error on each side, the same size, cancelling each other out. The totals agree even though both accounts are wrong.
- *Why (BM)* **Satu kesilapan di setiap belah, sama besar, saling membatalkan. Jumlah seimbang walaupun kedua-dua akaun salah.**
- → 

**`ce-101`** · classify · difficulty 3 · error-types, trial-balance

- *Question (EN)* A credit sale was omitted from the books entirely. Will the trial balance still agree?
- *Question (BM)* **Jualan kredit tertinggal sepenuhnya daripada buku. Adakah imbangan duga masih seimbang?**
- *Choices* Yes — the trial balance still agrees → **Ya — imbangan duga masih seimbang** · No — the trial balance will not agree → **Tidak — imbangan duga tidak akan seimbang**
- *Why (EN)* Nothing was entered on either side, so both totals are equally understated.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada apa dimasukkan di kedua-dua belah, jadi kedua-dua jumlah kurang sama banyak.**
- → 

**`ce-102`** · classify · difficulty 4 · error-types, trial-balance

- *Question (EN)* The sales account was undercast by RM400. Will the trial balance still agree?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun jualan tercampur kurang RM400. Adakah imbangan duga masih seimbang?**
- *Choices* Yes — the trial balance still agrees → **Ya — imbangan duga masih seimbang** · No — the trial balance will not agree → **Tidak — imbangan duga tidak akan seimbang**
- *Why (EN)* Only the credit side is wrong, so the two totals differ by RM400 — a suspense account is needed.
- *Why (BM)* **Hanya sebelah kredit yang salah, jadi kedua-dua jumlah berbeza RM400 — akaun penyelesaian diperlukan.**
- → 

#### Clearing the Suspense Account

`l22-suspense-corrections` · BM title: `Menyelesaikan Akaun Penyelesaian`

> **Worked example (EN)** When the trial balance will not agree, the difference is parked in a suspense account so the books can carry on. Each one-sided error found is then corrected with a journal entry: the real account gets the entry it should have had, and the suspense account takes the other side. When every error has been found, the suspense account is empty.
> **(BM)** Apabila imbangan duga tidak seimbang, perbezaannya diletakkan dalam akaun penyelesaian supaya buku boleh diteruskan. Setiap kesilapan sebelah yang dijumpai kemudian dibetulkan dengan catatan jurnal: akaun sebenar mendapat catatan yang sepatutnya, dan akaun penyelesaian mengambil sebelah lagi. Apabila semua kesilapan dijumpai, akaun penyelesaian menjadi kosong.
- → 

**`ce-201`** · debit_credit · difficulty 4 · suspense, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* The trial balance debit total exceeds the credit total by RM500. The suspense account opens with a balance on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Jumlah debit imbangan duga melebihi jumlah kredit sebanyak RM500. Akaun penyelesaian dibuka dengan baki di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The credit side is short, so RM500 of credit is added to make the totals agree.
- *Why (BM)* **Sebelah kredit kurang, jadi RM500 kredit ditambah untuk menyeimbangkan jumlah.**
- → 

**`ce-202`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · suspense, trial-balance

- *Question (EN)* A trial balance shows a debit total of RM45,600 and a credit total of RM45,100. What amount goes into the suspense account?
- *Question (BM)* **Imbangan duga menunjukkan jumlah debit RM45,600 dan jumlah kredit RM45,100. Berapakah amaun yang masuk ke akaun penyelesaian?**
- *Why (EN)* 45,600 − 45,100 = 500. The suspense account holds the difference until the errors are found.
- *Why (BM)* **45,600 − 45,100 = 500. Akaun penyelesaian memegang perbezaan sehingga kesilapan dijumpai.**
- → 

**`ce-203`** · journal_entry · difficulty 4 · suspense

- *Question (EN)* Wages of RM250 were correctly credited to Bank but never debited to the Wages account. Record the correcting entry.
- *Question (BM)* **Gaji RM250 dikreditkan dengan betul ke Bank tetapi tidak pernah didebitkan ke akaun Gaji. Rekod catatan pembetulan.**
- *Accounts offered* Wages → **Gaji** · Suspense → **Penyelesaian** · Bank → **Bank** · Rent → **Sewa**
- *Why (EN)* The Bank side was already right, so it is not touched. Wages gets the debit it was missing, and suspense supplies the other side.
- *Why (BM)* **Sebelah Bank sudah betul, jadi ia tidak disentuh. Gaji mendapat debit yang tertinggal, dan penyelesaian membekalkan sebelah lagi.**
- → 

**`ce-204`** · journal_entry · difficulty 5 · suspense

- *Question (EN)* The sales account was undercast by RM400. Record the correcting entry.
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun jualan tercampur kurang RM400. Rekod catatan pembetulan.**
- *Accounts offered* Suspense → **Penyelesaian** · Sales → **Jualan** · Purchases → **Belian** · Bank → **Bank**
- *Why (EN)* Sales was understated, so it needs RM400 more credit. Suspense takes the matching debit.
- *Why (BM)* **Jualan kurang dinyatakan, jadi ia perlu RM400 kredit lagi. Penyelesaian mengambil debit yang sepadan.**
- → 

**`err-301`** · spot_error · difficulty 5 · error-types

- *Question (EN)* A van bought for RM12,000 by cheque was recorded as: Dr Purchases 12,000 / Cr Bank 12,000. Fix it.
- *Question (BM)* **Van dibeli RM12,000 dengan cek direkod sebagai: Dt Belian 12,000 / Kt Bank 12,000. Betulkan.**
- *Accounts offered* Purchases → **Belian** · Motor Vehicles → **Kenderaan Bermotor** · Bank → **Bank** · Suspense → **Penyelesaian**
- *Why (EN)* An error of principle: the van is a non-current asset, not goods for resale. The Bank side was always right, and the trial balance never noticed.
- *Why (BM)* **Kesilapan prinsip: van ialah aset bukan semasa, bukan barang untuk dijual semula. Sebelah Bank sentiasa betul, dan imbangan duga tidak pernah perasan.**
- → 

#### Fading the Suspense Account

`l23-faded-suspense` · BM title: `Melunturkan Akaun Penyelesaian`

> **Worked example (EN)** Clearing a suspense account is a countdown: start with the difference it was opened with, take off each error as you correct it, and the account must finish at zero. If it does not, an error is still out there.
> **(BM)** Menyelesaikan akaun penyelesaian ialah kiraan menurun: mula dengan perbezaan yang membukanya, tolak setiap kesilapan sambil anda membetulkannya, dan akaun itu mesti berakhir pada sifar. Jika tidak, masih ada kesilapan di luar sana.
- → 

**`fd-601`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · suspense, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* The trial balance debit total exceeded the credit total by RM500, so a suspense account was opened. Two errors were then found: sales undercast by RM400, and discount received of RM100 never posted.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Jumlah debit imbangan duga melebihi kredit sebanyak RM500, jadi akaun penyelesaian dibuka. Dua kesilapan kemudian dijumpai: jualan tercampur kurang RM400, dan diskaun diterima RM100 tidak pernah dicatat.**
- *Question (EN)* The corrections are listed. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Pembetulan sudah disenaraikan. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Suspense opening difference → **Perbezaan awal penyelesaian**
  - Less: sales undercast, now credited to Sales → **Tolak: jualan tercampur kurang, kini dikredit ke Jualan**
  - Less: discount received now posted → **Tolak: diskaun diterima kini dicatat**
  - ␣ Suspense balance remaining → **Baki penyelesaian yang tinggal**
- *Why (EN)* 500 − 400 − 100 = 0. Both missing entries were credits, which is exactly why the credit side was short.
- *Why (BM)* **500 − 400 − 100 = 0. Kedua-dua catatan yang tertinggal ialah kredit, itulah sebabnya sebelah kredit kurang.**
- → 

**`fd-602`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · suspense, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* The trial balance credit total exceeded the debit total by RM300, so a suspense account was opened. Purchases were undercast by RM200, and rent paid of RM100 was never posted to the Rent account.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Jumlah kredit imbangan duga melebihi debit sebanyak RM300, jadi akaun penyelesaian dibuka. Belian tercampur kurang RM200, dan sewa dibayar RM100 tidak pernah dicatat ke akaun Sewa.**
- *Question (EN)* Two lines are missing. Clear the suspense account.
- *Question (BM)* **Dua baris hilang. Selesaikan akaun penyelesaian.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Suspense opening difference → **Perbezaan awal penyelesaian**
  - Less: purchases undercast, now debited to Purchases → **Tolak: belian tercampur kurang, kini didebit ke Belian**
  - ␣ Less: rent now debited to Rent → **Tolak: sewa kini didebit ke Sewa**
  - ␣ Suspense balance remaining → **Baki penyelesaian yang tinggal**
- *Why (EN)* 300 − 200 − 100 = 0. This time the missing entries were both debits, so the debit side was the short one.
- *Why (BM)* **300 − 200 − 100 = 0. Kali ini catatan yang tertinggal kedua-duanya debit, jadi sebelah debit yang kurang.**
- → 

**`fd-603`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · suspense, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* The trial balance debit total exceeded the credit total by RM750, so a suspense account was opened. Sales were undercast by RM450, and discount received of RM300 was never posted.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Jumlah debit imbangan duga melebihi kredit sebanyak RM750, jadi akaun penyelesaian dibuka. Jualan tercampur kurang RM450, dan diskaun diterima RM300 tidak pernah dicatat.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the scenario is given. Work the whole suspense account.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya senario diberi. Kerjakan keseluruhan akaun penyelesaian.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - ␣ Suspense opening difference → **Perbezaan awal penyelesaian**
  - ␣ Less: sales undercast, now credited to Sales → **Tolak: jualan tercampur kurang, kini dikredit ke Jualan**
  - ␣ Less: discount received now posted → **Tolak: diskaun diterima kini dicatat**
  - ␣ Suspense balance remaining → **Baki penyelesaian yang tinggal**
- *Why (EN)* 750 − 450 − 300 = 0. A suspense account that will not clear to zero means at least one error is still undiscovered.
- *Why (BM)* **750 − 450 − 300 = 0. Akaun penyelesaian yang tidak menjadi sifar bermakna sekurang-kurangnya satu kesilapan masih belum dijumpai.**
- → 

### Incomplete Records

`t15-incomplete-records` · BM title: `Rekod Tak Lengkap`
- → 

#### Profit Without a Ledger

`l24-capital-comparison` · BM title: `Untung Tanpa Lejar`

> **Worked example (EN)** Many small traders keep no double entry at all, so there is no income statement to read the profit from. It has to be reasoned out instead: capital is whatever the business owns minus what it owes (a Statement of Affairs), and any GROWTH in capital over the year must have come from profit. Two adjustments finish it — drawings are ADDED back, because money the owner took out was still profit earned; capital introduced is DEDUCTED, because that growth came from the owner's pocket, not from trading.
> **(BM)** Ramai peniaga kecil langsung tidak menyimpan catatan bergu, jadi tiada penyata pendapatan untuk membaca untung. Ia perlu ditaakul: modal ialah apa yang perniagaan miliki tolak apa yang dihutang (Penyata Hal Ehwal), dan sebarang PERTAMBAHAN modal sepanjang tahun mesti datang daripada untung. Dua pelarasan melengkapkannya — ambilan DITAMBAH semula, kerana wang yang diambil pemilik tetap untung yang diperoleh; modal dimasukkan DITOLAK, kerana pertambahan itu datang daripada poket pemilik, bukan daripada perniagaan.
- → 

**`ir-001`** · numeric · difficulty 2 · incomplete-records

- *Question (EN)* A business has assets of RM45,000 and liabilities of RM12,000. What is the capital?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah perniagaan mempunyai aset RM45,000 dan liabiliti RM12,000. Berapakah modalnya?**
- *Why (EN)* Capital = assets − liabilities = 45,000 − 12,000 = 33,000. The accounting equation, rearranged.
- *Why (BM)* **Modal = aset − liabiliti = 45,000 − 12,000 = 33,000. Persamaan perakaunan, disusun semula.**
- → 

**`ir-002`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · incomplete-records

- *Question (EN)* Opening capital was RM25,000 and closing capital is RM33,000. There were no drawings and no capital introduced. What is the profit for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Modal awal RM25,000 dan modal akhir RM33,000. Tiada ambilan dan tiada modal dimasukkan. Berapakah untung bagi tahun itu?**
- *Why (EN)* 33,000 − 25,000 = 8,000. With nothing taken out or put in, the whole growth in capital is profit.
- *Why (BM)* **33,000 − 25,000 = 8,000. Tanpa apa-apa dikeluarkan atau dimasukkan, seluruh pertambahan modal ialah untung.**
- → 

**`ir-003`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · incomplete-records

- *Question (EN)* Opening capital RM25,000, closing capital RM33,000, drawings RM6,000. What is the profit for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Modal awal RM25,000, modal akhir RM33,000, ambilan RM6,000. Berapakah untung bagi tahun itu?**
- *Why (EN)* 33,000 − 25,000 + 6,000 = 14,000. The RM6,000 the owner took out was profit too — capital only looks smaller because it left.
- *Why (BM)* **33,000 − 25,000 + 6,000 = 14,000. RM6,000 yang diambil pemilik juga untung — modal hanya kelihatan kecil kerana ia telah keluar.**
- → 

**`ir-004`** · numeric · difficulty 5 · incomplete-records

- *Question (EN)* Opening capital RM25,000, closing capital RM40,000, drawings RM6,000, additional capital introduced RM5,000. What is the profit for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Modal awal RM25,000, modal akhir RM40,000, ambilan RM6,000, modal tambahan dimasukkan RM5,000. Berapakah untung bagi tahun itu?**
- *Why (EN)* 40,000 − 25,000 + 6,000 − 5,000 = 16,000. The RM5,000 came from the owner, not from trading, so it must come back out.
- *Why (BM)* **40,000 − 25,000 + 6,000 − 5,000 = 16,000. RM5,000 itu datang daripada pemilik, bukan daripada perniagaan, jadi ia mesti ditolak semula.**
- → 

**`ir-101`** · classify · difficulty 3 · incomplete-records

- *Question (EN)* In the capital-comparison profit formula, drawings are...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam formula untung perbandingan modal, ambilan adalah...**
- *Choices* Added → **Ditambah** · Deducted → **Ditolak**
- *Why (EN)* Drawings shrank the closing capital, but the money was still profit the business earned. Add it back.
- *Why (BM)* **Ambilan mengecilkan modal akhir, tetapi wang itu tetap untung yang diperoleh perniagaan. Tambah semula.**
- → 

**`ir-102`** · classify · difficulty 4 · incomplete-records

- *Question (EN)* In the capital-comparison profit formula, additional capital introduced is...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam formula untung perbandingan modal, modal tambahan yang dimasukkan adalah...**
- *Choices* Added → **Ditambah** · Deducted → **Ditolak**
- *Why (EN)* It grew the capital without the business earning anything, so it must be stripped out before you can call the rest profit.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia menambah modal tanpa perniagaan memperoleh apa-apa, jadi ia mesti dikeluarkan sebelum yang selebihnya boleh dipanggil untung.**
- → 

**`ir-201`** · statement_build · difficulty 4 · incomplete-records, statement-placement

- *Question (EN)* Sort these balances into a Statement of Affairs, then work out the capital.
- *Question (BM)* **Asingkan baki ini ke dalam Penyata Hal Ehwal, kemudian kira modalnya.**
- *Why (EN)* Assets 45,000 − liabilities 12,000 = capital 33,000. A statement of affairs is just a balance sheet built from whatever records survive.
- *Why (BM)* **Aset 45,000 − liabiliti 12,000 = modal 33,000. Penyata hal ehwal hanyalah kunci kira-kira yang dibina daripada rekod yang masih ada.**
- → 

**`ir-301`** · numeric · difficulty 5 · incomplete-records, control-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Opening debtors RM9,000, closing debtors RM11,000, cash received from customers RM50,000. What were the credit sales?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun Belum Terima awal RM9,000, Akaun Belum Terima akhir RM11,000, tunai diterima daripada pelanggan RM50,000. Berapakah jualan kredit?**
- *Why (EN)* 50,000 + 11,000 − 9,000 = 52,000. Reconstruct the control account: whatever is still owed at the end, plus what was collected, minus what was already owed at the start.
- *Why (BM)* **50,000 + 11,000 − 9,000 = 52,000. Bina semula akaun kawalan: apa yang masih dihutang di akhir, campur apa yang dikutip, tolak apa yang sudah dihutang di awal.**
- → 

**`ir-302`** · numeric · difficulty 5 · incomplete-records, control-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Opening creditors RM6,000, closing creditors RM8,000, payments to suppliers RM31,000. What were the credit purchases?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun Belum Bayar awal RM6,000, Akaun Belum Bayar akhir RM8,000, bayaran kepada pembekal RM31,000. Berapakah belian kredit?**
- *Why (EN)* 31,000 + 8,000 − 6,000 = 33,000 — the same reconstruction, run on the creditors side.
- *Why (BM)* **31,000 + 8,000 − 6,000 = 33,000 — pembinaan semula yang sama, dijalankan di sebelah Akaun Belum Bayar.**
- → 

#### Fading the Profit Calculation

`l25-faded-incomplete` · BM title: `Melunturkan Pengiraan Untung`

> **Worked example (EN)** The calculation always runs the same way: closing capital, take off opening capital to find the increase, add back drawings, then take off any capital introduced. What is left is the profit. Each rung below hides one more line.
> **(BM)** Pengiraan sentiasa berjalan sama: modal akhir, tolak modal awal untuk mencari pertambahan, tambah semula ambilan, kemudian tolak sebarang modal dimasukkan. Yang tinggal ialah untung. Setiap anak tangga di bawah menyembunyikan satu baris lagi.
- → 

**`fd-701`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · incomplete-records, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Opening capital RM25,000, closing capital RM40,000, drawings RM6,000, additional capital introduced RM5,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Modal awal RM25,000, modal akhir RM40,000, ambilan RM6,000, modal tambahan dimasukkan RM5,000.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Closing capital → **Modal akhir**
  - Less: opening capital → **Tolak: modal awal**
  - Increase in capital → **Pertambahan modal**
  - Add: drawings → **Tambah: ambilan**
  - Less: capital introduced → **Tolak: modal dimasukkan**
  - ␣ Profit for the year → **Untung bagi tahun**
- *Why (EN)* 15,000 + 6,000 − 5,000 = 16,000.
- *Why (BM)* **15,000 + 6,000 − 5,000 = 16,000.**
- → 

**`fd-702`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · incomplete-records, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Opening capital RM40,000, closing capital RM58,000, drawings RM9,000, additional capital introduced RM4,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Modal awal RM40,000, modal akhir RM58,000, ambilan RM9,000, modal tambahan dimasukkan RM4,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Work out the profit.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Kira untungnya.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Closing capital → **Modal akhir**
  - Less: opening capital → **Tolak: modal awal**
  - Increase in capital → **Pertambahan modal**
  - ␣ Add: drawings → **Tambah: ambilan**
  - ␣ Less: capital introduced → **Tolak: modal dimasukkan**
  - ␣ Profit for the year → **Untung bagi tahun**
- *Why (EN)* 18,000 + 9,000 − 4,000 = 23,000. Drawings up, capital introduced down — the two adjustments always pull in opposite directions.
- *Why (BM)* **18,000 + 9,000 − 4,000 = 23,000. Ambilan naik, modal dimasukkan turun — kedua-dua pelarasan sentiasa menarik ke arah bertentangan.**
- → 

**`fd-703`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · incomplete-records, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Opening capital RM55,000, closing capital RM72,000, drawings RM12,000, additional capital introduced RM3,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Modal awal RM55,000, modal akhir RM72,000, ambilan RM12,000, modal tambahan dimasukkan RM3,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the closing capital is given. Work out the profit.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya modal akhir diberi. Kira untungnya.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Closing capital → **Modal akhir**
  - ␣ Less: opening capital → **Tolak: modal awal**
  - ␣ Increase in capital → **Pertambahan modal**
  - ␣ Add: drawings → **Tambah: ambilan**
  - ␣ Less: capital introduced → **Tolak: modal dimasukkan**
  - ␣ Profit for the year → **Untung bagi tahun**
- *Why (EN)* 72,000 − 55,000 = 17,000 increase, then 17,000 + 12,000 − 3,000 = 26,000. Note the profit is far bigger than the capital growth — that is normal when the owner draws heavily.
- *Why (BM)* **72,000 − 55,000 = 17,000 pertambahan, kemudian 17,000 + 12,000 − 3,000 = 26,000. Perhatikan untung jauh lebih besar daripada pertambahan modal — itu biasa apabila pemilik banyak mengambil.**
- → 

### Club & Society Accounts

`t16-club-accounts` · BM title: `Akaun Kelab dan Persatuan`
- → 

#### Two Different Accounts

`l26-receipts-vs-income` · BM title: `Dua Akaun Berbeza`

> **Worked example (EN)** A club is not trading for profit, so its statements are renamed — but the accounting is the one you already know. The Receipts and Payments Account is simply a summarised cash book: every ringgit in and out, including equipment bought and subscriptions for other years. The Income and Expenditure Account is the club's income statement: only THIS year's revenue items, with accruals and prepayments applied exactly as in Stage 5. Profit becomes SURPLUS, loss becomes DEFICIT, and capital becomes the ACCUMULATED FUND.
> **(BM)** Kelab tidak berniaga untuk untung, jadi penyatanya dinamakan semula — tetapi perakaunannya sama seperti yang anda sudah tahu. Akaun Terimaan dan Pembayaran hanyalah buku tunai yang diringkaskan: setiap ringgit masuk dan keluar, termasuk peralatan dibeli dan yuran bagi tahun lain. Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan ialah penyata pendapatan kelab: hanya item hasil bagi tahun INI, dengan terakru dan prabayar dikenakan sama seperti Tahap 5. Untung menjadi LEBIHAN, rugi menjadi DEFISIT, dan modal menjadi KUMPULAN WANG TERKUMPUL.
- → 

**`cl-001`** · classify · difficulty 3 · club-accounts

- *Question (EN)* The club buys new sports equipment for RM4,000 cash. Where does the RM4,000 appear?
- *Question (BM)* **Kelab membeli peralatan sukan baharu RM4,000 secara tunai. Di manakah RM4,000 itu muncul?**
- *Choices* Receipts and Payments Account → **Akaun Terimaan dan Pembayaran** · Income and Expenditure Account → **Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan**
- *Why (EN)* Cash left the club, so it belongs in the cash summary. The equipment is an asset, not a cost of the year — only its depreciation reaches the Income and Expenditure Account.
- *Why (BM)* **Tunai keluar dari kelab, jadi ia masuk dalam ringkasan tunai. Peralatan ialah aset, bukan kos tahun ini — hanya susut nilainya sampai ke Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan.**
- → 

**`cl-002`** · classify · difficulty 4 · club-accounts, depreciation

- *Question (EN)* Depreciation of the club's equipment for the year is RM1,200. Where does it appear?
- *Question (BM)* **Susut nilai peralatan kelab bagi tahun ini RM1,200. Di manakah ia muncul?**
- *Choices* Receipts and Payments Account → **Akaun Terimaan dan Pembayaran** · Income and Expenditure Account → **Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan**
- *Why (EN)* No cash moves for depreciation, so the cash summary never sees it — but it is a genuine cost of the year.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada tunai bergerak untuk susut nilai, jadi ringkasan tunai tidak melihatnya — tetapi ia kos sebenar bagi tahun ini.**
- → 

**`cl-003`** · classify · difficulty 4 · club-accounts, subscriptions

- *Question (EN)* A member pays next year's subscription early. In which account is the cash recorded?
- *Question (BM)* **Seorang ahli membayar yuran tahun depan lebih awal. Dalam akaun manakah tunai itu direkod?**
- *Choices* Receipts and Payments Account → **Akaun Terimaan dan Pembayaran** · Income and Expenditure Account → **Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan**
- *Why (EN)* The cash really arrived, so the cash summary records all of it. It is not income of THIS year, so it is kept out of the Income and Expenditure Account.
- *Why (BM)* **Tunai itu benar-benar masuk, jadi ringkasan tunai merekod semuanya. Ia bukan pendapatan tahun INI, jadi ia dikecualikan daripada Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan.**
- → 

**`cl-101`** · classify · difficulty 2 · club-accounts

- *Question (EN)* In a club, what is capital called?
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam kelab, apakah nama bagi modal?**
- *Choices* Accumulated Fund → **Kumpulan Wang Terkumpul** · Capital → **Modal**
- *Why (EN)* A club has no owner to hold capital, so the members' collective stake is the accumulated fund.
- *Why (BM)* **Kelab tiada pemilik yang memegang modal, jadi kepentingan bersama ahli ialah kumpulan wang terkumpul.**
- → 

**`cl-102`** · classify · difficulty 2 · club-accounts

- *Question (EN)* In a club, income exceeding expenditure is called a...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam kelab, pendapatan yang melebihi perbelanjaan dipanggil...**
- *Choices* Surplus → **Lebihan** · Net profit → **Untung bersih**
- *Why (EN)* A club is not trading for profit, so the excess is a surplus — and a shortfall is a deficit.
- *Why (BM)* **Kelab tidak berniaga untuk untung, jadi lebihannya ialah lebihan — dan kekurangan ialah defisit.**
- → 

**`cl-201`** · classify · difficulty 4 · club-accounts, subscriptions

- *Question (EN)* Subscriptions still owed by members at the year end are shown in the statement of financial position as...
- *Question (BM)* **Yuran yang masih terhutang oleh ahli pada akhir tahun ditunjukkan dalam penyata kedudukan kewangan sebagai...**
- *Choices* An asset → **Aset** · A liability → **Liabiliti**
- *Why (EN)* The club is owed money — arrears are accrued income, exactly like a receivable.
- *Why (BM)* **Kelab dihutang wang — tunggakan ialah pendapatan terakru, sama seperti Akaun Belum Terima.**
- → 

**`cl-202`** · classify · difficulty 4 · club-accounts, subscriptions

- *Question (EN)* Subscriptions received in advance for next year are shown in the statement of financial position as...
- *Question (BM)* **Yuran diterima terdahulu bagi tahun depan ditunjukkan dalam penyata kedudukan kewangan sebagai...**
- *Choices* An asset → **Aset** · A liability → **Liabiliti**
- *Why (EN)* The club owes those members a year of membership it has not yet provided.
- *Why (BM)* **Kelab berhutang kepada ahli itu satu tahun keahlian yang belum disediakan.**
- → 

**`cl-301`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · club-accounts, subscriptions

- *Question (EN)* Subscriptions received during the year were RM9,000, and RM700 is still owed by members at the year end. There are no other adjustments. What is the subscription income for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Yuran diterima sepanjang tahun ialah RM9,000, dan RM700 masih terhutang oleh ahli pada akhir tahun. Tiada pelarasan lain. Berapakah pendapatan yuran bagi tahun itu?**
- *Why (EN)* 9,000 + 700 = 9,700. The club earned that RM700 this year even though it has not arrived — the same rule as accrued income.
- *Why (BM)* **9,000 + 700 = 9,700. Kelab memperoleh RM700 itu tahun ini walaupun ia belum sampai — peraturan yang sama seperti pendapatan terakru.**
- → 

**`cl-302`** · numeric · difficulty 5 · club-accounts, subscriptions

- *Question (EN)* Subscriptions received RM12,000. Arrears at the start RM800, arrears at the end RM600, advance at the start RM400, advance at the end RM500. What is the subscription income for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Yuran diterima RM12,000. Tunggakan awal RM800, tunggakan akhir RM600, terdahulu awal RM400, terdahulu akhir RM500. Berapakah pendapatan yuran bagi tahun itu?**
- *Why (EN)* 12,000 − 800 + 600 + 400 − 500 = 11,700. Opening arrears were collected this year but earned last year; closing arrears were earned this year but not collected.
- *Why (BM)* **12,000 − 800 + 600 + 400 − 500 = 11,700. Tunggakan awal dikutip tahun ini tetapi diperoleh tahun lepas; tunggakan akhir diperoleh tahun ini tetapi belum dikutip.**
- → 

**`cl-401`** · statement_build · difficulty 4 · club-accounts, statement-placement

- *Question (EN)* Sort these into the Income and Expenditure Account, then work out the surplus.
- *Question (BM)* **Asingkan ini ke dalam Akaun Pendapatan dan Perbelanjaan, kemudian kira lebihannya.**
- *Why (EN)* Income 13,700 − expenditure 8,700 = surplus 5,000. Depreciation belongs here even though no cash moved.
- *Why (BM)* **Pendapatan 13,700 − perbelanjaan 8,700 = lebihan 5,000. Susut nilai tergolong di sini walaupun tiada tunai bergerak.**
- → 

#### Fading the Subscriptions Working

`l27-faded-subscriptions` · BM title: `Melunturkan Kerja Kira Yuran`

> **Worked example (EN)** Subscriptions are the one club figure that is almost never just the cash. Start from the cash received, then ask of each adjustment: does this belong to THIS year? Opening arrears were earned last year, so take them off; closing arrears were earned this year, so add them on. Opening advances were paid for this year, so add them; closing advances belong to next year, so take them off.
> **(BM)** Yuran ialah satu-satunya angka kelab yang hampir tidak pernah sama dengan tunai. Mula daripada tunai diterima, kemudian tanya bagi setiap pelarasan: adakah ini milik tahun INI? Tunggakan awal diperoleh tahun lepas, jadi tolak; tunggakan akhir diperoleh tahun ini, jadi tambah. Terdahulu awal dibayar untuk tahun ini, jadi tambah; terdahulu akhir milik tahun depan, jadi tolak.
- → 

**`fd-801`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · club-accounts, subscriptions, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Subscriptions received RM12,000. Arrears at the start RM800, arrears at the end RM600, advance at the start RM400, advance at the end RM500.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Yuran diterima RM12,000. Tunggakan awal RM800, tunggakan akhir RM600, terdahulu awal RM400, terdahulu akhir RM500.**
- *Question (EN)* The adjustments are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Pelarasan sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Subscriptions received in cash → **Yuran diterima secara tunai**
  - Less: arrears at the start → **Tolak: tunggakan awal**
  - Add: arrears at the end → **Tambah: tunggakan akhir**
  - Add: advance at the start → **Tambah: terdahulu awal**
  - Less: advance at the end → **Tolak: terdahulu akhir**
  - ␣ Subscription income for the year → **Pendapatan yuran bagi tahun**
- *Why (EN)* 12,000 − 800 + 600 + 400 − 500 = 11,700.
- *Why (BM)* **12,000 − 800 + 600 + 400 − 500 = 11,700.**
- → 

**`fd-802`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · club-accounts, subscriptions, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Subscriptions received RM15,000. Arrears at the start RM1,000, arrears at the end RM1,200, advance at the start RM300, advance at the end RM700.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Yuran diterima RM15,000. Tunggakan awal RM1,000, tunggakan akhir RM1,200, terdahulu awal RM300, terdahulu akhir RM700.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Work out the subscription income.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Kira pendapatan yuran.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Subscriptions received in cash → **Yuran diterima secara tunai**
  - Less: arrears at the start → **Tolak: tunggakan awal**
  - Add: arrears at the end → **Tambah: tunggakan akhir**
  - ␣ Add: advance at the start → **Tambah: terdahulu awal**
  - ␣ Less: advance at the end → **Tolak: terdahulu akhir**
  - ␣ Subscription income for the year → **Pendapatan yuran bagi tahun**
- *Why (EN)* 15,000 − 1,000 + 1,200 + 300 − 700 = 14,800. The two arrears lines pull opposite ways, and so do the two advance lines.
- *Why (BM)* **15,000 − 1,000 + 1,200 + 300 − 700 = 14,800. Dua baris tunggakan menarik arah bertentangan, begitu juga dua baris terdahulu.**
- → 

**`fd-803`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · club-accounts, subscriptions, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Subscriptions received RM20,000. Arrears at the start RM1,500, arrears at the end RM900, advance at the start RM600, advance at the end RM1,100.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Yuran diterima RM20,000. Tunggakan awal RM1,500, tunggakan akhir RM900, terdahulu awal RM600, terdahulu akhir RM1,100.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the cash received is given. Work out the subscription income.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya tunai diterima diberi. Kira pendapatan yuran.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Subscriptions received in cash → **Yuran diterima secara tunai**
  - ␣ Less: arrears at the start → **Tolak: tunggakan awal**
  - ␣ Add: arrears at the end → **Tambah: tunggakan akhir**
  - ␣ Add: advance at the start → **Tambah: terdahulu awal**
  - ␣ Less: advance at the end → **Tolak: terdahulu akhir**
  - ␣ Subscription income for the year → **Pendapatan yuran bagi tahun**
- *Why (EN)* 20,000 − 1,500 + 900 + 600 − 1,100 = 18,900. Here the income is LESS than the cash — a lot of last year's arrears were collected this year.
- *Why (BM)* **20,000 − 1,500 + 900 + 600 − 1,100 = 18,900. Di sini pendapatan KURANG daripada tunai — banyak tunggakan tahun lepas dikutip tahun ini.**
- → 

### Partnership Accounts

`t17-partnership` · BM title: `Akaun Perkongsian`
- → 

#### Capital or Current?

`l28-capital-vs-current` · BM title: `Modal atau Semasa?`

> **Worked example (EN)** With two or more owners, one capital account is no longer enough — you must be able to see what each partner is entitled to. So each partner gets TWO accounts. The CAPITAL account holds their permanent investment and barely moves. The CURRENT account holds everything that changes year to year: credited with their share of profit, interest on capital and any salary; debited with drawings and interest on drawings. Anything a partner earns from the business is a credit to their current account; anything they take out is a debit.
> **(BM)** Dengan dua atau lebih pemilik, satu akaun modal tidak lagi mencukupi — anda mesti dapat melihat hak setiap pekongsi. Jadi setiap pekongsi mendapat DUA akaun. Akaun MODAL memegang pelaburan tetap mereka dan jarang berubah. Akaun SEMASA memegang segala yang berubah dari tahun ke tahun: dikredit dengan bahagian untung, faedah atas modal dan sebarang gaji; didebit dengan ambilan dan faedah atas ambilan. Apa sahaja yang pekongsi peroleh daripada perniagaan ialah kredit ke akaun semasa; apa sahaja yang mereka ambil ialah debit.
- → 

**`pa-001`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* A partner's share of the year's profit is credited to which account?
- *Question (BM)* **Bahagian untung tahunan seorang pekongsi dikreditkan ke akaun yang mana?**
- *Choices* Current Account → **Akaun Semasa** · Capital Account → **Akaun Modal**
- *Why (EN)* Profit share changes every year, so it belongs in the current account — the capital account is for the permanent stake.
- *Why (BM)* **Bahagian untung berubah setiap tahun, jadi ia masuk akaun semasa — akaun modal untuk pegangan tetap.**
- → 

**`pa-002`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* A partner pays in a further RM20,000 as a permanent investment. Which account is credited?
- *Question (BM)* **Seorang pekongsi memasukkan RM20,000 lagi sebagai pelaburan tetap. Akaun manakah dikreditkan?**
- *Choices* Current Account → **Akaun Semasa** · Capital Account → **Akaun Modal**
- *Why (EN)* A permanent increase in what the partner has invested goes to the capital account.
- *Why (BM)* **Pertambahan tetap dalam pelaburan pekongsi masuk ke akaun modal.**
- → 

**`pa-003`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* A partner's drawings during the year are debited to which account?
- *Question (BM)* **Ambilan seorang pekongsi sepanjang tahun didebitkan ke akaun yang mana?**
- *Choices* Current Account → **Akaun Semasa** · Capital Account → **Akaun Modal**
- *Why (EN)* Drawings are part of the year-to-year movement, so they reduce the current account, not the capital.
- *Why (BM)* **Ambilan sebahagian daripada pergerakan tahunan, jadi ia mengurangkan akaun semasa, bukan modal.**
- → 

**`pa-101`** · debit_credit · difficulty 4 · partnership, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In a partner's current account, interest on capital is entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam akaun semasa pekongsi, faedah atas modal dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The partner earns it for leaving money in the business, so it increases what the business owes them.
- *Why (BM)* **Pekongsi memperolehnya kerana meninggalkan wang dalam perniagaan, jadi ia menambah hutang perniagaan kepada mereka.**
- → 

**`pa-102`** · debit_credit · difficulty 5 · partnership, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In a partner's current account, interest on drawings is entered on the...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam akaun semasa pekongsi, faedah atas ambilan dicatat di sebelah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* It is a charge ON the partner for taking money out early — the opposite direction to interest on capital.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia caj KE ATAS pekongsi kerana mengambil wang awal — arah bertentangan dengan faedah atas modal.**
- → 

**`pa-201`** · classify · difficulty 5 · partnership, appropriation

- *Question (EN)* In the appropriation account, interest on drawings is...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam akaun pengagihan, faedah atas ambilan adalah...**
- *Choices* Added to profit → **Ditambah kepada untung** · Deducted from profit → **Ditolak daripada untung**
- *Why (EN)* The partners are charged it, so it comes back INTO the pool of profit available to share. It is the one line that goes up.
- *Why (BM)* **Pekongsi dicaj untuknya, jadi ia kembali MASUK ke dalam untung yang boleh dibahagi. Ia satu-satunya baris yang naik.**
- → 

**`pa-202`** · classify · difficulty 4 · partnership, appropriation

- *Question (EN)* In the appropriation account, a partner's salary is...
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam akaun pengagihan, gaji seorang pekongsi adalah...**
- *Choices* Added to profit → **Ditambah kepada untung** · Deducted from profit → **Ditolak daripada untung**
- *Why (EN)* It is taken out before the rest is shared — a partner's salary is an appropriation of profit, never an expense in the income statement.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia dikeluarkan sebelum yang selebihnya dibahagi — gaji pekongsi ialah pengagihan untung, bukan belanja dalam penyata pendapatan.**
- → 

**`pa-301`** · numeric · difficulty 5 · partnership, appropriation

- *Question (EN)* Net profit RM60,000. Interest on capital: Ali RM4,000, Bakar RM3,000. Salary to Bakar RM8,000. Interest on drawings: Ali RM500, Bakar RM300. What is the residual profit left to share?
- *Question (BM)* **Untung bersih RM60,000. Faedah atas modal: Ali RM4,000, Bakar RM3,000. Gaji kepada Bakar RM8,000. Faedah atas ambilan: Ali RM500, Bakar RM300. Berapakah untung baki yang tinggal untuk dibahagi?**
- *Why (EN)* 60,000 − 4,000 − 3,000 − 8,000 + 800 = 45,800. Everything the partners are entitled to comes out first; the interest they are charged goes back in.
- *Why (BM)* **60,000 − 4,000 − 3,000 − 8,000 + 800 = 45,800. Semua hak pekongsi dikeluarkan dahulu; faedah yang dicaj kepada mereka dimasukkan semula.**
- → 

**`pa-302`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · partnership, appropriation

- *Question (EN)* Residual profit of RM45,800 is shared between Ali and Bakar in the ratio 3:2. What is Ali's share?
- *Question (BM)* **Untung baki RM45,800 dibahagi antara Ali dan Bakar dengan nisbah 3:2. Berapakah bahagian Ali?**
- *Why (EN)* 3 + 2 = 5 parts, so Ali takes 3/5: 45,800 × 3 ÷ 5 = 27,480.
- *Why (BM)* **3 + 2 = 5 bahagian, jadi Ali mengambil 3/5: 45,800 × 3 ÷ 5 = 27,480.**
- → 

**`pa-401`** · t_account · difficulty 5 · partnership, ledger

- *Question (EN)* Post these to Ali's Current Account, then find the closing balance.
- *Question (BM)* **Catat ini ke Akaun Semasa Ali, kemudian cari baki penutup.**
- *Why (EN)* Credits 2,000 + 4,000 + 27,480 = 33,480. Debits 15,000 + 500 = 15,500. Balance 17,980 credit — the business still owes Ali that much.
- *Why (BM)* **Kredit 2,000 + 4,000 + 27,480 = 33,480. Debit 15,000 + 500 = 15,500. Baki 17,980 kredit — perniagaan masih berhutang sebanyak itu kepada Ali.**
- → 

#### Fading the Appropriation

`l29-faded-appropriation` · BM title: `Melunturkan Pengagihan`

> **Worked example (EN)** The appropriation account always runs in the same order: start at net profit, take out everything the partners are individually entitled to (interest on capital, then salaries), add back the interest they are charged on drawings, and whatever remains is shared in the profit-sharing ratio. Only the last line of the first rung is blank; after that more of it goes.
> **(BM)** Akaun pengagihan sentiasa berjalan dalam susunan yang sama: mula pada untung bersih, keluarkan segala hak individu pekongsi (faedah atas modal, kemudian gaji), tambah semula faedah yang dicaj atas ambilan, dan apa yang tinggal dibahagi mengikut nisbah perkongsian untung. Hanya baris terakhir anak tangga pertama yang kosong; selepas itu lebih banyak hilang.
- → 

**`fd-901`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · partnership, appropriation, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Net profit RM60,000. Interest on capital: Ali RM4,000, Bakar RM3,000. Salary to Bakar RM8,000. Interest on drawings totals RM800. Ali and Bakar share profits 3:2.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Untung bersih RM60,000. Faedah atas modal: Ali RM4,000, Bakar RM3,000. Gaji kepada Bakar RM8,000. Faedah atas ambilan berjumlah RM800. Ali dan Bakar berkongsi untung 3:2.**
- *Question (EN)* The appropriation is done. Work out Ali's share.
- *Question (BM)* **Pengagihan sudah siap. Kira bahagian Ali.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Net profit → **Untung bersih**
  - Less: interest on capital — Ali → **Tolak: faedah atas modal — Ali**
  - Less: interest on capital — Bakar → **Tolak: faedah atas modal — Bakar**
  - Less: salary — Bakar → **Tolak: gaji — Bakar**
  - Add: interest on drawings → **Tambah: faedah atas ambilan**
  - Residual profit to share → **Untung baki untuk dibahagi**
  - ␣ Ali's share (3/5) → **Bahagian Ali (3/5)**
- *Why (EN)* 45,800 × 3 ÷ 5 = 27,480.
- *Why (BM)* **45,800 × 3 ÷ 5 = 27,480.**
- → 

**`fd-902`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · partnership, appropriation, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Net profit RM80,000. Interest on capital: Ali RM5,000, Bakar RM4,000. Salary to Bakar RM10,000. Interest on drawings totals RM1,000. Ali and Bakar share profits 3:2.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Untung bersih RM80,000. Faedah atas modal: Ali RM5,000, Bakar RM4,000. Gaji kepada Bakar RM10,000. Faedah atas ambilan berjumlah RM1,000. Ali dan Bakar berkongsi untung 3:2.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Complete the appropriation.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Lengkapkan pengagihan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Net profit → **Untung bersih**
  - Less: interest on capital — Ali → **Tolak: faedah atas modal — Ali**
  - Less: interest on capital — Bakar → **Tolak: faedah atas modal — Bakar**
  - Less: salary — Bakar → **Tolak: gaji — Bakar**
  - ␣ Add: interest on drawings → **Tambah: faedah atas ambilan**
  - ␣ Residual profit to share → **Untung baki untuk dibahagi**
  - ␣ Ali's share (3/5) → **Bahagian Ali (3/5)**
- *Why (EN)* 80,000 − 5,000 − 4,000 − 10,000 + 1,000 = 62,000, and 62,000 × 3 ÷ 5 = 37,200. Interest on drawings is the only line that is ADDED.
- *Why (BM)* **80,000 − 5,000 − 4,000 − 10,000 + 1,000 = 62,000, dan 62,000 × 3 ÷ 5 = 37,200. Faedah atas ambilan satu-satunya baris yang DITAMBAH.**
- → 

**`fd-903`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · partnership, appropriation, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Net profit RM50,000. Interest on capital: Ali RM3,000, Bakar RM2,000. Salary to Bakar RM6,000. Interest on drawings totals RM500. Ali and Bakar share profits 3:2.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Untung bersih RM50,000. Faedah atas modal: Ali RM3,000, Bakar RM2,000. Gaji kepada Bakar RM6,000. Faedah atas ambilan berjumlah RM500. Ali dan Bakar berkongsi untung 3:2.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the net profit is given. Work the whole appropriation.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya untung bersih diberi. Kerjakan keseluruhan pengagihan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Net profit → **Untung bersih**
  - ␣ Less: interest on capital — Ali → **Tolak: faedah atas modal — Ali**
  - ␣ Less: interest on capital — Bakar → **Tolak: faedah atas modal — Bakar**
  - ␣ Less: salary — Bakar → **Tolak: gaji — Bakar**
  - ␣ Add: interest on drawings → **Tambah: faedah atas ambilan**
  - ␣ Residual profit to share → **Untung baki untuk dibahagi**
  - ␣ Ali's share (3/5) → **Bahagian Ali (3/5)**
- *Why (EN)* 50,000 − 3,000 − 2,000 − 6,000 + 500 = 39,500, and 39,500 × 3 ÷ 5 = 23,700. Bakar takes the other 2/5 — RM15,800 — plus his salary and interest on capital.
- *Why (BM)* **50,000 − 3,000 − 2,000 − 6,000 + 500 = 39,500, dan 39,500 × 3 ÷ 5 = 23,700. Bakar mengambil 2/5 yang lain — RM15,800 — campur gaji dan faedah atas modalnya.**
- → 

#### When There Is No Agreement

`l51-no-agreement` · BM title: `Apabila Tiada Perjanjian`

> **Worked example (EN)** Partners are free to agree whatever they like. When they have agreed NOTHING in writing, section 26 of the Partnership Act 1961 decides for them, and its rules are deliberately plain: profits and losses are shared EQUALLY however unequal the capitals are; no partner gets a salary; no interest is allowed on capital; no interest is charged on drawings; and a partner who lends the firm money beyond their capital gets 5% a year on that loan. The exam tests this by giving unequal capitals and no agreement — the tempting answer is to split profit in the capital ratio, and it is wrong.
> **(BM)** Pekongsi bebas bersetuju apa sahaja yang mereka mahu. Apabila mereka TIDAK bersetuju apa-apa secara bertulis, seksyen 26 Akta Perkongsian 1961 menentukannya untuk mereka, dan peraturannya memang mudah: untung dan rugi dikongsi SAMA RATA walau betapa tidak sama modalnya; tiada pekongsi mendapat gaji; tiada faedah dibenarkan atas modal; tiada faedah dikenakan atas ambilan; dan pekongsi yang meminjamkan wang kepada firma melebihi modalnya mendapat 5% setahun atas pinjaman itu. Peperiksaan mengujinya dengan memberi modal yang tidak sama dan tiada perjanjian — jawapan yang menggoda ialah membahagi untung mengikut nisbah modal, dan ia salah.
- → 

**`pa-501`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* There is no partnership agreement. How are profits shared?
- *Question (BM)* **Tiada perjanjian perkongsian. Bagaimanakah untung dikongsi?**
- *Choices* Equally → **Sama rata** · In the capital ratio → **Mengikut nisbah modal** · In proportion to work done → **Mengikut kadar kerja yang dilakukan**
- *Why (EN)* Section 26 says equally, no matter how unequal the capitals are. Splitting by capital is the classic wrong answer.
- *Why (BM)* **Seksyen 26 menyatakan sama rata, tidak kira betapa tidak sama modalnya. Membahagi mengikut modal ialah jawapan salah yang klasik.**
- → 

**`pa-502`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* With no agreement, is a partner entitled to a salary for managing the business?
- *Question (BM)* **Tanpa perjanjian, adakah seorang pekongsi berhak mendapat gaji kerana menguruskan perniagaan?**
- *Choices* No → **Tidak** · Yes → **Ya**
- *Why (EN)* A salary has to be agreed. Without an agreement, the reward for working in the firm is the share of profit.
- *Why (BM)* **Gaji perlu dipersetujui. Tanpa perjanjian, ganjaran bekerja dalam firma ialah bahagian untung.**
- → 

**`pa-503`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* With no agreement, is interest allowed on partners’ capital?
- *Question (BM)* **Tanpa perjanjian, adakah faedah dibenarkan atas modal pekongsi?**
