- *Choices* No → **Tidak** · Yes, 5% a year → **Ya, 5% setahun** · Yes, 8% a year → **Ya, 8% setahun**
- *Why (EN)* None at all. The 5% in section 26 is for a LOAN a partner makes to the firm, not for capital.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada langsung. Kadar 5% dalam seksyen 26 adalah untuk PINJAMAN yang diberikan pekongsi kepada firma, bukan untuk modal.**
- → 

**`pa-504`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* With no agreement, a partner lends the firm RM20,000 on top of their capital. How much interest is due for one year?
- *Question (BM)* **Tanpa perjanjian, seorang pekongsi meminjamkan firma RM20,000 selain modalnya. Berapakah faedah yang perlu dibayar bagi satu tahun?**
- *Why (EN)* 5% × 20,000 = 1,000. This is the one payment section 26 does allow, and it is on a loan, not on capital.
- *Why (BM)* **5% × 20,000 = 1,000. Inilah satu-satunya bayaran yang dibenarkan seksyen 26, dan ia atas pinjaman, bukan atas modal.**
- → 

**`pa-505`** · classify · difficulty 4 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* With no agreement, is interest charged on a partner’s drawings?
- *Question (BM)* **Tanpa perjanjian, adakah faedah dikenakan atas ambilan pekongsi?**
- *Choices* No → **Tidak** · Yes → **Ya**
- *Why (EN)* Interest on drawings must be agreed. Section 26 charges none.
- *Why (BM)* **Faedah atas ambilan mesti dipersetujui. Seksyen 26 tidak mengenakan apa-apa.**
- → 

**`pa-506`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · partnership

- *Question (EN)* Two partners have capitals of RM60,000 and RM20,000 and no agreement. Profit is RM36,000. What is the partner with RM20,000 capital entitled to?
- *Question (BM)* **Dua pekongsi mempunyai modal RM60,000 dan RM20,000 serta tiada perjanjian. Untung ialah RM36,000. Berapakah hak pekongsi yang bermodal RM20,000?**
- *Why (EN)* Equally, so 36,000 ÷ 2 = 18,000. Sharing 3:1 by capital would give 9,000, and that is exactly the trap.
- *Why (BM)* **Sama rata, jadi 36,000 ÷ 2 = 18,000. Berkongsi 3:1 mengikut modal akan memberi 9,000, dan itulah perangkapnya.**
- → 

#### Winding the Firm Up

`l52-dissolution` · BM title: `Membubarkan Firma`

> **Worked example (EN)** When a partnership is dissolved, the assets are sold, the creditors are paid, and whatever is left goes to the partners. A REALISATION ACCOUNT does the arithmetic. Debit it with the book value of every asset being sold and with the expenses of winding up; credit it with what the assets actually fetched and with any asset a partner takes over at an agreed value. The balance is the profit or loss on realisation, and it is shared between the partners in their profit-sharing ratio — which is the point: nobody knows what an asset will fetch until it is sold, so the gain or shortfall belongs to the partners, not to the business.
> **(BM)** Apabila perkongsian dibubarkan, aset dijual, pemiutang dibayar, dan bakinya diberikan kepada pekongsi. AKAUN REALISASI melakukan pengiraannya. Debitkan ia dengan nilai buku setiap aset yang dijual dan dengan belanja pembubaran; kreditkan ia dengan hasil sebenar jualan aset dan dengan mana-mana aset yang diambil alih oleh pekongsi pada nilai yang dipersetujui. Bakinya ialah untung atau rugi atas realisasi, dan ia dikongsi antara pekongsi mengikut nisbah perkongsian untung — itulah maksudnya: tiada siapa tahu harga sesuatu aset sehingga ia dijual, jadi lebihan atau kekurangan itu milik pekongsi, bukan milik perniagaan.
- → 

**`pd-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · partnership, dissolution

- *Question (EN)* On dissolution, which account is opened to work out the gain or loss on selling the assets?
- *Question (BM)* **Semasa pembubaran, akaun manakah dibuka untuk mengira untung atau rugi atas penjualan aset?**
- *Choices* Realisation Account → **Akaun Realisasi** · Appropriation Account → **Akaun Pengagihan** · Capital Account → **Akaun Modal** · Current Account → **Akaun Semasa**
- *Why (EN)* Everything to do with turning assets into cash passes through it.
- *Why (BM)* **Segala yang berkaitan menukar aset kepada tunai melaluinya.**
- → 

**`pd-002`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership, dissolution, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Realisation Account, on which side is the BOOK VALUE of an asset entered?
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Realisasi, di sebelah manakah NILAI BUKU sesuatu aset dicatatkan?**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* The asset arrives at its book value on the debit side; what it actually sells for comes in on the credit side to be compared against it.
- *Why (BM)* **Aset masuk pada nilai bukunya di sebelah debit; harga jualan sebenarnya masuk di sebelah kredit untuk dibandingkan dengannya.**
- → 

**`pd-003`** · classify · difficulty 3 · partnership, dissolution, debit-credit

- *Question (EN)* In the Realisation Account, on which side are the PROCEEDS from selling the assets entered?
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam Akaun Realisasi, di sebelah manakah HASIL daripada penjualan aset dicatatkan?**
- *Choices* Credit → **Kredit** · Debit → **Debit**
- *Why (EN)* Money coming in for the assets is credited, so a credit total above the debit total is a profit on realisation.
- *Why (BM)* **Wang masuk bagi aset dikreditkan, jadi jumlah kredit melebihi jumlah debit ialah untung atas realisasi.**
- → 

**`pd-004`** · classify · difficulty 4 · partnership, dissolution

- *Question (EN)* A partner takes over a vehicle at an agreed value instead of it being sold. How is that treated?
- *Question (BM)* **Seorang pekongsi mengambil alih sebuah kenderaan pada nilai yang dipersetujui dan bukannya dijual. Bagaimanakah ia dilayan?**
- *Choices* Credit Realisation, debit that partner’s capital → **Kredit Realisasi, debit modal pekongsi itu** · Debit Realisation, credit that partner’s capital → **Debit Realisasi, kredit modal pekongsi itu** · It is ignored → **Ia diabaikan**
- *Why (EN)* It is a disposal like any other — the firm parts with the asset and that partner owes the firm its agreed value.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia pelupusan seperti yang lain — firma melepaskan aset itu dan pekongsi berkenaan berhutang kepada firma pada nilai yang dipersetujui.**
- → 

**`pd-005`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · partnership, dissolution

- *Question (EN)* Assets with a book value of RM50,000 are sold for RM56,000. How much is the profit on realisation?
- *Question (BM)* **Aset bernilai buku RM50,000 dijual pada harga RM56,000. Berapakah untung atas realisasi?**
- *Why (EN)* 56,000 − 50,000 = 6,000, shared between the partners in their profit-sharing ratio.
- *Why (BM)* **56,000 − 50,000 = 6,000, dikongsi antara pekongsi mengikut nisbah perkongsian untung.**
- → 

**`pd-006`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · partnership, dissolution

- *Question (EN)* Assets with a book value of RM80,000 fetch RM71,000 and dissolution expenses are RM2,000. How much is the LOSS on realisation?
- *Question (BM)* **Aset bernilai buku RM80,000 laku RM71,000 dan belanja pembubaran ialah RM2,000. Berapakah RUGI atas realisasi?**
- *Why (EN)* 80,000 + 2,000 debited against 71,000 credited = 11,000 loss. The winding-up costs sit on the debit side with the assets.
- *Why (BM)* **80,000 + 2,000 didebit berbanding 71,000 dikredit = rugi 11,000. Belanja pembubaran berada di sebelah debit bersama aset.**
- → 

**`pd-007`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · partnership, dissolution

- *Question (EN)* A loss on realisation of RM12,000 is shared between two partners in the ratio 2:1. What is the SMALLER share?
- *Question (BM)* **Rugi atas realisasi RM12,000 dikongsi antara dua pekongsi mengikut nisbah 2:1. Berapakah bahagian yang LEBIH KECIL?**
- *Why (EN)* 12,000 ÷ 3 = 4,000 for one part. A loss is shared in the profit-sharing ratio, exactly as a profit would be.
- *Why (BM)* **12,000 ÷ 3 = 4,000 bagi satu bahagian. Rugi dikongsi mengikut nisbah perkongsian untung, sama seperti untung.**
- → 

**`pd-008`** · classify · difficulty 4 · partnership, dissolution

- *Question (EN)* In what order are the proceeds of a dissolution applied?
- *Question (BM)* **Dalam susunan manakah hasil pembubaran digunakan?**
- *Choices* Outside creditors, then partners’ loans, then capitals → **Pemiutang luar, kemudian pinjaman pekongsi, kemudian modal** · Partners’ capitals, then outside creditors → **Modal pekongsi, kemudian pemiutang luar** · Everyone is paid at the same time → **Semua dibayar pada masa yang sama**
- *Why (EN)* People outside the firm are paid first. The partners are the owners, so they rank last — that is what ownership means when a business ends.
- *Why (BM)* **Pihak luar firma dibayar dahulu. Pekongsi ialah pemilik, jadi mereka berada di kedudukan terakhir — itulah maksud pemilikan apabila sesuatu perniagaan berakhir.**
- → 

### Limited Company Accounts

`t18-limited-companies` · BM title: `Akaun Syarikat Berhad`
- → 

#### Shares, Debentures & Reserves

`l32-company-capital` · BM title: `Syer, Debentur & Rizab`

> **Worked example (EN)** A company is owned by its shareholders and financed partly by lenders. Share capital and every reserve belong to the owners, so they are equity. A debenture is a loan: the holder is a lender, not an owner, and must be paid interest whether or not the company makes a profit.
> **(BM)** Syarikat dimiliki oleh pemegang syernya dan sebahagian pembiayaannya datang daripada pemberi pinjaman. Modal syer dan setiap rizab adalah milik pemilik, jadi ia ekuiti. Debentur pula ialah pinjaman: pemegangnya pemberi pinjaman, bukan pemilik, dan mesti dibayar faedah sama ada syarikat untung atau tidak.
- → 

**`co-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · company-accounts, share-capital

- *Question (EN)* Where does Ordinary Share Capital belong?
- *Question (BM)* **Di manakah Modal Syer Biasa tergolong?**
- *Choices* Equity → **Ekuiti** · Non-current liability → **Liabiliti bukan semasa** · Current liability → **Liabiliti semasa**
- *Why (EN)* Ordinary shareholders own the company, so what they put in is equity.
- *Why (BM)* **Pemegang syer biasa memiliki syarikat, jadi apa yang mereka masukkan ialah ekuiti.**
- → 

**`co-002`** · classify · difficulty 3 · company-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Where do 8% Debentures repayable in 2035 belong?
- *Question (BM)* **Di manakah Debentur 8% yang perlu dibayar balik pada 2035 tergolong?**
- *Choices* Equity → **Ekuiti** · Non-current liability → **Liabiliti bukan semasa** · Current liability → **Liabiliti semasa**
- *Why (EN)* A debenture is a long-term loan, not ownership. It is repayable more than a year away, so it is a non-current liability — and its interest is an expense, not a dividend.
- *Why (BM)* **Debentur ialah pinjaman jangka panjang, bukan pemilikan. Ia perlu dibayar balik lebih setahun lagi, jadi ia liabiliti bukan semasa — dan faedahnya ialah belanja, bukan dividen.**
- → 

**`co-003`** · classify · difficulty 2 · company-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Where does Retained Profit belong?
- *Question (BM)* **Di manakah Untung Tertahan tergolong?**
- *Choices* Equity → **Ekuiti** · Non-current liability → **Liabiliti bukan semasa** · Current liability → **Liabiliti semasa**
- *Why (EN)* Retained profit is profit the owners have earned but left in the business, so it is still theirs.
- *Why (BM)* **Untung tertahan ialah untung yang telah diperoleh pemilik tetapi dibiarkan dalam perniagaan, jadi ia masih milik mereka.**
- → 

**`co-004`** · classify · difficulty 3 · company-accounts, dividends

- *Question (EN)* Where does a Proposed Ordinary Dividend belong?
- *Question (BM)* **Di manakah Dividen Biasa Dicadangkan tergolong?**
- *Choices* Equity → **Ekuiti** · Non-current liability → **Liabiliti bukan semasa** · Current liability → **Liabiliti semasa**
- *Why (EN)* Once a dividend is proposed the company owes it to the shareholders and will pay it within the year — an amount owed, not an amount held.
- *Why (BM)* **Sebaik dividen dicadangkan, syarikat terhutang kepada pemegang syer dan akan membayarnya dalam tahun ini — jumlah yang terhutang, bukan yang dipegang.**
- → 

**`co-005`** · classify · difficulty 3 · company-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Where does a General Reserve belong?
- *Question (BM)* **Di manakah Rizab Am tergolong?**
- *Choices* Equity → **Ekuiti** · Non-current liability → **Liabiliti bukan semasa** · Current liability → **Liabiliti semasa**
- *Why (EN)* Transferring profit to a general reserve moves it from one equity pocket to another. Nothing leaves the company, so it never becomes a liability.
- *Why (BM)* **Memindahkan untung ke rizab am hanya mengalihkannya dari satu poket ekuiti ke poket ekuiti yang lain. Tiada apa yang keluar dari syarikat, jadi ia tidak pernah menjadi liabiliti.**
- → 

**`co-006`** · debit_credit · difficulty 2 · company-accounts

- *Question (EN)* The company pays its debenture interest by cheque. The Debenture Interest account is...
- *Question (BM)* **Syarikat membayar faedah debenturnya dengan cek. Akaun Faedah Debentur ialah...**
- *Choices* Debit → **Debit** · Credit → **Kredit**
- *Why (EN)* Debenture interest is an expense, and an expense increasing is debited. The credit goes to Bank.
- *Why (BM)* **Faedah debentur ialah belanja, dan belanja yang bertambah didebitkan. Kreditnya pula ke akaun Bank.**
- → 

#### Appropriating the Profit

`l33-company-appropriation` · BM title: `Mengagihkan Untung`

> **Worked example (EN)** Once profit after tax is known, the company decides what to do with it: park some in a reserve, pay the preference shareholders their fixed percentage, pay the ordinary shareholders whatever is declared, and carry the rest forward. Interest is NOT part of this — it was already deducted to arrive at the profit.
> **(BM)** Setelah untung selepas cukai diketahui, syarikat memutuskan apa yang hendak dibuat dengannya: menyimpan sebahagian dalam rizab, membayar pemegang syer keutamaan peratusan tetap mereka, membayar pemegang syer biasa apa yang diisytiharkan, dan membawa bakinya ke hadapan. Faedah BUKAN sebahagian daripada ini — ia sudah ditolak sebelum untung diperoleh.
- → 

**`co-007`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · company-accounts, dividends

- *Question (EN)* A company has 200,000 6% preference shares of RM1 each. How much is the preference dividend for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah syarikat mempunyai 200,000 syer keutamaan 6% bernilai RM1 setiap satu. Berapakah dividen keutamaan bagi tahun tersebut?**
- *Why (EN)* 6% of RM200,000 = RM12,000. A preference dividend is a fixed percentage of the preference share capital — it does not change with profit.
- *Why (BM)* **6% daripada RM200,000 = RM12,000. Dividen keutamaan ialah peratusan tetap daripada modal syer keutamaan — ia tidak berubah mengikut untung.**
- → 

**`co-008`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · company-accounts, dividends

- *Question (EN)* A company declares an ordinary dividend of 4 sen per share on 500,000 ordinary shares. How much is the ordinary dividend?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah syarikat mengisytiharkan dividen biasa 4 sen sesyer ke atas 500,000 syer biasa. Berapakah dividen biasa itu?**
- *Why (EN)* 500,000 × RM0.04 = RM20,000. An ordinary dividend is declared per SHARE, so it depends on the number of shares, not on their value.
- *Why (BM)* **500,000 × RM0.04 = RM20,000. Dividen biasa diisytiharkan bagi setiap SYER, jadi ia bergantung pada bilangan syer, bukan pada nilainya.**
- → 

**`co-009`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · company-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Profit after tax RM150,000. Transfer to general reserve RM25,000, preference dividend RM12,000, ordinary dividend RM20,000. What is the retained profit for the year?
- *Question (BM)* **Untung selepas cukai RM150,000. Pindahan ke rizab am RM25,000, dividen keutamaan RM12,000, dividen biasa RM20,000. Berapakah untung tertahan bagi tahun tersebut?**
- *Why (EN)* 150,000 − 25,000 − 12,000 − 20,000 = 93,000. All three are appropriations of profit already earned.
- *Why (BM)* **150,000 − 25,000 − 12,000 − 20,000 = 93,000. Ketiga-tiganya ialah pengagihan untung yang telah diperoleh.**
- → 

**`co-010`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · company-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Retained profit brought forward is RM60,000 and the retained profit for the year is RM93,000. What is the retained profit carried forward?
- *Question (BM)* **Untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan ialah RM60,000 dan untung tertahan bagi tahun ini RM93,000. Berapakah untung tertahan yang dibawa ke hadapan?**
- *Why (EN)* 60,000 + 93,000 = 153,000. Retained profit accumulates year after year — it is a running total, not a single year's figure.
- *Why (BM)* **60,000 + 93,000 = 153,000. Untung tertahan terkumpul dari tahun ke tahun — ia jumlah berjalan, bukan angka satu tahun sahaja.**
- → 

**`co-011`** · classify · difficulty 4 · company-accounts

- *Question (EN)* Debenture interest is...
- *Question (BM)* **Faedah debentur ialah...**
- *Choices* An expense in the income statement → **Belanja dalam penyata pendapatan** · An appropriation of profit → **Pengagihan untung**
- *Why (EN)* This is the classic trap. Interest is the cost of borrowing and is deducted BEFORE profit is arrived at; a dividend is a share of profit already earned. Treating interest as an appropriation overstates the profit.
- *Why (BM)* **Inilah perangkap klasiknya. Faedah ialah kos meminjam dan ditolak SEBELUM untung diperoleh; dividen pula ialah bahagian daripada untung yang sudah diperoleh. Menganggap faedah sebagai pengagihan akan melebihkan untung.**
- → 

#### Fading the Appropriation

`l34-faded-company` · BM title: `Melunturkan Pengagihan Untung`

> **Worked example (EN)** The appropriation runs top to bottom in one direction: start from profit after tax, take off every appropriation, and what is left joins the retained profit already carried forward from last year. Each rung below hands you less of it.
> **(BM)** Pengagihan berjalan dari atas ke bawah dalam satu arah: mula dari untung selepas cukai, tolak setiap pengagihan, dan apa yang tinggal bergabung dengan untung tertahan yang sudah dibawa ke hadapan dari tahun lepas. Setiap anak tangga di bawah memberi anda kurang daripadanya.
- → 

**`fd-1101`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · company-accounts, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Profit after tax RM150,000. Transfer to general reserve RM25,000. Preference dividend RM12,000. Ordinary dividend RM20,000. Retained profit brought forward RM60,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Untung selepas cukai RM150,000. Pindahan ke rizab am RM25,000. Dividen keutamaan RM12,000. Dividen biasa RM20,000. Untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan RM60,000.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Profit after tax → **Untung selepas cukai**
  - Less: transfer to general reserve → **Tolak: pindahan ke rizab am**
  - Less: preference dividend → **Tolak: dividen keutamaan**
  - Less: ordinary dividend → **Tolak: dividen biasa**
  - Retained profit for the year → **Untung tertahan bagi tahun ini**
  - Add: retained profit brought forward → **Tambah: untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan**
  - ␣ Retained profit carried forward → **Untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan**
- *Why (EN)* 93,000 + 60,000 = 153,000. The carried-forward figure is what appears in the equity section of the statement of financial position.
- *Why (BM)* **93,000 + 60,000 = 153,000. Angka yang dibawa ke hadapan inilah yang muncul dalam bahagian ekuiti penyata kedudukan kewangan.**
- → 

**`fd-1102`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · company-accounts, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Profit after tax RM210,000. Transfer to general reserve RM40,000. Preference dividend RM9,000. Ordinary dividend RM36,000. Retained profit brought forward RM85,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Untung selepas cukai RM210,000. Pindahan ke rizab am RM40,000. Dividen keutamaan RM9,000. Dividen biasa RM36,000. Untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan RM85,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Complete the appropriation.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Lengkapkan pengagihan itu.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Profit after tax → **Untung selepas cukai**
  - Less: transfer to general reserve → **Tolak: pindahan ke rizab am**
  - Less: preference dividend → **Tolak: dividen keutamaan**
  - Less: ordinary dividend → **Tolak: dividen biasa**
  - ␣ Retained profit for the year → **Untung tertahan bagi tahun ini**
  - ␣ Add: retained profit brought forward → **Tambah: untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan**
  - ␣ Retained profit carried forward → **Untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan**
- *Why (EN)* 210,000 − 40,000 − 9,000 − 36,000 = 125,000, plus 85,000 brought forward = 210,000. Notice the year's profit and the closing retained profit are equal here purely by coincidence — they measure different things.
- *Why (BM)* **210,000 − 40,000 − 9,000 − 36,000 = 125,000, tambah 85,000 dibawa ke hadapan = 210,000. Perhatikan untung tahun ini dan untung tertahan penutup adalah sama di sini secara kebetulan sahaja — kedua-duanya mengukur perkara berbeza.**
- → 

**`fd-1103`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · company-accounts, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Profit after tax RM96,000. Transfer to general reserve RM15,000. Preference dividend RM6,000. Ordinary dividend RM25,000. Retained profit brought forward RM42,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Untung selepas cukai RM96,000. Pindahan ke rizab am RM15,000. Dividen keutamaan RM6,000. Dividen biasa RM25,000. Untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan RM42,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the profit after tax is given. Build the whole appropriation.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya untung selepas cukai diberi. Bina keseluruhan pengagihan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Profit after tax → **Untung selepas cukai**
  - ␣ Less: transfer to general reserve → **Tolak: pindahan ke rizab am**
  - ␣ Less: preference dividend → **Tolak: dividen keutamaan**
  - ␣ Less: ordinary dividend → **Tolak: dividen biasa**
  - ␣ Retained profit for the year → **Untung tertahan bagi tahun ini**
  - ␣ Add: retained profit brought forward → **Tambah: untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan**
  - ␣ Retained profit carried forward → **Untung tertahan dibawa ke hadapan**
- *Why (EN)* 96,000 − 15,000 − 6,000 − 25,000 = 50,000, plus 42,000 = 92,000. Every line after the first reduces the profit except the last two, which add back what was already there.
- *Why (BM)* **96,000 − 15,000 − 6,000 − 25,000 = 50,000, tambah 42,000 = 92,000. Setiap baris selepas yang pertama mengurangkan untung kecuali dua baris terakhir, yang menambah semula apa yang sudah ada.**
- → 

#### Issuing the Shares

`l55-share-issue` · BM title: `Menerbitkan Syer`

> **Worked example (EN)** A company invites the public to apply for shares. If applications come in for more shares than were offered the issue is OVERSUBSCRIBED, and the excess money is not the company’s to keep — it is refunded to the applicants, or held and set against the next amount they owe. Undersubscribed is the opposite problem: fewer applications than shares offered, and the underwriter who guaranteed the issue takes up the shortfall. Note also who ranks where: preference shareholders get their dividend at a fixed rate BEFORE the ordinary shareholders get anything, and in exchange they usually have no vote.
> **(BM)** Sesebuah syarikat menjemput orang ramai memohon syer. Jika permohonan melebihi syer yang ditawarkan, terbitan itu TERLEBIH LANGGANAN, dan lebihan wang itu bukan milik syarikat — ia dikembalikan kepada pemohon, atau ditahan dan ditolak daripada jumlah seterusnya yang mereka perlu bayar. Terkurang langganan ialah masalah sebaliknya: permohonan kurang daripada syer yang ditawarkan, dan penaja jamin yang menjamin terbitan itu mengambil kekurangannya. Perhatikan juga kedudukan masing-masing: pemegang syer keutamaan mendapat dividen pada kadar tetap SEBELUM pemegang syer biasa mendapat apa-apa, dan sebagai pertukaran mereka biasanya tiada hak mengundi.
- → 

**`si-001`** · classify · difficulty 3 · company-accounts, share-capital

- *Question (EN)* Applications are received for more shares than the company offered. What is this called?
- *Question (BM)* **Permohonan diterima bagi syer melebihi yang ditawarkan syarikat. Apakah ini dipanggil?**
- *Choices* Oversubscription → **Terlebih langganan** · Undersubscription → **Terkurang langganan** · Forfeiture → **Pelucuthakan**
- *Why (EN)* More money arrived than there are shares to give out.
- *Why (BM)* **Lebih banyak wang tiba berbanding syer yang ada untuk diberikan.**
- → 

**`si-002`** · classify · difficulty 4 · company-accounts, share-capital

- *Question (EN)* An issue is oversubscribed. What happens to the excess application money?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu terbitan terlebih langganan. Apakah yang berlaku kepada lebihan wang permohonan?**
- *Choices* It is refunded to applicants → **Ia dikembalikan kepada pemohon** · The company keeps it as profit → **Syarikat menyimpannya sebagai untung** · It becomes a reserve → **Ia menjadi rizab** · New shares are issued to absorb it → **Syer baharu diterbitkan untuk menampungnya**
- *Why (EN)* The company never sold them those shares, so the money is not its own. Issuing extra shares to keep it would change the capital the company said it was raising.
- *Why (BM)* **Syarikat tidak pernah menjual syer itu kepada mereka, jadi wang itu bukan miliknya. Menerbitkan syer tambahan untuk menyimpannya akan mengubah modal yang syarikat nyatakan hendak dikumpulkan.**
- → 

**`si-003`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · company-accounts, share-capital

- *Question (EN)* A company offers 400,000 shares at RM1.50 each. Applications are received for 500,000 shares, with the full amount sent in. How much must be refunded?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah syarikat menawarkan 400,000 syer pada RM1.50 sesyer. Permohonan diterima bagi 500,000 syer, dengan jumlah penuh dihantar. Berapakah yang mesti dikembalikan?**
- *Why (EN)* 100,000 shares too many × RM1.50 = 150,000 refunded.
- *Why (BM)* **100,000 syer terlebih × RM1.50 = 150,000 dikembalikan.**
- → 

**`si-004`** · classify · difficulty 3 · company-accounts, share-capital

- *Question (EN)* Which shareholders receive their dividend first?
- *Question (BM)* **Pemegang syer manakah menerima dividen mereka dahulu?**
- *Choices* Preference shareholders → **Pemegang syer keutamaan** · Ordinary shareholders → **Pemegang syer biasa** · Both at the same time → **Kedua-duanya pada masa yang sama**
- *Why (EN)* That priority is what "preference" means, and it comes at a fixed rate.
- *Why (BM)* **Keutamaan itulah maksud "keutamaan", dan ia pada kadar tetap.**
- → 

**`si-005`** · classify · difficulty 4 · company-accounts, share-capital

- *Question (EN)* Which is TRUE of ordinary shares compared with preference shares?
- *Question (BM)* **Yang manakah BENAR tentang syer biasa berbanding syer keutamaan?**
- *Choices* They usually carry voting rights → **Ia biasanya membawa hak mengundi** · They receive a fixed dividend → **Ia menerima dividen tetap** · They are paid before preference shares → **Ia dibayar sebelum syer keutamaan**
- *Why (EN)* Ordinary shareholders take more risk — no fixed dividend, paid last — and the vote is what they get for it.
- *Why (BM)* **Pemegang syer biasa menanggung lebih risiko — tiada dividen tetap, dibayar terakhir — dan hak mengundi ialah balasannya.**
- → 

### Manufacturing Accounts

`t19-manufacturing` · BM title: `Akaun Pengilangan`
- → 

#### Direct, Indirect or Neither

`l35-manufacturing-costs` · BM title: `Langsung, Tak Langsung atau Bukan Kilang`

> **Worked example (EN)** A cost is DIRECT if you can trace it to the product itself — the material in it, the wages of the person who made it, a royalty paid on each unit. A cost is a factory OVERHEAD if it keeps the factory running but cannot be traced to one unit. A cost that has nothing to do with the factory never enters the manufacturing account at all.
> **(BM)** Sesuatu kos adalah LANGSUNG jika ia boleh dikesan terus kepada produk itu sendiri — bahan di dalamnya, gaji orang yang membuatnya, royalti yang dibayar bagi setiap unit. Sesuatu kos ialah OVERHED kilang jika ia mengekalkan operasi kilang tetapi tidak boleh dikesan kepada satu unit. Kos yang tiada kaitan dengan kilang langsung tidak masuk ke dalam akaun pengilangan.
- → 

**`mf-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · manufacturing

- *Question (EN)* Raw materials bought for production are...
- *Question (BM)* **Bahan mentah yang dibeli untuk pengeluaran ialah...**
- *Choices* Direct material → **Bahan langsung** · Direct labour → **Buruh langsung** · Direct expense → **Belanja langsung** · Factory overhead → **Overhed kilang** · Non-factory expense → **Belanja bukan kilang**
- *Why (EN)* The material physically becomes part of the finished product, so it is traceable to it.
- *Why (BM)* **Bahan itu secara fizikal menjadi sebahagian daripada produk siap, jadi ia boleh dikesan kepadanya.**
- → 

**`mf-002`** · classify · difficulty 2 · manufacturing

- *Question (EN)* The wages of the machine operators who make the product are...
- *Question (BM)* **Gaji pengendali mesin yang membuat produk itu ialah...**
- *Choices* Direct material → **Bahan langsung** · Direct labour → **Buruh langsung** · Direct expense → **Belanja langsung** · Factory overhead → **Overhed kilang** · Non-factory expense → **Belanja bukan kilang**
- *Why (EN)* They work on the product itself, so their wages are traceable to it.
- *Why (BM)* **Mereka bekerja pada produk itu sendiri, jadi gaji mereka boleh dikesan kepadanya.**
- → 

**`mf-003`** · classify · difficulty 3 · manufacturing

- *Question (EN)* The salary of the factory supervisor is...
- *Question (BM)* **Gaji penyelia kilang ialah...**
- *Choices* Direct material → **Bahan langsung** · Direct labour → **Buruh langsung** · Direct expense → **Belanja langsung** · Factory overhead → **Overhed kilang** · Non-factory expense → **Belanja bukan kilang**
- *Why (EN)* The supervisor is in the factory but does not work on any one product, so the cost cannot be traced to a unit — indirect, not direct labour.
- *Why (BM)* **Penyelia berada di kilang tetapi tidak bekerja pada mana-mana produk tertentu, jadi kosnya tidak boleh dikesan kepada satu unit — tak langsung, bukan buruh langsung.**
- → 

**`mf-004`** · classify · difficulty 3 · manufacturing

- *Question (EN)* Depreciation of factory machinery is...
- *Question (BM)* **Susut nilai jentera kilang ialah...**
- *Choices* Direct material → **Bahan langsung** · Direct labour → **Buruh langsung** · Direct expense → **Belanja langsung** · Factory overhead → **Overhed kilang** · Non-factory expense → **Belanja bukan kilang**
- *Why (EN)* It is a cost of running the factory, spread across everything made there. Depreciation of OFFICE equipment would not belong here at all.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia kos mengendalikan kilang, tersebar merentasi segala yang dibuat di situ. Susut nilai peralatan PEJABAT pula langsung tidak tergolong di sini.**
- → 

**`mf-005`** · classify · difficulty 4 · manufacturing

- *Question (EN)* A royalty paid to a designer on every unit produced is...
- *Question (BM)* **Royalti yang dibayar kepada pereka bagi setiap unit yang dikeluarkan ialah...**
- *Choices* Direct material → **Bahan langsung** · Direct labour → **Buruh langsung** · Direct expense → **Belanja langsung** · Factory overhead → **Overhed kilang** · Non-factory expense → **Belanja bukan kilang**
- *Why (EN)* It rises and falls exactly with output and is traceable to each unit, but it is neither material nor labour — that is what a direct expense is. It belongs in prime cost.
- *Why (BM)* **Ia naik dan turun tepat mengikut pengeluaran dan boleh dikesan kepada setiap unit, tetapi ia bukan bahan mahupun buruh — itulah maksud belanja langsung. Ia tergolong dalam kos prima.**
- → 

**`mf-006`** · classify · difficulty 3 · manufacturing

- *Question (EN)* Office stationery is...
- *Question (BM)* **Alat tulis pejabat ialah...**
- *Choices* Direct material → **Bahan langsung** · Direct labour → **Buruh langsung** · Direct expense → **Belanja langsung** · Factory overhead → **Overhed kilang** · Non-factory expense → **Belanja bukan kilang**
- *Why (EN)* Nothing about it touches production. It is an expense of the income statement and never enters the manufacturing account.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada apa padanya yang menyentuh pengeluaran. Ia belanja penyata pendapatan dan tidak pernah masuk ke akaun pengilangan.**
- → 

#### Building the Cost

`l36-cost-of-production` · BM title: `Membina Kos`

> **Worked example (EN)** The manufacturing account is built in a fixed order: work out the materials actually used, add the direct costs to get prime cost, add the factory overheads, adjust for work still unfinished at each end, and you have the cost of production. Only then does the income statement take over.
> **(BM)** Akaun pengilangan dibina mengikut susunan tetap: kira bahan yang benar-benar digunakan, tambah kos langsung untuk mendapat kos prima, tambah overhed kilang, laraskan untuk kerja yang belum siap di kedua-dua hujung, dan anda akan dapat kos pengeluaran. Barulah penyata pendapatan mengambil alih.
- → 

**`mf-007`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · manufacturing, cost-of-production

- *Question (EN)* Opening raw materials RM8,000. Purchases of raw materials RM52,000. Carriage inwards RM2,000. Closing raw materials RM6,000. What is the cost of raw materials consumed?
- *Question (BM)* **Bahan mentah awal RM8,000. Belian bahan mentah RM52,000. Angkutan masuk RM2,000. Bahan mentah akhir RM6,000. Berapakah kos bahan mentah yang digunakan?**
- *Why (EN)* 8,000 + 52,000 + 2,000 − 6,000 = 56,000. Carriage inwards is part of the cost of getting the material in, so it is added to purchases — never treated as an overhead.
- *Why (BM)* **8,000 + 52,000 + 2,000 − 6,000 = 56,000. Angkutan masuk ialah sebahagian daripada kos membawa masuk bahan, jadi ia ditambah kepada belian — bukan dianggap overhed.**
- → 

**`mf-008`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · manufacturing, cost-of-production

- *Question (EN)* Raw materials consumed RM56,000. Direct wages RM34,000. Royalties on units produced RM3,000. What is the prime cost?
- *Question (BM)* **Bahan mentah digunakan RM56,000. Upah langsung RM34,000. Royalti atas unit dikeluarkan RM3,000. Berapakah kos prima?**
- *Why (EN)* 56,000 + 34,000 + 3,000 = 93,000. Prime cost is every DIRECT cost added together — material, labour and direct expense — and nothing else.
- *Why (BM)* **56,000 + 34,000 + 3,000 = 93,000. Kos prima ialah setiap kos LANGSUNG yang dijumlahkan — bahan, buruh dan belanja langsung — dan tiada yang lain.**
- → 

**`mf-009`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · manufacturing

- *Question (EN)* Factory rent RM12,000. Indirect wages RM9,000. Depreciation of factory machinery RM5,000. What are the total factory overheads?
- *Question (BM)* **Sewa kilang RM12,000. Upah tak langsung RM9,000. Susut nilai jentera kilang RM5,000. Berapakah jumlah overhed kilang?**
- *Why (EN)* 12,000 + 9,000 + 5,000 = 26,000. Every one of these keeps the factory running without being traceable to a single unit.
- *Why (BM)* **12,000 + 9,000 + 5,000 = 26,000. Setiap satu daripadanya mengekalkan operasi kilang tanpa boleh dikesan kepada satu unit.**
- → 

**`mf-010`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · manufacturing, cost-of-production

- *Question (EN)* Prime cost RM93,000. Factory overheads RM26,000. Opening work in progress RM4,000. Closing work in progress RM7,000. What is the cost of production?
- *Question (BM)* **Kos prima RM93,000. Overhed kilang RM26,000. Kerja dalam proses awal RM4,000. Kerja dalam proses akhir RM7,000. Berapakah kos pengeluaran?**
- *Why (EN)* 93,000 + 26,000 + 4,000 − 7,000 = 116,000. Opening work in progress is finished this year so it is added; closing work in progress is not finished yet, so it is taken out.
- *Why (BM)* **93,000 + 26,000 + 4,000 − 7,000 = 116,000. Kerja dalam proses awal disiapkan tahun ini jadi ia ditambah; kerja dalam proses akhir belum siap, jadi ia dikeluarkan.**
- → 

**`mf-011`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · manufacturing, cost-of-production

- *Question (EN)* Opening finished goods RM15,000. Cost of production RM116,000. Closing finished goods RM11,000. What is the cost of sales?
- *Question (BM)* **Barang siap awal RM15,000. Kos pengeluaran RM116,000. Barang siap akhir RM11,000. Berapakah kos jualan?**
- *Why (EN)* 15,000 + 116,000 − 11,000 = 120,000. In a manufacturing business the cost of production replaces purchases in the cost of sales working.
- *Why (BM)* **15,000 + 116,000 − 11,000 = 120,000. Dalam perniagaan pengilangan, kos pengeluaran menggantikan belian dalam kerja kira kos jualan.**
- → 

#### Fading the Manufacturing Account

`l37-faded-manufacturing` · BM title: `Melunturkan Akaun Pengilangan`

> **Worked example (EN)** The manufacturing account never changes shape: direct costs give prime cost, overheads are added on top, then the two work-in-progress figures adjust for what was unfinished at each end. Each rung below takes one more line away.
> **(BM)** Akaun pengilangan tidak pernah berubah bentuk: kos langsung memberi kos prima, overhed ditambah di atasnya, kemudian dua angka kerja dalam proses melaraskan apa yang belum siap di kedua-dua hujung. Setiap anak tangga di bawah menanggalkan satu baris lagi.
- → 

**`fd-1201`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · manufacturing, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Raw materials consumed RM56,000. Direct wages RM34,000. Factory overheads RM26,000. Opening work in progress RM4,000. Closing work in progress RM7,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Bahan mentah digunakan RM56,000. Upah langsung RM34,000. Overhed kilang RM26,000. Kerja dalam proses awal RM4,000. Kerja dalam proses akhir RM7,000.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Raw materials consumed → **Bahan mentah digunakan**
  - Add: direct wages → **Tambah: upah langsung**
  - Prime cost → **Kos prima**
  - Add: factory overheads → **Tambah: overhed kilang**
  - Add: opening work in progress → **Tambah: kerja dalam proses awal**
  - Less: closing work in progress → **Tolak: kerja dalam proses akhir**
  - ␣ Cost of production → **Kos pengeluaran**
- *Why (EN)* 90,000 + 26,000 + 4,000 − 7,000 = 113,000.
- *Why (BM)* **90,000 + 26,000 + 4,000 − 7,000 = 113,000.**
- → 

**`fd-1202`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · manufacturing, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Raw materials consumed RM72,000. Direct wages RM41,000. Factory overheads RM30,000. Opening work in progress RM6,000. Closing work in progress RM9,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Bahan mentah digunakan RM72,000. Upah langsung RM41,000. Overhed kilang RM30,000. Kerja dalam proses awal RM6,000. Kerja dalam proses akhir RM9,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Complete the manufacturing account.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Lengkapkan akaun pengilangan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Raw materials consumed → **Bahan mentah digunakan**
  - Add: direct wages → **Tambah: upah langsung**
  - Prime cost → **Kos prima**
  - Add: factory overheads → **Tambah: overhed kilang**
  - ␣ Add: opening work in progress → **Tambah: kerja dalam proses awal**
  - ␣ Less: closing work in progress → **Tolak: kerja dalam proses akhir**
  - ␣ Cost of production → **Kos pengeluaran**
- *Why (EN)* 72,000 + 41,000 = 113,000 prime cost, then + 30,000 + 6,000 − 9,000 = 140,000. Overheads are added AFTER prime cost, never inside it.
- *Why (BM)* **72,000 + 41,000 = 113,000 kos prima, kemudian + 30,000 + 6,000 − 9,000 = 140,000. Overhed ditambah SELEPAS kos prima, bukan di dalamnya.**
- → 

**`fd-1203`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · manufacturing, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* Raw materials consumed RM48,000. Direct wages RM27,000. Factory overheads RM22,000. Opening work in progress RM5,000. Closing work in progress RM3,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Bahan mentah digunakan RM48,000. Upah langsung RM27,000. Overhed kilang RM22,000. Kerja dalam proses awal RM5,000. Kerja dalam proses akhir RM3,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the materials figure is given. Build the whole manufacturing account.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya angka bahan diberi. Bina keseluruhan akaun pengilangan.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Raw materials consumed → **Bahan mentah digunakan**
  - ␣ Add: direct wages → **Tambah: upah langsung**
  - ␣ Prime cost → **Kos prima**
  - ␣ Add: factory overheads → **Tambah: overhed kilang**
  - ␣ Add: opening work in progress → **Tambah: kerja dalam proses awal**
  - ␣ Less: closing work in progress → **Tolak: kerja dalam proses akhir**
  - ␣ Cost of production → **Kos pengeluaran**
- *Why (EN)* 48,000 + 27,000 = 75,000, + 22,000 + 5,000 − 3,000 = 99,000. Here closing work in progress is smaller than opening, so the adjustment adds to the cost overall — the direction follows the figures, not a rule to memorise.
- *Why (BM)* **48,000 + 27,000 = 75,000, + 22,000 + 5,000 − 3,000 = 99,000. Di sini kerja dalam proses akhir lebih kecil daripada yang awal, jadi pelarasan itu menambah kos secara keseluruhan — arahnya mengikut angka, bukan peraturan untuk dihafal.**
- → 

#### Break-Even Point

`l53-break-even` · BM title: `Titik Pulang Modal`

> **Worked example (EN)** Some costs change with how much you make — materials, piece-rate wages — and some do not: rent is the same whether you produce one unit or a thousand. Every unit sold earns its selling price less its VARIABLE cost, and that difference is the contribution. Fixed costs have to be covered before there is any profit at all, so the break-even point is simply fixed costs ÷ contribution per unit: the number of units at which total revenue exactly equals total cost and profit is zero. Below it the business loses money; above it every further unit adds its whole contribution to profit.
> **(BM)** Sesetengah kos berubah mengikut berapa banyak anda keluarkan — bahan, upah ikut unit — dan sesetengahnya tidak: sewa tetap sama sama ada anda mengeluarkan satu unit atau seribu. Setiap unit yang dijual memperoleh harga jualannya tolak kos BERUBAHnya, dan beza itu ialah sumbangan. Kos tetap mesti ditampung sebelum ada sebarang untung, jadi titik pulang modal ialah kos tetap ÷ sumbangan seunit: bilangan unit apabila jumlah hasil sama tepat dengan jumlah kos dan untung ialah sifar. Di bawahnya perniagaan rugi; di atasnya setiap unit tambahan menyumbang keseluruhan sumbangannya kepada untung.
- → 

**`be-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · manufacturing, break-even

- *Question (EN)* What does the break-even point tell a business?
- *Question (BM)* **Apakah yang diberitahu oleh titik pulang modal kepada sesuatu perniagaan?**
- *Choices* The output at which it makes neither profit nor loss → **Pengeluaran apabila ia tidak untung dan tidak rugi** · Its total cost of production → **Jumlah kos pengeluarannya** · Its maximum possible profit → **Untung maksimum yang mungkin**
- *Why (EN)* Total revenue exactly covers total cost. Every unit after that one starts adding profit.
- *Why (BM)* **Jumlah hasil menampung tepat jumlah kos. Setiap unit selepas itu mula menambah untung.**
- → 

**`be-002`** · classify · difficulty 3 · manufacturing, break-even

- *Question (EN)* Which of these is a FIXED cost?
- *Question (BM)* **Antara berikut, yang manakah kos TETAP?**
- *Choices* Factory rent → **Sewa kilang** · Direct materials → **Bahan langsung** · Piece-rate wages → **Upah ikut unit** · Packing per unit → **Pembungkusan seunit**
- *Why (EN)* The rent is the same whether the factory makes one unit or a thousand. The others rise and fall with output.
- *Why (BM)* **Sewa itu sama sama ada kilang mengeluarkan satu unit atau seribu. Yang lain naik turun mengikut pengeluaran.**
- → 

**`be-003`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · manufacturing, break-even

- *Question (EN)* A product sells for RM25 and its variable cost is RM15. What is the contribution per unit?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu produk dijual pada RM25 dan kos berubahnya ialah RM15. Berapakah sumbangan seunit?**
- *Why (EN)* 25 − 15 = 10. That is what each unit contributes towards the fixed costs.
- *Why (BM)* **25 − 15 = 10. Itulah sumbangan setiap unit terhadap kos tetap.**
- → 

**`be-004`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · manufacturing, break-even

- *Question (EN)* Fixed costs are RM40,000 and the contribution is RM10 per unit. What is the break-even point in units?
- *Question (BM)* **Kos tetap ialah RM40,000 dan sumbangan ialah RM10 seunit. Berapakah titik pulang modal dalam unit?**
- *Why (EN)* 40,000 ÷ 10 = 4,000 units.
- *Why (BM)* **40,000 ÷ 10 = 4,000 unit.**
- → 

**`be-005`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · manufacturing, break-even

- *Question (EN)* A product sells for RM30 with a variable cost of RM18. Fixed costs are RM60,000. What is the break-even point in units?
- *Question (BM)* **Sesuatu produk dijual pada RM30 dengan kos berubah RM18. Kos tetap ialah RM60,000. Berapakah titik pulang modal dalam unit?**
- *Why (EN)* Contribution is 30 − 18 = 12, so 60,000 ÷ 12 = 5,000 units. Dividing the fixed costs by the SELLING price instead ignores what each unit costs to make.
- *Why (BM)* **Sumbangan ialah 30 − 18 = 12, jadi 60,000 ÷ 12 = 5,000 unit. Membahagi kos tetap dengan harga JUALAN sebaliknya mengabaikan kos membuat setiap unit.**
- → 

**`be-006`** · classify · difficulty 4 · manufacturing, break-even

- *Question (EN)* Fixed costs rise but the selling price and variable cost stay the same. What happens to the break-even point?
- *Question (BM)* **Kos tetap meningkat tetapi harga jualan dan kos berubah kekal sama. Apakah yang berlaku kepada titik pulang modal?**
- *Choices* It rises → **Ia meningkat** · It falls → **Ia menurun** · It is unchanged → **Ia tidak berubah**
- *Why (EN)* There is more to cover and each unit still contributes the same, so more units are needed.
- *Why (BM)* **Lebih banyak perlu ditampung dan setiap unit masih menyumbang jumlah yang sama, jadi lebih banyak unit diperlukan.**
- → 

**`be-007`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · manufacturing, break-even

- *Question (EN)* Fixed costs are RM40,000 and contribution is RM10 per unit. The business sells 6,500 units. What is the profit?
- *Question (BM)* **Kos tetap ialah RM40,000 dan sumbangan ialah RM10 seunit. Perniagaan menjual 6,500 unit. Berapakah untungnya?**
- *Why (EN)* Break-even is 4,000 units, so 2,500 units beyond it each add their full 10: 2,500 × 10 = 25,000.
- *Why (BM)* **Titik pulang modal ialah 4,000 unit, jadi 2,500 unit selepasnya masing-masing menambah 10 penuh: 2,500 × 10 = 25,000.**
- → 

### Analysing the Statements

`t20-ratio-analysis` · BM title: `Analisis Penyata Kewangan`
- → 

#### Working Out the Ratio

`l38-ratio-compute` · BM title: `Mengira Nisbah`

> **Worked example (EN)** A ratio compares two figures that already exist in the statements. Get the two figures the right way round and the arithmetic is easy: margin is measured against SALES, mark-up against COST, and a liquidity ratio compares what you can turn into cash against what you owe soon.
> **(BM)** Nisbah membandingkan dua angka yang sudah wujud dalam penyata. Dapatkan kedua-dua angka dalam susunan yang betul dan pengiraannya mudah: margin diukur terhadap JUALAN, tokokan terhadap KOS, dan nisbah kecairan membandingkan apa yang boleh ditukar kepada tunai dengan apa yang perlu dibayar tidak lama lagi.
- → 

**`ra-001`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · ratio-analysis, profitability

- *Question (EN)* Sales RM500,000 and gross profit RM100,000. What is the gross profit margin?
- *Question (BM)* **Jualan RM500,000 dan untung kasar RM100,000. Berapakah margin untung kasar?**
- *Why (EN)* 100,000 ÷ 500,000 × 100 = 20%. A MARGIN is always measured against sales.
- *Why (BM)* **100,000 ÷ 500,000 × 100 = 20%. MARGIN sentiasa diukur terhadap jualan.**
- → 

**`ra-002`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · ratio-analysis, profitability

- *Question (EN)* Same business: gross profit RM100,000 and cost of sales RM400,000. What is the mark-up?
- *Question (BM)* **Perniagaan yang sama: untung kasar RM100,000 dan kos jualan RM400,000. Berapakah tokokan?**
- *Why (EN)* 100,000 ÷ 400,000 × 100 = 25%. Same profit, different base: a 20% margin is a 25% mark-up. Dividing by the wrong figure is the most common error in this whole topic.
- *Why (BM)* **100,000 ÷ 400,000 × 100 = 25%. Untung yang sama, asas yang berbeza: margin 20% ialah tokokan 25%. Membahagi dengan angka yang salah ialah kesilapan paling lazim dalam keseluruhan topik ini.**
- → 

**`ra-003`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · ratio-analysis, profitability

- *Question (EN)* Sales RM500,000 and net profit RM60,000. What is the net profit margin?
- *Question (BM)* **Jualan RM500,000 dan untung bersih RM60,000. Berapakah margin untung bersih?**
- *Why (EN)* 60,000 ÷ 500,000 × 100 = 12%. The gap between this and the gross margin is what the expenses cost the business.
- *Why (BM)* **60,000 ÷ 500,000 × 100 = 12%. Jurang antara ini dan margin kasar ialah kos belanja kepada perniagaan.**
- → 

**`ra-004`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · ratio-analysis, liquidity

- *Question (EN)* Current assets RM150,000 and current liabilities RM50,000. What is the current ratio?
- *Question (BM)* **Aset semasa RM150,000 dan liabiliti semasa RM50,000. Berapakah nisbah semasa?**
- *Why (EN)* 150,000 ÷ 50,000 = 3. The business has RM3 of short-term assets for every RM1 it owes soon.
- *Why (BM)* **150,000 ÷ 50,000 = 3. Perniagaan mempunyai RM3 aset jangka pendek bagi setiap RM1 yang perlu dibayar tidak lama lagi.**
- → 

**`ra-005`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · ratio-analysis, liquidity

- *Question (EN)* Current assets RM150,000, of which inventory is RM50,000. Current liabilities RM50,000. What is the acid test ratio?
- *Question (BM)* **Aset semasa RM150,000, termasuk inventori RM50,000. Liabiliti semasa RM50,000. Berapakah nisbah ujian asid?**
- *Why (EN)* (150,000 − 50,000) ÷ 50,000 = 2. Inventory is removed because it still has to be SOLD before it becomes cash — that is the whole point of the acid test.
- *Why (BM)* **(150,000 − 50,000) ÷ 50,000 = 2. Inventori dikeluarkan kerana ia masih perlu DIJUAL sebelum menjadi tunai — itulah tujuan ujian asid.**
- → 

**`ra-006`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · ratio-analysis, profitability

- *Question (EN)* Net profit RM60,000 and capital employed RM400,000. What is the return on capital employed?
- *Question (BM)* **Untung bersih RM60,000 dan modal diguna RM400,000. Berapakah pulangan atas modal diguna?**
- *Why (EN)* 60,000 ÷ 400,000 × 100 = 15%. This is the ratio an owner compares against simply leaving the money in the bank.
- *Why (BM)* **60,000 ÷ 400,000 × 100 = 15%. Inilah nisbah yang dibandingkan oleh pemilik dengan sekadar meninggalkan wangnya di bank.**
- → 

#### Reading the Ratio

`l39-ratio-meaning` · BM title: `Membaca Nisbah`

> **Worked example (EN)** A ratio on its own says nothing. It only means something next to last year's figure, or next to another business. What matters in an exam is being able to say WHICH figure must have moved to produce the change you can see.
> **(BM)** Nisbah dengan sendirinya tidak bermakna apa-apa. Ia hanya bermakna apabila diletakkan di sebelah angka tahun lepas, atau di sebelah perniagaan lain. Apa yang penting dalam peperiksaan ialah keupayaan menyatakan angka MANA yang mesti telah berubah sehingga menghasilkan perubahan yang anda lihat.
- → 

**`ra-007`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · ratio-analysis

- *Question (EN)* Cost of sales RM400,000 and average inventory RM50,000. What is the rate of inventory turnover?
- *Question (BM)* **Kos jualan RM400,000 dan inventori purata RM50,000. Berapakah kadar pusing ganti inventori?**
- *Why (EN)* 400,000 ÷ 50,000 = 8 times. The business sells and replaces its whole inventory eight times a year — a higher figure usually means goods are not sitting on the shelf.
- *Why (BM)* **400,000 ÷ 50,000 = 8 kali. Perniagaan menjual dan menggantikan keseluruhan inventorinya lapan kali setahun — angka yang lebih tinggi biasanya bermakna barang tidak tersadai di rak.**
- → 

**`ra-008`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · ratio-analysis

- *Question (EN)* Trade receivables RM30,000 and credit sales RM365,000. What is the debtors collection period, in days?
- *Question (BM)* **Akaun Belum Terima RM30,000 dan jualan kredit RM365,000. Berapakah tempoh kutipan Akaun Belum Terima, dalam hari?**
- *Why (EN)* 30,000 ÷ 365,000 × 365 = 30 days. Only CREDIT sales are used — a cash sale was never owed in the first place.
- *Why (BM)* **30,000 ÷ 365,000 × 365 = 30 hari. Hanya jualan KREDIT digunakan — jualan tunai tidak pernah terhutang sejak awal lagi.**
- → 

**`ra-009`** · classify · difficulty 4 · ratio-analysis, profitability

- *Question (EN)* A shop's gross profit margin fell from 25% to 18% while its selling prices were unchanged. What must have happened?
- *Question (BM)* **Margin untung kasar sebuah kedai jatuh dari 25% kepada 18% sedangkan harga jualannya tidak berubah. Apakah yang mesti telah berlaku?**
- *Choices* Cost of sales has risen → **Kos jualan telah meningkat** · Operating expenses have risen → **Belanja pengendalian telah meningkat** · Sales have fallen → **Jualan telah menurun**
- *Why (EN)* Gross profit is sales minus cost of sales, so only those two can move it. Operating expenses come further down and change the NET margin, not the gross one. Falling sales alone would not change the percentage.
- *Why (BM)* **Untung kasar ialah jualan tolak kos jualan, jadi hanya kedua-dua itu boleh mengubahnya. Belanja pengendalian datang lebih ke bawah dan mengubah margin BERSIH, bukan yang kasar. Jualan yang menurun sahaja tidak akan mengubah peratusannya.**
- → 

**`ra-010`** · classify · difficulty 3 · ratio-analysis, liquidity

- *Question (EN)* A business has a current ratio of 0.8:1. What does this suggest?
- *Question (BM)* **Sebuah perniagaan mempunyai nisbah semasa 0.8:1. Apakah yang ditunjukkan oleh ini?**
- *Choices* It may not be able to pay its short-term debts → **Ia mungkin tidak mampu membayar hutang jangka pendeknya** · It is holding too much inventory → **Ia menyimpan terlalu banyak inventori**
- *Why (EN)* Below 1:1 the current liabilities are larger than everything the business could turn into cash within the year.
- *Why (BM)* **Di bawah 1:1, liabiliti semasa lebih besar daripada segala yang boleh ditukar oleh perniagaan kepada tunai dalam tahun itu.**
- → 

**`ra-011`** · classify · difficulty 3 · ratio-analysis

- *Question (EN)* A firm's debtors collection period rose from 30 days to 60 days. What does this mean?
- *Question (BM)* **Tempoh kutipan Akaun Belum Terima sebuah firma meningkat dari 30 hari kepada 60 hari. Apakah maksudnya?**
- *Choices* Customers are paying more slowly → **Pelanggan membayar lebih lambat** · Customers are paying faster → **Pelanggan membayar lebih cepat**
- *Why (EN)* A LONGER period means the money sits with the customer for longer — worse for cash flow, even though sales themselves may look healthy.
- *Why (BM)* **Tempoh yang LEBIH PANJANG bermakna wang berada lebih lama dengan pelanggan — lebih buruk untuk aliran tunai, walaupun jualan itu sendiri mungkin kelihatan sihat.**
- → 

#### Fading the Ratio Working

`l40-faded-ratios` · BM title: `Melunturkan Kerja Kira Nisbah`

> **Worked example (EN)** Both margins come out of the same short column: sales, minus cost of sales, gives gross profit and the gross margin; minus expenses gives net profit and the net margin. Every percentage on that column is measured against SALES. Each rung below hands you less of it.
> **(BM)** Kedua-dua margin terhasil daripada lajur pendek yang sama: jualan, tolak kos jualan, memberi untung kasar dan margin kasar; tolak belanja pula memberi untung bersih dan margin bersih. Setiap peratusan dalam lajur itu diukur terhadap JUALAN. Setiap anak tangga di bawah memberi anda kurang daripadanya.
- → 

**`fd-1301`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · ratio-analysis, faded-step, profitability

- *Scenario (EN)* Sales RM500,000. Cost of sales RM400,000. Expenses RM40,000. Give each margin in whole percent.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Jualan RM500,000. Kos jualan RM400,000. Belanja RM40,000. Berikan setiap margin dalam peratus bulat.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Sales → **Jualan**
  - Less: cost of sales → **Tolak: kos jualan**
  - Gross profit → **Untung kasar**
  - Gross profit margin → **Margin untung kasar** · unit: % → **%**
  - Less: expenses → **Tolak: belanja**
  - Net profit → **Untung bersih**
  - ␣ Net profit margin → **Margin untung bersih** · unit: % → **%**
- *Why (EN)* 60,000 ÷ 500,000 × 100 = 12%.
- *Why (BM)* **60,000 ÷ 500,000 × 100 = 12%.**
- → 

**`fd-1302`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · ratio-analysis, faded-step, profitability

- *Scenario (EN)* Sales RM250,000. Cost of sales RM175,000. Expenses RM25,000. Give each margin in whole percent.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Jualan RM250,000. Kos jualan RM175,000. Belanja RM25,000. Berikan setiap margin dalam peratus bulat.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Complete both margins.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Lengkapkan kedua-dua margin.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Sales → **Jualan**
  - Less: cost of sales → **Tolak: kos jualan**
  - Gross profit → **Untung kasar**
  - Gross profit margin → **Margin untung kasar** · unit: % → **%**
  - ␣ Less: expenses → **Tolak: belanja**
  - ␣ Net profit → **Untung bersih**
  - ␣ Net profit margin → **Margin untung bersih** · unit: % → **%**
- *Why (EN)* Gross profit 75,000 is 30% of sales; after 25,000 of expenses the net profit of 50,000 is 20% of sales. Both percentages use SALES as the base, never the profit above them.
- *Why (BM)* **Untung kasar 75,000 ialah 30% daripada jualan; selepas belanja 25,000, untung bersih 50,000 ialah 20% daripada jualan. Kedua-dua peratusan menggunakan JUALAN sebagai asas, bukan untung di atasnya.**
- → 

**`fd-1303`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · ratio-analysis, faded-step, profitability

- *Scenario (EN)* Sales RM800,000. Cost of sales RM600,000. Expenses RM120,000. Give each margin in whole percent.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Jualan RM800,000. Kos jualan RM600,000. Belanja RM120,000. Berikan setiap margin dalam peratus bulat.**
- *Question (EN)* Only sales is given. Work out both margins from the scenario.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya jualan diberi. Kira kedua-dua margin daripada senario.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Sales → **Jualan**
  - ␣ Less: cost of sales → **Tolak: kos jualan**
  - ␣ Gross profit → **Untung kasar**
  - ␣ Gross profit margin → **Margin untung kasar** · unit: % → **%**
  - ␣ Less: expenses → **Tolak: belanja**
  - ␣ Net profit → **Untung bersih**
  - ␣ Net profit margin → **Margin untung bersih** · unit: % → **%**
- *Why (EN)* 800,000 − 600,000 = 200,000, which is 25% of sales; less 120,000 of expenses leaves 80,000, which is 10%. A wide gap between the two margins is a business whose expenses, not whose buying, are the problem.
- *Why (BM)* **800,000 − 600,000 = 200,000, iaitu 25% daripada jualan; tolak belanja 120,000 meninggalkan 80,000, iaitu 10%. Jurang lebar antara kedua-dua margin menunjukkan perniagaan yang masalahnya pada belanja, bukan pada pembelian.**
- → 

### Cash Budgets

`t21-cash-budget` · BM title: `Belanjawan Tunai`
- → 

#### In the Budget or Not?

`l41-cash-budget-basics` · BM title: `Dalam Belanjawan atau Tidak?`

> **Worked example (EN)** A cash budget records MONEY moving, and nothing else. If cash comes in it is a receipt, if cash goes out it is a payment — and if no money changes hands at all, it does not appear, however large the figure is in the income statement.
> **(BM)** Belanjawan tunai merekod WANG yang bergerak, dan tiada yang lain. Jika tunai masuk ia terimaan, jika tunai keluar ia bayaran — dan jika tiada wang bertukar tangan langsung, ia tidak muncul, walau sebesar mana pun angkanya dalam penyata pendapatan.
- → 

**`cb-001`** · classify · difficulty 2 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* Cash sales for the month are...
- *Question (BM)* **Jualan tunai bagi bulan itu ialah...**
- *Choices* Receipt → **Terimaan** · Payment → **Bayaran** · Not in the cash budget → **Tiada dalam belanjawan tunai**
- *Why (EN)* Money comes in during the month it is sold, so it is a receipt of that month.
- *Why (BM)* **Wang masuk dalam bulan ia dijual, jadi ia terimaan bulan itu.**
- → 

**`cb-002`** · classify · difficulty 2 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* A cheque paid to a supplier is...
- *Question (BM)* **Cek yang dibayar kepada pembekal ialah...**
- *Choices* Receipt → **Terimaan** · Payment → **Bayaran** · Not in the cash budget → **Tiada dalam belanjawan tunai**
- *Why (EN)* Money leaves the bank, so it is a payment in the month the cheque is paid.
- *Why (BM)* **Wang keluar dari bank, jadi ia bayaran dalam bulan cek itu dibayar.**
- → 

**`cb-003`** · classify · difficulty 3 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* Depreciation of the delivery van is...
- *Question (BM)* **Susut nilai van penghantaran ialah...**
- *Choices* Receipt → **Terimaan** · Payment → **Bayaran** · Not in the cash budget → **Tiada dalam belanjawan tunai**
- *Why (EN)* Depreciation is an accounting charge, not a payment — no money leaves the business. The van was paid for once, when it was bought.
- *Why (BM)* **Susut nilai ialah caj perakaunan, bukan bayaran — tiada wang keluar dari perniagaan. Van itu dibayar sekali sahaja, iaitu semasa ia dibeli.**
- → 

**`cb-004`** · classify · difficulty 4 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* A bad debt written off is...
- *Question (BM)* **Hutang lapuk yang dihapus kira ialah...**
- *Choices* Receipt → **Terimaan** · Payment → **Bayaran** · Not in the cash budget → **Tiada dalam belanjawan tunai**
- *Why (EN)* Nothing is paid out — what happens is that a receipt you were expecting never arrives. It reduces the receipts you budget for, but it is not itself a payment.
- *Why (BM)* **Tiada apa yang dibayar keluar — apa yang berlaku ialah terimaan yang anda jangkakan tidak pernah sampai. Ia mengurangkan terimaan yang dibelanjawankan, tetapi ia sendiri bukan bayaran.**
- → 

**`cb-005`** · classify · difficulty 3 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* A bank loan received is...
- *Question (BM)* **Pinjaman bank yang diterima ialah...**
- *Choices* Receipt → **Terimaan** · Payment → **Bayaran** · Not in the cash budget → **Tiada dalam belanjawan tunai**
- *Why (EN)* Cash comes in, so it is a receipt — even though it is not income and never appears in the income statement.
- *Why (BM)* **Tunai masuk, jadi ia terimaan — walaupun ia bukan pendapatan dan tidak pernah muncul dalam penyata pendapatan.**
- → 

**`cb-006`** · classify · difficulty 3 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* The owner's cash drawings are...
- *Question (BM)* **Ambilan tunai pemilik ialah...**
- *Choices* Receipt → **Terimaan** · Payment → **Bayaran** · Not in the cash budget → **Tiada dalam belanjawan tunai**
- *Why (EN)* Cash leaves the business, so the budget must show it — again, even though drawings are not an expense.
- *Why (BM)* **Tunai keluar dari perniagaan, jadi belanjawan mesti menunjukkannya — sekali lagi, walaupun ambilan bukan belanja.**
- → 

#### Month by Month

`l42-cash-budget-build` · BM title: `Bulan demi Bulan`

> **Worked example (EN)** The hard part of a cash budget is TIMING, not arithmetic. A credit sale is income in the month of sale but a receipt in the month the customer actually pays. Each month then runs the same three lines: opening balance, plus receipts, less payments — and the closing balance becomes next month's opening balance.
> **(BM)** Bahagian sukar belanjawan tunai ialah MASA, bukan pengiraan. Jualan kredit ialah pendapatan dalam bulan jualan tetapi terimaan dalam bulan pelanggan benar-benar membayar. Setiap bulan kemudiannya berjalan dengan tiga baris yang sama: baki awal, campur terimaan, tolak bayaran — dan baki akhir menjadi baki awal bulan berikutnya.
- → 

**`cb-007`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* Customers pay one month after the sale. Credit sales were January RM20,000, February RM24,000, March RM30,000. How much is received from debtors in March?
- *Question (BM)* **Pelanggan membayar sebulan selepas jualan. Jualan kredit ialah Januari RM20,000, Februari RM24,000, Mac RM30,000. Berapakah yang diterima daripada Akaun Belum Terima pada bulan Mac?**
- *Why (EN)* March's receipts are February's sales: RM24,000. March's own sales of RM30,000 will not be received until April.
- *Why (BM)* **Terimaan Mac ialah jualan Februari: RM24,000. Jualan Mac sendiri sebanyak RM30,000 tidak akan diterima sehingga April.**
- → 

**`cb-008`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* In March, cash sales are RM12,000 and receipts from debtors are RM24,000. What are the total receipts for March?
- *Question (BM)* **Pada bulan Mac, jualan tunai ialah RM12,000 dan terimaan daripada Akaun Belum Terima RM24,000. Berapakah jumlah terimaan bagi bulan Mac?**
- *Why (EN)* 12,000 + 24,000 = 36,000.
- *Why (BM)* **12,000 + 24,000 = 36,000.**
- → 

**`cb-009`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* In March the business pays suppliers RM18,000, wages RM7,000 and rent RM3,000. What are the total payments for March?
- *Question (BM)* **Pada bulan Mac perniagaan membayar pembekal RM18,000, gaji RM7,000 dan sewa RM3,000. Berapakah jumlah bayaran bagi bulan Mac?**
- *Why (EN)* 18,000 + 7,000 + 3,000 = 28,000.
- *Why (BM)* **18,000 + 7,000 + 3,000 = 28,000.**
- → 

**`cb-010`** · numeric · difficulty 3 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* March opens with RM5,000 in the bank. Receipts are RM36,000 and payments RM28,000. What is the closing balance at the end of March?
- *Question (BM)* **Bulan Mac bermula dengan RM5,000 dalam bank. Terimaan RM36,000 dan bayaran RM28,000. Berapakah baki akhir pada penghujung Mac?**
- *Why (EN)* 5,000 + 36,000 − 28,000 = 13,000.
- *Why (BM)* **5,000 + 36,000 − 28,000 = 13,000.**
- → 

**`cb-011`** · numeric · difficulty 4 · cash-budget

- *Question (EN)* April opens with March's closing balance of RM13,000. April receipts are RM30,000 and April payments are RM40,000, which include RM20,000 for new equipment. What is the closing balance at the end of April?
- *Question (BM)* **Bulan April bermula dengan baki akhir Mac sebanyak RM13,000. Terimaan April RM30,000 dan bayaran April RM40,000, termasuk RM20,000 untuk peralatan baharu.  Berapakah baki akhir pada penghujung April?**
- *Why (EN)* 13,000 + 30,000 − 40,000 = 3,000. This is exactly what a cash budget is for: the equipment is affordable, but only just, and one month later it would not have been.
- *Why (BM)* **13,000 + 30,000 − 40,000 = 3,000. Inilah tujuan sebenar belanjawan tunai: peralatan itu mampu dibeli, tetapi nyaris sahaja, dan sebulan lebih lewat ia sudah tidak mampu.**
- → 

#### Fading the Cash Budget

`l43-faded-cash-budget` · BM title: `Melunturkan Belanjawan Tunai`

> **Worked example (EN)** Every month of a cash budget is the same short column: what you started with, everything that came in, everything that went out, and what is left. The figure at the bottom becomes the figure at the top of the next month. Each rung below takes one more line away.
> **(BM)** Setiap bulan dalam belanjawan tunai ialah lajur pendek yang sama: apa yang anda mulakan, segala yang masuk, segala yang keluar, dan apa yang tinggal. Angka di bawah menjadi angka di atas bulan berikutnya. Setiap anak tangga di bawah menanggalkan satu baris lagi.
- → 

**`fd-1401`** · faded_step · difficulty 3 · cash-budget, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* March. Opening cash RM5,000. Cash sales RM12,000. Received from debtors RM24,000. Paid suppliers RM18,000. Wages RM7,000. Rent RM3,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Mac. Tunai awal RM5,000. Jualan tunai RM12,000. Terimaan daripada Akaun Belum Terima RM24,000. Bayaran kepada pembekal RM18,000. Gaji RM7,000. Sewa RM3,000.**
- *Question (EN)* The workings are done. Finish the last line.
- *Question (BM)* **Kerja kira sudah siap. Selesaikan baris terakhir.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Opening cash balance → **Baki tunai awal**
  - Add: cash sales → **Tambah: jualan tunai**
  - Add: receipts from debtors → **Tambah: terimaan daripada Akaun Belum Terima**
  - Less: payments to suppliers → **Tolak: bayaran kepada pembekal**
  - Less: wages → **Tolak: gaji**
  - Less: rent → **Tolak: sewa**
  - ␣ Closing cash balance → **Baki tunai akhir**
- *Why (EN)* 5,000 + 36,000 − 28,000 = 13,000.
- *Why (BM)* **5,000 + 36,000 − 28,000 = 13,000.**
- → 

**`fd-1402`** · faded_step · difficulty 4 · cash-budget, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* April. Opening cash RM8,000. Cash sales RM15,000. Received from debtors RM20,000. Paid suppliers RM22,000. Wages RM9,000. Rent RM4,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **April. Tunai awal RM8,000. Jualan tunai RM15,000. Terimaan daripada Akaun Belum Terima RM20,000. Bayaran kepada pembekal RM22,000. Gaji RM9,000. Sewa RM4,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Three lines are missing. Complete the month.
- *Question (BM)* **Tiga baris hilang. Lengkapkan bulan itu.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Opening cash balance → **Baki tunai awal**
  - Add: cash sales → **Tambah: jualan tunai**
  - Add: receipts from debtors → **Tambah: terimaan daripada Akaun Belum Terima**
  - Less: payments to suppliers → **Tolak: bayaran kepada pembekal**
  - ␣ Less: wages → **Tolak: gaji**
  - ␣ Less: rent → **Tolak: sewa**
  - ␣ Closing cash balance → **Baki tunai akhir**
- *Why (EN)* 8,000 + 35,000 − 35,000 = 8,000. Receipts and payments cancelled out exactly, so the month ends where it began — a flat month is not the same as a month with nothing happening in it.
- *Why (BM)* **8,000 + 35,000 − 35,000 = 8,000. Terimaan dan bayaran saling membatalkan dengan tepat, jadi bulan itu berakhir di tempat ia bermula — bulan yang rata bukanlah sama dengan bulan yang tiada apa-apa berlaku.**
- → 

**`fd-1403`** · faded_step · difficulty 5 · cash-budget, faded-step

- *Scenario (EN)* May. Opening cash RM3,000. Cash sales RM18,000. Received from debtors RM26,000. Paid suppliers RM25,000. Wages RM11,000. Rent RM5,000.
- *Scenario (BM)* **Mei. Tunai awal RM3,000. Jualan tunai RM18,000. Terimaan daripada Akaun Belum Terima RM26,000. Bayaran kepada pembekal RM25,000. Gaji RM11,000. Sewa RM5,000.**
- *Question (EN)* Only the opening balance is given. Build the whole month.
- *Question (BM)* **Hanya baki awal diberi. Bina keseluruhan bulan itu.**
- *Worked steps* (␣ marks a step the learner must supply)
  - Opening cash balance → **Baki tunai awal**
  - ␣ Add: cash sales → **Tambah: jualan tunai**
  - ␣ Add: receipts from debtors → **Tambah: terimaan daripada Akaun Belum Terima**
  - ␣ Less: payments to suppliers → **Tolak: bayaran kepada pembekal**
  - ␣ Less: wages → **Tolak: gaji**
  - ␣ Less: rent → **Tolak: sewa**
  - ␣ Closing cash balance → **Baki tunai akhir**
- *Why (EN)* 3,000 + 44,000 − 41,000 = 6,000. The business is solvent all month, but only because the debtors paid: without that RM26,000 it would have run out of cash.
- *Why (BM)* **3,000 + 44,000 − 41,000 = 6,000. Perniagaan mampu bertahan sepanjang bulan, tetapi hanya kerana pelanggan membayar: tanpa RM26,000 itu ia akan kehabisan tunai.**
- →
